# Changelog

All notable changes to Steward are documented here.

The format follows [Keep a Changelog 1.1](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/), and the project uses [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/). Commits follow [Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/), so entries can be drafted from the commit history.

## [Unreleased]

### Added

- **Plant proposals in parts** ([docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#proposals-in-parts), [docs/MCP.md](docs/MCP.md#plant-registry)). An AI assistant that reads a manual with hundreds of data points sends `propose_plant_changes` in parts of up to 48 kB (`more: true`, then `proposalId`; at most 25 parts and 1 MB together): each part is a document of the import format, checked when it comes, and Steward merges the parts by key into one proposal – the same entry in several parts is merged, interfaces, groups and data points by their ids, and a value given differently in two parts refuses that part. The import's preview and the rules of a proposal (only additions and changes, no credentials) run on the combined document with the last part; only then do reviewers see it and are told. An unfinished proposal is its owner's, can be withdrawn and expires 24 hours after its last part; every part is audited (`plant.proposal_part_received`).
- **Suppliers** (_Catalogue → Suppliers_, [docs/SUPPLIERS.md](docs/SUPPLIERS.md), [ADR 0029](docs/adr/0029-iso-27001-support.md)): suppliers with contacts, contracts (notice-by date, renewal, SLA, contractual RTO/RPO), certificates and the data processing agreement, with reminders to their owners 90 and 30 days before a certificate runs out or a notice-by date (`supplier.certificate_expiring`, `supplier.notice_due`) and a daily summary section; applications, systems, ticket references, page watches, cost plan lines (with the contract they pay for) and duties link them through a supplier picker, keeping their vendor text as a fallback, and the example process _Supplier review_ makes the yearly review a duty. Read through `GET /api/v1/suppliers`, the MCP tools `list_suppliers` and `get_supplier` and the dataset `suppliers.expiring` – contacts without e-mail or phone; writes only in the app. Migrations `suppliers-001-suppliers`, `suppliers-002-links`. **Check before updating:** the update turns every distinct free-text vendor into a supplier _to confirm_ and links it – someone with the designer or administrator role works through _Suppliers → To confirm_ once (confirm, merge, or mark _not a supplier_ such as `On-premises`); until then they are left out of reminders and summaries.
- **Sources of data points** ([docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#sources-of-data-points)). A data point can name the document and the place that describe it – `source: { document, page, section }` in the import format, its export and JSON Schema, the data point's form and page, a quiet line in the profile's data point list, and the MCP tools `get_device_profile` and `get_data_point`. A proposal's review lists the sources of the data points it adds or changes by document, names the record that keeps the manual when one is linked to the profile, and otherwise offers _Attach the manual_ on the profile's Documents panel. No migration: data points are stored with their profile.

### Changed

- **Decision record 0029 accepted: ISO/IEC 27001 support** ([ADR 0029](docs/adr/0029-iso-27001-support.md#decided-november-2026), [docs/ROADMAP.md](docs/ROADMAP.md#next)): suppliers as records instead of free-text vendor fields, a statement of applicability with a responsibility matrix per service, one register of corrective actions with an effectiveness check, and an ISMS summary section for scheduled reports – planned for 0.17.0, with the open questions decided as recommended (_Decided (November 2026)_). Not built yet.

## [0.16.0] - 2026-11-10

Steward 0.16.0 adds lightweight cost planning per service: plan lines and cost entries per fiscal year (one start month for the instance), set against the cloud and platform costs Steward already reads – planned, incurred, expected and the difference. Administrators define scheduled exports of readable files to S3 – cost plans, the plant registry, the catalogue, monitors, the audit log – each pinned to the target they confirmed, and choose what a backup holds. Devices and data collections move between assets as reviewed change sets with Revert, and the monitor States tab explains itself, shows settling and sets what a contradiction makes the monitor. A review of architecture, security, performance, UI and wording brought fixes throughout: exports written in pieces, credentials redacted, cost plans computed with a fixed number of queries, British spelling and the German glossary terms. The Industrial Edge examples read app versions from the right endpoint. Migrations are additive (`costs-002-service-plans`, `ops-015-export-jobs`, `ops-016-export-target`).

### Added

- **Cost planning per service and fiscal year** (Costs module, [docs/COSTS.md](docs/COSTS.md#planning-per-service), [ADR 0026](docs/adr/0026-service-cost-planning.md)). One fiscal year start for the instance (_Costs → Settings_, default January; _FY 2026/27_ when it starts later in the year; changing it asks first and says how many cost entries would fall into another fiscal year than their plan line – they keep the link, count as unplanned in their year and stay editable). _Catalogue → the service → Cost plan_ (linked to and from the service's costs read): plan lines (licences, cloud, service and support, hardware, other; yearly spread evenly, monthly or once; for an application or system of the service, a platform, or a vendor or contract; an optional renewal date) and cost entries with a reference (invoice or order number; amounts in an ISO 4217 currency, today counted in the instance's time zone) – with a plan line or **unplanned**. The year view shows per line and currency planned, planned to date, incurred to date, expected for the year and the difference, a month chart with its table, renewals in the next 90 days and the unplanned entries; a cloud line for an application or platform takes the costs Steward already reads for it, the rest shows as _cloud and platform (read)_ – only for readers who see every application of the service. _Copy to the next fiscal year_, CSV export, a team rollup on the team's costs page (the team rollup, the dataset and the scheduled export cover the same services: not archived, with plan lines or entries in the year; a year view takes the same few database statements however many lines and entries it has, and work over many services pauses between them), `GET /api/v1/services/{serviceId}/cost-plan?fiscalYear=` (one name, `fiscalYear`, for the fiscal year in every query, body and answer; not paged – at most 200 lines and 2,000 entries per service and year), the dataset _Cost plans_ (`costs.plans`) and the MCP tool `get_service_cost_plan`. Changes are audited (`cost.plan_*`, `cost.entry_*`), without a step-up. No person-hours, approvals, invoice processing, chargeback or currency conversion. Migration `costs-002-service-plans`; the demo starts its fiscal year in October and plans two services.
- **Scheduled exports to S3** ([docs/EXPORTS.md](docs/EXPORTS.md), [ADR 0027](docs/adr/0027-backup-contents-and-exports.md)): under _Settings → Exports_ administrators define exports of readable files for sharing and archiving outside Steward – not for a restore. Parts from a registry: cost plans and entries of a fiscal year (CSV), the plant registry in its import format (YAML or JSON), services, applications and systems (CSV), monitors (YAML), the audit log of the last days (CSV or JSON Lines); a part of a switched-off module is skipped and named. Each run writes `<prefix><name>/<UTC time>/` to the backup's bucket or another S3 platform, with `manifest.json` (SHA-256 of every file); _Run now_, a run history with sizes and errors, the newest runs kept (only files the export wrote are removed; each file is recorded before it is uploaded, so what a failed or interrupted run leaves is removed later, and a platform that still holds such files cannot be deleted). Readable by default; optionally encrypted with the backup key (`steward backup decrypt` reads such files, and uploaded audit archives). Runs as the system and never writes a secret – credentials typed into a monitor (sensitive request headers, user information and keys in URLs) are written as `<redacted>` and listed in the manifest; defining, changing and removing an export asks for a step-up (new control point `export.settings`). Each export is pinned to the S3 target an administrator confirmed (platform, address, region, connection, access key id, folder): when whoever manages the platform changes it, runs fail before writing anything (`EXPORT_TARGET_CHANGED`) until an administrator confirms the target again (_Confirm target_, step-up). Audited as `export.*`; a failed run notifies the administrators (new event `export.failed`); a scheduled time missed while Steward was not running gets one catch-up run after the start (`export.missed`). New tables `export_jobs` and `export_runs` (migrations `ops-015-export-jobs`, `ops-016-export-target`).
- **What a backup holds** ([docs/BACKUPS.md](docs/BACKUPS.md#contents)): _Settings → Backups → Contents_ shows every part with its size and what a restore lacks without it. Run artifacts, archived reports and evidence packs can be left out (all included by default; they are evidence, so the page warns and asks for a confirmation that names what a restore lacks); the database, `vault.json`, `audit-anchor.json`, the audit archives and the manifest never – the API refuses them (`BACKUP_PART_REQUIRED`). A backup that leaves parts out names them in `manifest.json` (`excluded`), in its header (`npm run backup -- info`), in its steps and in the restore test (step _contents_).
- **Plant registry: move devices and data collections between assets, move several assets at once, duplicate a device** ([docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#moving-devices-and-data-collections)). _Move to another asset_ (in the installation's own word for assets) in a device's menu, for the devices selected on a site's or an asset's _Devices_ tab, and on a data collection's page: a dialog with a searchable target asset (its site first, other sites on request), the preview of what moves along and field by field, and _Move_ or _Add to draft_. A device keeps its id under the new asset (`<asset>/<device>`) or gets a new one where the target has it (a free one is suggested). Its data paths follow when they name only moving devices – a data path that would be cut in two is refused –, the data collections of those paths stay on their asset and lose the link by default (their items are mapped to that asset's model) or move along; checks, maintenance window targets, history, old ids, the Insights Hub state, links and open drafts follow through the rename registry. To another site only where the person manages both: the device leaves its VLAN and so its zones. A data collection moves on its own with its items, sinks, mappings and kept files. Each move is a change set (operation and source `move`) with review where the site needs it, history, **Revert** (moves back), a fingerprint (`PLANT_MOVE_CHANGED`), a step-up beyond 25 entries – counting what moves along – and for every move to another site, and the audit action `plant.moved`; `POST /api/plant/move[/preview]`. _Move to another place_ for the assets selected in the asset list moves them to one place of their site as a bulk change. _Duplicate_ copies a device to its asset or another without its host name, address, MAC address, serial number, port and outlet, checks and monitoring (`POST /api/plant/assets/:assetId/devices/:deviceId/duplicate`). No migration.

### Changed

- **Decision records 0022–0024 accepted** ([ADR 0022](docs/adr/0022-privileged-access-checkout.md#decided-november-2026), [0023](docs/adr/0023-edge-runner.md#decided-november-2026), [0024](docs/adr/0024-signed-releases.md#decided-november-2026)): the open questions of the PAM checkout, the edge runner and signed releases are decided, each with the recommended answer (_Decided (November 2026)_ in each record); none of them is built yet.
- **A monitor that settles says so** ([docs/MONITORS.md](docs/MONITORS.md#settle-time)). While a state's settle time runs, the monitor page showed a green _Up_ next to red cards; now the status is followed by _settling until 14:00 – 1 check not as expected yet_, the deviating cards are amber with _Settling – not counted yet_, and the checks table, the check's panel, the monitor list and the status wall mark them _settling_. The status itself is computed as before.
- **_On contradiction_ in the States tab** ([docs/MONITORS.md](docs/MONITORS.md#the-editors-states-tab)): each state sets what a contradiction makes the monitor – _Down_ (default) or _Degraded_ – in the editor, not only in YAML (`contradiction:`); the legend names the other way, a check that is not _Critical_.
- **One wording across the English and German texts** ([docs/I18N.md](docs/I18N.md#english-style)). English is British throughout (colour, catalogue, licence, organisation, recognised – the _Appearance_ and _About_ pages and the colour-scheme toggle were American), labels say _ID_, _user name_, _Last sign-in_ and _on-premises_, the vendor is spelled _Checkmk_ (also in check messages, platform presets and examples), and records are _documents_ and _document types_ wherever the UI still said _record_ (plant registry entries are _entries_). Buttons that open a dialog lose their "…" (_Import package_, _Generate PDF report_, _Owning teams for the whole service_), progress texts have one form (_Loading…_; German _Wird geladen …_). German follows its glossary: _Ausführung_ for a run (no more _Lauf_ next to _Ausführung_), _Datenquelle_ for a dataset (not _Datensatz_, which reads as one row), _Service_ for catalogue services, _Audit-Log_, _Zugangsdaten_, _Dokumentart_, _OK_ for an up check in the States tab and _Kennung_ for the ids of plant entries; the glossary names _Testlauf_, _Debug-Lauf_ and _Kennung_. Example names with _CheckMk_ or _optimization_ change with them. Texts only – keys, stored values and API fields are unchanged; the demo platform keeps its name _CheckMk (demo)_.
- **The States tab says what each cell does** ([docs/MONITORS.md](docs/MONITORS.md#the-editors-states-tab)). A cell's choices are grouped – _Any_, **Selects this state** (_Up · selects_ …), **Expected in this state** (_Up · expected_, _Down · expected_, _Off · expected_, formerly plain _Up_, _Down_, _Off_) and **Precondition** (_Required_) – and a legend under the table explains the groups, that _Off_ is offered only in sub-monitor columns, and that the _Shows_ column decides whether a state shows grey Off – open for a new monitor, folded under _How to read the table_ once it is saved. The stored values are unchanged.

### Fixed

- **Orange status texts are readable in the light scheme.** About 30 texts – failing component checks, refused AI calls, KPI and plant notes, failing inputs – used Mantine's raw orange (3.6:1 on white, below WCAG AA); they now use the contrast-safe text colour, as badges already did.
- **Buttons in dialogs keep their label.** In a narrow dialog a long description next to a button squeezed it – the dashboard templates showed _U_ instead of _Use_. A button in a row now never shrinks below its label; the text next to it wraps.
- **A CSV table input without a header row is read by the declared columns** ([docs/PROCESSES.md](docs/PROCESSES.md#inputs)). A pasted line such as `new.operator@example.com,New,Operator` was taken as the header, so the run read no rows and _Insights Hub – Onboard users with group bundles_ asked "Onboard 0 user(s)?". With declared `columns`, a first line that names none of them is now data, read by position (extra fields ignored, missing ones empty); a header in another case takes the declared spelling. A required table input with no rows is refused when the run starts (`INPUT_TABLE_EMPTY`), the run dialog shows the rows it read under the field, and the onboarding example checks _At least one user_ before the approval.
- **Duplicating a monitor with states no longer fails when it includes a sub-monitor you may not see** ([docs/MONITORS.md](docs/MONITORS.md#permissions-and-audit)). The copy left the hidden sub-monitor's check out but kept its cells in the states table, so saving the copy was refused; the cells are now left out too, a state whose condition named only that sub-monitor is dropped (counted in the message after copying), and a monitor that follows one you may not see is copied without its states.
- **A changed settle time applies to a running settle** ([docs/MONITORS.md](docs/MONITORS.md#settle-time)). The end of a monitor state's settle time was fixed at the state change, so shortening or lengthening `settleSeconds` (or the checks it is derived from) only applied to the next state change; while it runs, its end now follows the current definition – the state change plus today's settle time – and the recomputed end is stored for restarts. A settle that has already ended stays ended: a longer settle time does not reopen it, so a confirmed contradiction does not turn back into _Up_.
- **The Industrial Edge examples use the right endpoints and device ids** ([examples/README.md](examples/README.md#verify-against-your-environment)). _Industrial Edge – App version compliance_ called `GET /portal/api/v1/devices/{id}/apps` per device with the device's `id`, which IEM devices do not carry, so a real IEM answered `HTTP 404 … /devices//apps`. It now reads the installations of all devices in one call from `GET /portal/api/v1/devices/installed-apps` (`versionNumber` per device and app), groups them by device, lists devices without any as _unknown_ and logs a warning when the answer reports more entries than it contains. All Industrial Edge examples read a device's id and name from `deviceId` and `deviceName` (falling back to `id` and `name`), so the stale-device cleanup deletes the right device; the simulated IEM answers with these fields and the `installed-apps` list.
- **A run artifact whose file is missing answers 410 instead of an error** ([docs/BACKUPS.md](docs/BACKUPS.md#contents)). After restoring a backup without run artifacts (or with a file removed from the data folder), `GET /api/artifacts/:id` failed with 500; it now answers `410 ARTIFACT_FILE_GONE` like a report file that is gone, and the run page shows _The file is not available_ instead of a broken image.

## [0.15.0] - 2026-11-10

Steward 0.15.0 consolidates 0.13 and 0.14. The plant change-set history takes a third of the space on SQLite and half on PostgreSQL – larger values are stored compressed, import baselines as the revision an import left – and its retention is a setting under Settings → Data & retention. The OT network diagram no longer redraws its parts right after drawing them, and the UI tests are sturdier on a loaded machine. People can choose events to receive in their own chat with the Steward Teams bot instead of by e-mail (preview, off by default), and a first-week guide walks a new team through setting Steward up.

### Added

- **Your first week with Steward** ([docs/FIRST-WEEK.md](docs/FIRST-WEEK.md)): a day-by-day plan for a team that just installed Steward – sign-in, a second administrator, the vault and backups; a first platform with an identity and its spec, a process from the examples and a dry run; a monitor with states for one data path, notifications and quiet hours; services, applications, owners and maintenance windows; one site in the plant registry with profiles, KPIs and the review of change sets – and what to leave for later. Linked from the README, Getting started, the docs index and the in-app getting-started checklist.
- **Teams (personal): your messages in your chat with the Teams bot** (preview, [docs/NOTIFICATIONS.md](docs/NOTIFICATIONS.md#teams-personal), [docs/TEAMS-APPROVALS.md](docs/TEAMS-APPROVALS.md#personal-notifications-in-the-chat-preview), [ADR 0025](docs/adr/0025-personal-teams-notifications.md)). With the Teams bot on, _Profile → Teams (personal)_ lets each person choose messages – those the instance sends by e-mail, e.g. _Run failed_ or _Credential due_ – that come to their own chat with the Steward app **instead of by e-mail**: the same text as the e-mail, links only, with their quiet hours and digests. Off by default for everybody. The bot records the chat when the person adds the app or first writes to it, uses it only while the account is active, linked through the bot's Entra sign-in to the same object id and in an allowed tenant, and stops using it when the app is removed, the account is deactivated or Teams answers 403/404. Throttling (429, 502–504) is retried three times with backoff, then the message comes by e-mail once. Audited (`notification.personal_channel_changed`, `teams.conversation_added`/`_removed` with the reason); the data export lists the choice, pseudonymisation deletes it. The card is hidden while the bot is off. API `GET`/`PUT /api/me/teams-personal`; migration `notifications-004-teams-personal` adds the table `notification_personal_channels` and the column `teams_conversations.aad_object_id`.

### Changed

- **Plant change-set history takes a third of the space on SQLite, half on PostgreSQL** ([ADR 0020](docs/adr/0020-plant-change-sets.md#phase-2-storage-and-retention-0150), [docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#history-retention)). History values of 96 characters or more are stored compressed (raw DEFLATE with a preset dictionary, as text on SQLite and PostgreSQL) when that is shorter; the import's baselines are kept per entry and import as the revision the import left (table `plant_entry_baselines`, migration `plant-030-entry-baselines`; 0.13's `plant_import_baselines` is moved into it), an equal one is not written again and the last 3 per entry are kept. On a plant of 5,000 assets, 10,000 devices and 10,000 data points the full import's history and baselines take 12.7 instead of 38.2 MB on SQLite (the file 26.1 instead of 51.6 MB) and 15.4 instead of 29.1 MB on PostgreSQL 16; a re-import of 5 % writes 790 baselines instead of rewriting 25,314. History, Revert, the merge, the change set's page and the audit entry's `historySha256` read the same; the daily job compacts rows of 0.13, a bounded number per run.
- **The retention of the plant history is a setting** (_Settings → Data & retention → Plant registry history_, administrators, audited as `plant.history_retention_changed`): the history of removed entries (default 3 years, 30–3,650 days), closed change sets (default 90 days, 7–3,650) and the import's baselines per entry (default 3, 1–10), with what history, baselines and change sets take in the database now (`GET|PUT /api/settings/plant/history`).

### Fixed

- **The plant OT network diagram no longer replaces its parts right after drawing them** – it was drawn before its frame had a size and again after – and a refresh that changes only live statuses (every minute while a linked check reports) no longer lays the picture out and fits it again under the pointer.
- **The History tab and Revert list the rows of one change set in a fixed order** (kind, scope and entry id after time and revision), the same on PostgreSQL after a row was rewritten.
- **Tests on a loaded machine**: isolated servers of the UI tests get 240 s to start with the demo (a hook that timed out stopped the server under its own setup, reported as `fetch failed` or 502), the test servers allow 6,000 requests per user and minute (production stays at 600), and the dashboards test waits for the demo's feeder runs instead of a fixed time (and stretches the expression time limit, which the CheckMk trend summary overran under load).

## [0.14.0] - 2026-11-10

Steward 0.14.0 brings the owner's priorities for 0.14. Platform identities can be marked privileged: every use asks for a step-up, and a second person can be required to approve a run before it starts. Confluent Cloud becomes a full platform – clusters, Schema Registry, service accounts, API key metadata and managed connectors, with a monitor check, access checks and costs. The plant registry matches CSAF 2.0 security advisories of device vendors to assets, shows what a planned maintenance window affects – data paths, collections, KPIs, dashboards, monitors and services – and adds planned downtime for those monitors in one step, lets documents be confirmed as still current with gentle review hints and a list of documentation gaps per site, and maps data collections to Insights Hub assets, aspects and variables, checked against the tenant with existing monitors found first. Decision records for a PAM checkout, an edge runner and signed releases are proposed. See _Check before updating_ under Changed.

### Added

- **Confluent Cloud as a platform** ([#123](https://github.com/vxrz/Steward/issues/123), [docs/CONFLUENT.md](docs/CONFLUENT.md)). The platform kind _Confluent Cloud_ (preset _Confluent Cloud API_, formerly _Confluent Cloud API (access reviews)_) now reads the organisation through the Cloud API – with `GET` requests only; processes may still manage it, mark the identity read-only to prevent that – every hour and on _Read now_ – environments, Kafka clusters (type, cloud, region, availability, status), Schema Registry clusters, service accounts, the metadata of API keys (owner, resource, created, expiry – never a secret; Confluent's list returns none) and the fully managed connectors with their tasks and the trimmed error of a failed one (no configuration kept) – and, with the new variable `schemaRegistryUrl` and a key of that registry (`registryKey`, `registrySecret`), its subjects with versions and compatibility. Each part is read on its own: a part the key may not read (API keys need OrganizationAdmin) shows its error and the others stand. The platform page opens on **Clusters**, with the tabs **Schemas**, **Connectors** and **API keys**; a cluster links to the Kafka platform whose `confluentClusterId` names it, a connector's topics to that platform's Kafka topic components, which list the managed connectors like Kafka Connect's. API keys show hints (expired, expires within 30 days, older than a year) and can be taken into the **credentials register** as reviewed drafts (metadata only). New monitor check type **`confluent`**: the clusters are provisioned, or the managed connectors and their tasks run (a failed task alarms after `failedMinutes`), with the platform-kind guard. The Costs API lines of clusters no Kafka platform names stay on the Confluent platform as before. Answers of the kind lose any `secret` field, and connectors' configurations and status their secret-looking values. Lists follow `metadata.next` on the API's own origin, `429` is retried with `Retry-After`; the Schema Registry endpoint goes through the outbound policy like any other host. The demo's _Confluent Cloud (demo)_ has three environments, two clusters, four service accounts, five keys and three connectors (one task failed). `GET /api/platforms/:id/confluent`, `POST /api/platforms/:id/confluent/read`; job `confluent`. Migration `confluent-001-readouts` (additive: a new table).
- **Security advisories for OT devices** (plant registry, preview, [docs/ADVISORIES.md](docs/ADVISORIES.md)). _Plant → Advisories_ reads **CSAF 2.0** documents of device vendors and CISA – from **sources** an administrator adds (a provider's `provider-metadata.json` with its ROLIE feeds or directory, an aggregator's `aggregator.json`, or one ROLIE feed; **none by default**; adding, changing or removing one asks for a step-up – new control point **`plant.advisorySources`**, on by default), read on a schedule through the **outbound policy** with every redirect hop checked and kept only when the published SHA-512 or SHA-256 matches (OpenPGP signatures are linked, not checked yet), or **uploaded** as files for plants without internet access (with an optional SHA-256 to check). Documents are checked against the needed part of the CSAF JSON schema with fixed limits (4 MB, 5,000 products, product tree depth 30 …); TLP:AMBER and RED are not kept. **Matching** compares each asset's manufacturer, model and firmware – its own and its device profile's, with the assignment's controller firmware – with the product tree: only an exact or normalised vendor (legal forms left out) and model (letters and digits only, model numbers with wildcards) with the firmware in the affected range (vers, vers-like constraints such as `<V3.0.29`, and the free text advisories use) counts, and every match says why (_matched on vendor Example Controls, model C80, firmware 3.2.0 in vers:generic/>=3.0.0|<3.4.0 via device profile …_); similar vendors or models, unknown firmware, ranges Steward does not read and products under investigation are **possible matches**, listed apart and never counted until someone confirms them. People **confirm or dismiss** a match per asset (with a reason), record the **remediation** per affected asset – open, planned (a maintenance window or change set), mitigated, accepted risk (with reason and end; counted as open again after it), not affected – with the fixed firmware the advisory names suggested, and mark an advisory **not applicable** per site; all audited. The list shows severity and CVSS, affected assets, sites, zones with their SL-T, dates and the status for the reader's sites, filtered (site, severity, status, vendor, text; by default only what affects the reader's sites), sorted and paged by the server so thousands of advisories stay quick; the advisory page groups the affected assets by site and zone; the asset page has a section _Advisories_; the start page a tile _Advisories affecting your sites_. New high and critical matches notify the site's managers (event **`plant.advisory_matched`**, quiet hours and digests apply). Matches follow plant changes (a firmware update past the fix ends the match), rematched in bounded batches that the job resumes, so a large feed never blocks a request or the job. Read in `/api/v1` (`/plant/advisories`, `/plant/advisories/{advisoryId}`, `/plant/assets/{assetId}/advisories`; OpenAPI regenerated) and through the MCP tools `list_plant_advisories` and `get_plant_advisory`, with the sites the reader may see. The demo adds a simulated provider with three invented advisories for its invented devices (`examples/plant/advisories/`, also an upload example). Migration `plant-020-advisories` (additive: five new tables).
- **Impact before maintenance** ([docs/APPLICATIONS.md](docs/APPLICATIONS.md#impact-before-maintenance)). A maintenance window shows what it affects – in the form before saving and on its page: the devices behind a gateway or switch, data paths and the paths that continue from them, data collections, the KPI jobs and coverage that read their data points, datasets, dashboards, the monitors with what they do meanwhile (_Goes Off_ for a monitor with an Off state, _Goes down_, _Stays quiet_), the applications and services the data passes and the owners and contacts to tell – grouped, counted and linked, naming only what the planner may see (the rest is counted) and bounded in depth and size. One click plans **planned downtime for the affected monitors** (or lists them in the window), and **tells the affected owners** with the window's announcement, once per version. API: `GET /api/maintenance-windows/impact?targets=…`, `GET …/:id/impact`, `POST …/:id/impact/downtime`, `POST …/:id/impact/notify`.
- **Plant targets on maintenance windows** ([docs/APPLICATIONS.md](docs/APPLICATIONS.md#plant-targets)): a window can list a site, an asset, a device (`<asset>/<device>`) or a data collection (its collector) of the plant registry; who manages the site plans it, renames follow, and site and asset pages show and plan their maintenance. Additive: `MAINTENANCE_TARGET_KINDS` and the `kind` enum of `/api/v1/maintenance-windows` targets gain `site`, `asset`, `device` and `collection`.
- **Documentation still current** (plant registry, [docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#documentation-still-current)). _Still current_ on each document of a Documents panel, and _All still current_ for an asset's documentation as a whole, records who confirmed it and when (_Last confirmed by … on …_, audited; a light action, not a change set). An optional **review interval** per site (its settings) and per document type (_Settings → Modules → Plant registry_) shows a gentle hint when a look is due – never red, never a notification. A site's new **Documentation** tab lists the gaps – assets without a profile, devices without a VLAN, data paths and collections without a sink, collections without items, KPIs a machine could have but nobody collects – and the confirmations due, each linked to where to fix it. Read in `/api/v1`: `GET /api/v1/plant/assets/{assetId}/documentation`, `docReviewMonths` on sites. Migration `plant-016-doc-confirmations`.
- **Insights Hub as a mapped sink** (plant registry, [docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#insights-hub-mapping), [docs/MONITORS.md](docs/MONITORS.md#from-an-insights-hub-mapping)). A sink whose target is an Insights Hub platform maps each item of its collection to an asset (by its Insights Hub id, the name kept for display), an aspect and a variable – freely per item, with a default asset and aspect on the sink and the placeholder `{asset.ref:<system>}` for the asset's external reference; tools: one asset and aspect for many items with the same variable names, CSV (`item,asset_id,asset_name,aspect,variable`, merge or replace, and download), per item. **Check mapping** reads the tenant – never writes, with the platform's default identity, at most 50 assets per run, aspects paged, each asset and aspect type once – and reports per item OK, unit or type differs (hints), asset, aspect or variable missing, not checked or failed (clear messages for 401, 403 and 429); the asset hierarchy is not compared with the plant's. The result is stored and shown on the sink and the data point's page. **Monitors first**: per asset and aspect the sink shows the Insights Hub freshness checks that already watch a variable, and only for the rest offers **Create monitor** – a **disabled** draft with a freshness check per asset and aspect, the allowance from the observed delay of the asset's other checks, and the states of the monitor the asset links to (`states.from`); the links of items to monitor checks are stored, so the monitor's page says what it covers. Coverage (mapped, verified, watched, gaps) per sink and on a new site tab **Insights Hub**; the KPI coverage marks _Collected – ready_ cells verified in Insights Hub (`ihVerified`, `potential.ihVerified`, additive). Rights: the collection's (manage the site to change it); checking also needs the use right on the platform and its default identity, creating a monitor the right to create monitors. Audit `plant.ih_mapping_checked`, `plant.ih_monitor_created`, mapping changes as `plant.collection_updated` with `insightsHub`. File format (`insights_hub`, `asset_id`, `asset_name`, `aspect`; JSON Schema regenerated), `GET /api/v1/plant/assets/{assetId}/collections/{collectionId}/sinks/{sinkId}/insights-hub` (OpenAPI regenerated) and the MCP tool `get_insights_hub_mapping` additive. The Insights Hub mock serves single assets, their aspects with variables and aspect types, with simulated 401/403/429. Migration `plant-021-ih-mapping` (additive: `plant_ih_checks`, `plant_ih_monitor_links`).
- **Privileged platform identities** ([docs/PLATFORM-SIGN-IN.md](docs/PLATFORM-SIGN-IN.md#privileged-identities), [ADR 0009](docs/adr/0009-platform-identities.md) step 4 without the PAM checkout, which waits for its own decision record). Platform managers mark an identity **Privileged** (never the default identity, `IDENTITY_DEFAULT_PRIVILEGED`; turning it off asks for a step-up); a _Privileged_ badge shows it in the _Identities_ tab, the run dialog, the run detail, AI requests and the credentials register. The new assurance control point **`platform.privilegedIdentity`** (group identity, step-up on, action `usePrivilegedIdentity`) asks for a step-up when a run starts with one (also _Run now_ of a schedule), a schedule or webhook trigger that names one is saved, the request explorer sends with one, an MCP request with one is approved, and a start approval is approved – API tokens cannot confirm, so scripts cannot start privileged runs. Its four-eyes (off by default; the policy can make it required) and the new process setting **_Privileged runs need a second person_** (`privilegedFourEyes`) make such a run start in **waiting** with a **start approval** before its first step: a person with the approve right on the process approves it – never the starter, nor the owner of its schedule, trigger or monitor – with a step-up and never in Microsoft Teams; rejected or not decided within 24 hours, the run is cancelled (`run.start_approval_requested`, `run.start_approval_expired`, job `start-approvals`). Scheduled and triggered runs wait the same way; test runs never wait; the flag counts as resolved at the start, so a running run never gains an approval mid-way. **MCP**: a privileged identity is _off_ or _approved writes_ with a second person – no reads (`privileged_refused`), `IDENTITY_PRIVILEGED_MCP` for other modes, setting the flag tightens the stored mode. Monitors never use a privileged identity (`IDENTITY_PRIVILEGED_BACKGROUND`). **Credentials register**: an entry can name the identity it documents (`identityId`, `?identity=` filter); the _Identities_ tab shows each identity's entries with status and next due date and warns about a privileged identity without one; new credentials offer _This is a rotation_ (on by default), recorded on those entries as `credential.rotated` with the identity. The PDF run report names each slot's identity (marked _privileged_), as do the run protocol, `run.started` and `/api/v1` `RunDetail.bindings.privileged` (OpenAPI regenerated). Connector types carry the flag. Migrations `platforms-008-start-approval` (additive: `run_tasks.start_approval`) and `credentials-005-identity` (additive: `credentials.identity_id`). Nothing changes until someone marks an identity privileged.

## [0.13.0] - 2026-11-10

Steward 0.13.0 makes monitoring aware of planned states and gives plant data a reviewed change path. Monitors get states with expected results: an asset that is off as planned shows _off_ and stays quiet, only checks that contradict each other alert, and a required heartbeat, a maximum off time and planned downtime keep a real outage from hiding as Off. Freshness checks take an allowed delay and measure the delay they see, with a suggestion to apply. Platforms get several identities – each with its own credentials, access list, MCP mode, read-only flag and operation filter – chosen per run, schedule, trigger, monitor and MCP call. Plant changes become change sets with a revision on every entry, drafts and proposals, review by a second person, history and revert; KPIs reach across profiles and parts. More lists sort and filter, the audit log on the server, and page headers show ids with a copy button. See _Check before updating_ under Changed.

### Added

- **KPI potential across profiles and parts** (plant registry, [docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#any-of-these-profiles)). **Bases across protocols**: a base profile's interface may have the protocol _other_ with data points without addresses; a profile that extends it sets the protocol and the addresses (`protocol` of an inherited interface – only where the base's is _other_ – in the API, the file format and the profile editor), and _Extract base profile_ no longer refuses data points on interfaces of different protocols: the base gets an interface of protocol _other_ (with an id of your choice, `interfaces` in the request) and every asset's model stays the same. **Any of these profiles**: a KPI input may name several device profiles (`profiles`; `profile` stays for one and is the first) with a data point **mapping** per profile (`mapping`) for models that cannot share a base – suggested in the editor by the base extraction's matching, refused when it names a data point the profile lacks; checks, units (the first profile's unit is expected), coverage, potential, data flow and lineage read each asset through its matching profile and the mapping, and id changes follow. **Roll-up from parts**: a KPI definition may count a part's job or delivered inputs for the machine it is part of (`rollUp: parts`, `roll_up` in the file format, off by default) – the coverage marks the machine _via part …_ and the part _counted for …_ and counts the KPI once per machine, also in the totals per site and area and on the site tiles; the asset page and the KPI page list the machine with the part. The **site tiles** show the KPI potential (_N ready · M need a collection_). API, MCP output, import format and JSON Schema additive (OpenAPI and schema regenerated). Migration `plant-015-kpi-roll-up` (additive).
- **Several identities per platform, and choosing them** ([docs/PLATFORM-SIGN-IN.md](docs/PLATFORM-SIGN-IN.md#identities), [ADR 0009](docs/adr/0009-platform-identities.md) steps 2 and 3). A platform keeps everything about the system once – base URL, specs, health, monitors – and has one or more **identities**: sets of credentials with their own name, sign-in settings (or the platform's), variables (never those that decide where requests go: `IDENTITY_VARIABLE_FIXED`), credentials in the vault, access list, owning teams, MCP mode, **read-only flag** and **operation filter**, shown as "Snowflake · Read only". The _Identities_ tab lists them and edits them in a dialog (platform managers). The default identity is open to everyone with _use_ on the platform; every other identity is **closed** until listed – administrators, owning-team designers and operators, its access list – and managing the platform does not make someone its user. A new default, an identity's access list or owning teams, loosening it (read-only off, a wider filter) and approved MCP writes need a step-up; everything is audited on the platform (`platform.identity_*`). Runs, schedules, webhook triggers, sub-processes (inherited), monitor checks and outputs, the request explorer and MCP (`identity` argument, mode per identity, `mcpIdentities` in `list_platforms`) name the identity they use – checked for the person or the owner when saved and at every fire or run (new skip reasons `mayNotUseIdentity`, `identityMissing`); a slot may need an identity that can write (`identity.writes`, `IDENTITY_WRITE_REQUIRED`). Runs record the identity per slot (run detail, `run.started`, `/api/v1` `RunDetail.bindings.identityId|identityName`; schedules `identities`, additive). A **read-only** identity refuses every changing request before anything is sent (`IDENTITY_READ_ONLY`; also in dry and test runs, for access-change steps and browser interactions); an **operation filter** (_Read_ or _Chosen_ operations of the platform's specs, the most specific operation deciding) refuses every other request (`IDENTITY_OPERATION_REFUSED`), on every redirect hop; stale references after a spec change match nothing and are counted. Connector types carry identity templates; _Copy specs_ can copy the operation filters. Seals, key rotation, backups and migration packages cover every identity; an identity of one platform can never be used with another. Migrations `platforms-006-identity-choices` (additive: `schedules.identities`, `webhook_triggers.identities`, `mcp_requests.identity_id`) and `platforms-007-identity-disabled` (additive: `platform_identities.disabled`). A platform with only its default identity behaves as before.
- **Monitor states: off is not an outage** ([docs/MONITORS.md](docs/MONITORS.md#states-off-is-not-an-outage), [ADR 0021](docs/adr/0021-monitor-states.md)). A monitor may have `states:` – a decision table whose rows (Running, Off, Standby …) select a state by a condition on the checks that react first (`when`), name **required** checks (`requires`, e.g. the gateway's own heartbeat) and the **expected** statuses of the others (`expect`); the first matching row wins, _Otherwise_ evaluates as before. When every check agrees, the monitor shows the state's status – the new status **`off`** (code **6**, grey, never an alert) for an off state – and only a **contradiction** is an issue: down (or degraded), root cause the deviating cells of the closest state ("PowerMeter reachable is down, but 3 checks say Running"). A **settle time** after each state change (derived from the expected checks' freshness windows and confirmations, or `settleSeconds`) lets lagging data paths follow; a required check that is not up makes the monitor down at once ("Gateway Hall 2 alive is silent – Off cannot be told from an outage"); off for longer than the **maximum off time** (`maxOffHours`, default 8 h) is degraded and notifies once; more than 4 state changes within an hour is degraded. A monitor can **follow** another monitor's state (`states.from`; cycles refused with `STATES_CYCLE`). States are part of the definition (YAML export and import, Git sync, templates, the API). The editor has a **States** tab – the decision table with _Matches now_ and **Try it**, computed by the same shared code as the engine – and the monitor page a grey _Off_ banner instead of the red root cause and a **Why this state** panel. Uptime, objectives with the monitor as source and the reports' availability section leave Off out and show it apart (_Off as expected_, a column _Off_). New notification event **`monitor.state`** for state changes as information (opt-in per notification output, `stateChanges`); Gatus outputs choose what to send while off (`whileOff: success | failure | skip`, default success); `sm_monitor_state{monitor,state}`; `state` (and on a contradiction `contradiction`) in the webhook payload and `/api/v1` (OpenAPI regenerated); the status wall sorts and can hide off monitors; plant live states draw off as offline in grey. The examples _Machine 17 – PowerMeter_ and _Machine 17 – machine data_ have states, with the new example _Gateway Hall 2_ (`09-gateway-hall-2.yaml`); the demo adds _Lathe 5_ (off, all checks agree) and _Mill 6_ (a contradiction). A monitor without states behaves exactly as before. Migrations `monitors-004-states` and `catalogue-014-downtime` (additive).
- **Planned downtime** – a kind of maintenance window (`kind: downtime`, [docs/APPLICATIONS.md](docs/APPLICATIONS.md#maintenance-windows)) for weekends, holidays and shutdowns: statuses stay as they are, monitors with states do not count it towards their maximum off time and still alert contradictions, monitors without states and objectives are not affected, and nothing is announced unless a reminder is set.
- **Freshness checks: an allowed delay, and the delay Steward observes** ([docs/MONITORS.md](docs/MONITORS.md#how-fresh-is-fresh-delays-in-insights-hub)). The freshness fields take `allowedDelaySeconds` (0–86400, default 0) – for `freshness` (both sources), `heartbeat` and the DynamoDB check: data may arrive this much later than its timestamp; `maxAge` is down after `maxAgeSeconds` + the allowance, `cadence` counts missed intervals from the age minus it, the look-back of queries grows by it, and messages name it. Insights Hub and API-source freshness checks measure, per variable, how old the newest value gets before a newer one is readable (**peak age**) and how late new values arrive (**delay**), as lower and upper bounds from consecutive reads, for 7 days (20-minute slots for the last 24 hours, 2-hour slots before; stored compactly, about 3 KB per variable, and only when something changed); failed reads, timestamps that go backwards or lie in the future (clock skew) and samples during maintenance are counted, not used, and a change of the check's source starts anew. The status uses the newest timestamp seen from the same source, so a lagging read (a timestamp that goes backwards) never makes data look older. The check's panel and the editor show the figures of the variable that gets oldest and, from 24 hours and 20 samples on, a suggestion (95 % of the peak age's upper bounds, rounded up to the minute, plus one check interval) that _Apply suggestion_ puts into the form, old and new values shown. `observedDelay` on each check in `GET /api/monitors/:id` and `GET /api/v1/monitors/{monitorId}/status` (OpenAPI regenerated). The Insights Hub mock simulates data paths – batched uploads, ingest delay, jitter, clock skew, outages, reads that go backwards or fail; the demo's PowerMeter PM-17 uploads every 5 minutes, and its example monitor allows 6 minutes. Migration `monitors-003-observed-delay` (additive).
- **Plant change sets: one path for every change to plant data** (preview, [docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#change-sets), [ADR 0020](docs/adr/0020-plant-change-sets.md), phase 1). Edits, imports, changes of many entries, id changes and assistants' proposals each become a change set – operations against what the registry held when they were written – with one preview, review, transactional apply, history and audit trail. Every entry has a **revision**; an edit form sends the one it loaded and gets the dialog _Changed by someone else_ when someone changed the entry since – a choice per field, _Keep mine_ or _Take theirs_ (`409 PLANT_ENTRY_CHANGED`; the in-place forms and _Move to…_ too). The new site right **propose** (by default operators, approvers and plant viewers) keeps **drafts** – _Propose a change_ on site, asset, device profile and KPI definition pages and the forms of the hierarchy, the OT network (devices, data paths, VLANs, zones, conduits, checks) and data collections for who may only propose, **Add to draft** in the import's preview and in _Edit selected_ (their previews for who may only propose, too), a **draft bar** on the plant pages – and proposes them. **Plant → Changes** lists _My drafts_, _To review_, _Applied_ and _Closed_ (assistants' proposals there and under _AI requests_); the **review page** shows what applying does now, field by field, with a **three-way merge** – _When drafted_, _Now in Steward_, _This change_ – and a choice for each conflict, and _Apply_, _Reject_, _Back to the author_, _Withdraw_, _Copy_. Applying needs the **fingerprint** of the preview the person saw (`PLANT_CHANGESET_CHANGED`); the import, bulk and rename previews return one too (`PLANT_IMPORT_CHANGED`, `PLANT_BULK_CHANGED`, `PLANT_RENAME_CHANGED`). The new assurance control point **`plant.changeSet`** (off by default) needs a **second person**: _optional_ – each site's switch _Changes need a second person_ (step-up; never in the import format) and the catalogue's – or _required_; under review every change goes through a draft or a proposed change set, no author applies (administrators included), and an import, bulk change or rename through its route answers `202` with the change set – also for API tokens. Every applied change writes **history** (full values before and after, revision, author, who applied it): a **History** tab on sites, assets, device profiles and KPI definitions, history that follows renames, survives the archiving of audit entries and moves with migration packages, and **Revert** of a change set or one row as a new draft (sets, additions and renames; removals in phase 2). Every import records its **baselines** (the values it wrote per entry) for the coming flags _changed in Steward since the last import_. Existing audit actions carry `changeSetId`; new `plant.change_set_*` actions, and `plant.change_set_applied` with the SHA-256 of the operations and of the history rows written. Assistants may propose with the _propose_ right; under review a token owner never applies their assistant's proposal. **Messages:** `plant.change_proposed` tells who may apply a proposed change set (never its authors, only who sees its sites), `plant.change_decided` its authors when someone else applied, rejected or returned it – with the comment – or it expired; in the app and by e-mail by default, in Teams per the routes, with quiet hours and digests (en, de). Revisions and history are tracked over what a change can reach – an edit's entry, a bulk edit's entries, an import's kinds – and written in batches: on a plant of 5,000 assets imports, edit form saves and assistants' proposals take about as long as in 0.12 and a bulk change of 500 entries under 0.1 s on SQLite (0.6 s on PostgreSQL) – as first merged an asset's save took 50 ms instead of 3 and such a bulk change over a second; history and baselines add about 1.5 kB per imported entry (measured on SQLite and PostgreSQL 16, `server/test/plant-perf.test.ts` with `SM_PLANT_PERF=1`, [ADR 0020](docs/adr/0020-plant-change-sets.md#performance)). Limits: 200 operations per change set, 20 open per person. Expired, rejected and withdrawn change sets go after 90 days, the history of removed entries after 3 years (job `plant-changes`). Migrations `plant-013-revisions` and `plant-014-change-sets` (additive: a `revision` on every entry table, five new tables, `review` on sites; pending proposals become proposed change sets).

### Changed

- **Confirming platform settings changed outside Steward keeps identities** ([docs/PLATFORM-SIGN-IN.md](docs/PLATFORM-SIGN-IN.md#identities)). The dialog lists each identity with its MCP mode, credential names, variables, own sign-in, flags, operation filter and access list. Confirming keeps and seals the default identity and every identity whose own seal verified; an identity added or changed outside Steward is kept but **disabled** – nothing can use it, no credentials are sent with it – until an administrator enables it one by one (step-up, audited `platform.identity_enabled`). Before, every identity but the default one was removed and the default's own settings were reset. **Check before updating:** after confirming, look for _Disabled_ identities on the platform's _Identities_ tab and enable only those whose settings and access list you have read; schedules, triggers and monitors that name a disabled identity are skipped until then. An identity's own sign-in settings may only send its credentials to the platform's own origins (`IDENTITY_AUTH_ORIGIN`). In `list_platforms` (MCP), `mcpGateway` is true when at least one identity is open to the token owner over MCP (with only the default identity: as before). Marking a default identity read-only also stops Steward's own writers that use it (monitor outputs, backups to S3) – give them an identity that can write.
- **A new monitor status `off` (code 6)** for monitors with states ([docs/MONITORS.md](docs/MONITORS.md#states-off-is-not-an-outage)). Codes 0–5 do not move. **Check before updating:** consumers that enumerate statuses – a Zabbix value map (add the row 6 → Off), API clients or scripts with a fixed list, rules on `sm_monitor_status > 1` (they fire on maintenance and off) – learn `off`; nothing changes until a monitor gets states. `upAndDown` and the uptime figures count `off` neither way. A sub-monitor that is off counts as up for a parent without states (as one in maintenance does).
- **Sortable, filterable lists for systems, services, applications, schedules, triggers, the plant registry (sites, assets, device profiles, KPI definitions), duties, maintenance windows, reports, evidence packs, AI requests, Git updates, document types, person reviews, the access reconciliation and backups.** They use the list table of the other main lists: every column with a sensible order sorts (dates as dates, names with numbers in order, criticality and status by severity), the chosen order is remembered per user, and phones show cards with a sort menu; the plant lists keep their selection and _Edit selected_ / _Remove selected_. Systems filter by kind, environment and owner, services by criticality, owner and archived, applications by service, criticality, protection need and archived, schedules by state and owner, triggers by state and authentication, person reviews and evidence packs by status – with a search field and all filters in the address, so a filtered list can be shared; the plant lists' search, archived duties, cancelled maintenance windows, the AI requests' scope and the Git updates' view are in the address too. Person reviews, findings, snapshots, evidence packs, generated reports, AI requests, Git updates and backup runs start newest first.
- **Plant registry: extracting a base profile no longer refuses interfaces of different protocols** ([docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#extracting-a-base-profile)): the base's interface gets the protocol _other_ and each profile sets its own; the problem code `protocol` is no longer reported. The unit a KPI input of several profiles expects is the first profile's.
- **The audit log sorts and filters on the server.** Its table is the shared list table now (cards on phones; the entries still open one or several at a time with their number): _Time_, _Actor_, _Action_ and the new column _Target_ (entity type and id) sort over the whole log, not only the loaded entries – newest first by default – and _Load more_ continues in the chosen order without gaps or repeats, also while new entries arrive. The search, category, actor, target type and a date range (_From_, _To (inclusive)_) are filters, and they and the order are in the address (`?q=`, `?action=`, `?actor=`, `?type=`, `?from=`, `?to=`, `?sort=`, `?order=`), so a filtered view can be shared. `GET /api/audit` and `GET /api/v1/audit` take `sort` (`time`, `actor`, `action`, `target`) and `order` (`asc`, `desc`); the internal list continues with `afterSeq`, the public API with its cursor (OpenAPI regenerated); `GET /api/audit/facets` lists the actors and target types for the filters. Who may read the log is unchanged. Migration `audit-002-target-sort` (an index; additive).
- **Ids with a copy button in the page headers.** The pages of platforms, processes, monitors, systems, applications, services and dashboards show the entry's id under the title with a _Copy id_ button (it announces _Copied_) – the id the public API, YAML files and MCP clients use; the edit dialogs of schedules and triggers show theirs the same way. The process page showed its id already, now with the button.
- **Development: a lighter web typecheck** ([docs/I18N.md](docs/I18N.md#writing-and-changing-texts)): translation keys are still checked at compile time – a typo or a removed key fails `npm run typecheck` – but TypeScript needs about a third less memory for the web app (1.57 instead of 2.44 GB, 0.93 instead of 1.28 million types, 9.5 instead of 15.4 million instantiations), so the web typecheck runs in Node's default heap again (no `--max-old-space-size=4096`). The keys come flat per namespace from the new generated file `web/src/i18n/keys.gen.ts` (`npm run i18n:types`; `npm run typecheck` writes it first, CI checks it with `--check` – commit it with the English texts), and `t()` has a simple first overload for a key of the hook's namespaces; calls it does not cover (a `context` option) still go through i18next's own types. No call changes, the runtime is unchanged.
- **Plant registry edits carry a revision; a stale one is refused** ([docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#change-sets)). Entries in the internal API and `/api/v1` have `revision`; the edit routes take it in the body (`DELETE`: `?revision=`) and answer `409 PLANT_ENTRY_CHANGED` with what the form needs (`entryChanged`: the values when loaded, now and the person's, and the conflicting fields) when it is not the entry's revision any more. **Check before updating:** a client that sends `revision` back – for example one that sends an entry back as it read it, as the collection editor does – gets `409` when the entry changed in between; read the entry again, or leave `revision` out to write as before (no revision sent, no check). With `plant.changeSet` on for a site, the edit routes answer `202` with `changeSet` (the person's draft) and the import, bulk and rename routes `202` with a proposed change set – scripts writing into such a site get a change set instead of a result. A site's `permissions` stay _view_ and _manage_; the new _propose_ right is `canPropose`.

## [0.12.0] - 2026-11-09

Steward 0.12.0 documents plant integrations end to end. Assistants read the plant registry over MCP and propose additions and changes for a person to apply; descriptions follow device profile inheritance; documents attach to device profiles, data collections and sinks; imported gateway configurations are kept without their secrets, as versions that can be compared; and data collections name their sinks and transformations. The OT network is edited where it is shown, with sorting, filters and data quality hints, and entries move in the hierarchy. KPIs work across device models: a base profile can be extracted from concrete ones, template profiles stay unassigned, KPI inputs check units, and KPI coverage shows which assets are ready because their data is already collected. Long pages fold their secondary content consistently, and Insights Hub freshness checks no longer take old records for the newest. See _Check before updating_ under Changed.

### Added

- **Edit the OT network in place** (plant registry, [docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#editing-in-place)). The site's _OT network → Devices_ and _Data paths_ tabs add, edit and remove entries with the asset's own forms (_Add device_ / _Add data path_ ask for the asset), and selected devices change together with _Edit selected_ / _Remove selected_ and the bulk preview. Both lists sort by every column, filter (VLAN, state, asset; asset for data paths), count what they show and become cards on a phone; inactive and retired devices are shown quieter.
- **Move to… in the plant hierarchy** ([docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#move-to)): a work center to another area, a work unit to another work center (with what is below it; its assets follow) and an asset to another place of its site, from a keyboard-friendly dialog. A moved node is placed last below its new parent.
- **Data quality hints for a site's devices** ([docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#data-quality-hints)): a shared IP address, MAC address or host name, a device without a VLAN or with a VLAN the site does not have, and switch names that differ only by letter case – in a _Data quality (N)_ section above the devices (each device leads to its row) and as a count on the overview. `dataQuality` in `GET /api/plant/sites/:siteId/network` and `/api/v1` (additive).
- **Documents on device profiles, data collections and sinks, with what they inherit** (plant registry, [docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#documents)). New link kinds `device-profile`, `data-collection` (`<asset>/<collection>`) and `data-sink` (`<asset>/<collection>/<sink>`): records link to them as far as the reader sees the profile or the asset's site. Sites, assets, device profiles and data collections have a **Documents** panel – the linked records with _New document_ (the link set), _Link existing_ and _Upload file_ – and, read-only and marked _Inherited_, the documents of a derived profile's bases, of an asset's profile chain and of its collections and sinks; records the reader may not read are counted, not named. Renames update the links; removals take them along only with _Also remove what belongs to it_. `GET /api/plant/documents?kind=&id=`.
- **Gateway configurations kept, versioned and compared** (plant registry, [docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#gateway-configurations), [ADR 0019 – Update 0.12](docs/adr/0019-data-collections.md#update-012-sinks-kept-gateway-configurations-documents)). Each import of data collections keeps the collector's files **with secrets removed** as a version per asset and collector (the same files make no new version, at most 50 are kept): listed with who imported them, the hash of the original and the paths where values were removed, downloadable, and compared by path (JSON, YAML) and by line. The import's audit entry names the version. Each import also keeps **one living document per collection** up to date – the new built-in type **GWC – Gateway configuration**, linked to the asset and the collection, with a generated summary that each import rewrites, the kept file attached and people's text below it; a published one gets a new edition, one in review is left alone, and who may not create records gets the import without it. `GET /api/plant/assets/:assetId/collection-sources[/diff | /:sourceId/file]`. Migration `plant-010-collection-sources` (additive).
- **Sinks and transformations: where collected data goes** (plant registry, [docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#sinks-and-transformations)). A data collection has any number of **sinks** – a Steward system, platform, application or dataset, or a target as text, with the object there (topic, table, bucket path), format, schedule, owner and description – each with ordered **transformation steps** (rename, scale, unit, aggregate, filter, enrich, compute, format) and an **item mapping**; documentation only, nothing is ever run. The importers fill them where the format names them (sections of a gateway JSON recognised by structure, Node-RED sink nodes with their function code as text, the connector's Databus, the table columns `sink` and `target`); a re-import keeps what people wrote. The collection's tab _Where the data goes_ shows items → steps → sinks → the data paths and dashboards that read them next; a data point's page says where it goes; the asset flow and lineage draw every sink – naming only what the reader may see. Format 1 takes `collection_sinks` per asset network (JSON Schema regenerated). `GET …/collections/:collectionId/flow`. Migration `plant-011-collection-sinks` (additive): a stored destination is read as the first sink.
- **AI assistants read the plant registry and propose changes to it** (0.12.0, [docs/MCP.md](docs/MCP.md#plant-registry), [docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#proposals-from-assistants)). New MCP tools read sites with their hierarchy, assets with their assignment (profile, endpoints, overrides, additions), device profiles resolved with their chain of bases and their own part, data points by known-as names with who delivers them, KPI definitions, data collections with their coverage, and the import format's JSON Schema – with the token owner's site rights, paged and bounded, listed only while the module is on (plant viewers still cannot use MCP clients). `propose_plant_changes` takes a document of the plant import format (at most 48 kB), runs the import's preview as the token owner – nothing is written – and puts the proposal under **AI requests**: added and changed entries, each change field by field. A proposal only **adds and changes**: `remove:`, removing what the document leaves out, id renames, other modes and any change that would **drop an item from a list** inside an entry (an override, interface, data point, known-as name, tag …; `PLANT_PROPOSAL_REMOVES`) are refused – when proposed and again when applied. A person always applies a proposal, without a step-up. Proposals take data collections with their items and sinks – a gateway or ETL integration end to end – and a document with a credential-looking value (a password, token or key by its key or as a setting, a password in a URL) is refused, by the rules that redact kept gateway configurations. `get_data_collection` returns a collection's sinks and, with `file: true`, the collection in the import format. A person who may import into the registry applies it; the preview runs again with their rights, a preview that changed since needs their confirmation (`PLANT_PROPOSAL_CHANGED`), one with problems cannot be applied (`PLANT_PROPOSAL_INVALID`), and the import core writes it in one transaction. Proposals expire after 14 days like the other AI requests. Audited as `plant.proposal_created`, `plant.proposal_applied` (with `plant.imported`) and `plant.proposal_rejected`. Table `plant_proposals` (migration `plant-009-proposals`, additive; not moved by migration packages).
- **Data collections in the plant import format** (0.12.0, [docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#ot-network)): an asset network takes `collections` – collector, interface, mode, interval, triggers, access patterns, settings, description and the items with their addresses, the collector's names and the data points they are (or none: raw) – next to `collection_sinks`, which may now name a collection of the same files. The export writes them and they import back unchanged; an import adds and changes them with the preview (each change field by field) and the site's manage right, checks their data points against the asset's model after the import (the same file may add one), and never removes a collection. JSON Schema regenerated; the preview's change kind `collection`.
- **Descriptions follow profile inheritance; collections get descriptions** (0.12.0, [docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#profile-inheritance)). A derived device profile inherits its base's description and the descriptions of inherited interfaces, data point groups, data points and file sets; the profile page marks them _Inherited from …_, and an editor describes one anew (stored in the profile's own part) or goes back to the inherited text. The data point page says where its description comes from; the resolved profile carries `descriptionFrom`, also per item. The export writes only the own part, and an inherited group described anew round-trips. Added data points and interfaces of an asset show their descriptions. Data collections and their items get an optional description (the table format reads a `description` column; migration `plant-008-collection-description`, additive).
- **Extract a base profile from two or more device profiles** (0.12.0, [docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#extracting-a-base-profile)). Choose the profiles (none with a base yet), a leading one and the data points they share – matched by id, known-as name and name (same unit preferred), changeable by hand –, preview, then one audited transaction (`device_profile.base_extracted`) creates the base (a template by default) and makes each profile extend it: differing ids are renamed with the id rename (old ids stay aliases; assignments, KPI definitions and data collections follow), fields that differ stay as the profile's overrides. The model of every asset is compared before and after and must not change. Data points on an interface of another protocol or in another group are refused with the reason. `POST /api/plant/profiles/extract-base[/candidates|/preview]`.
- **Template profiles** (0.12.0, [docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#profile-inheritance)): a device profile can be marked _only for other profiles to extend_ (`template` in the API and the import format, migration `plant-012-profile-template`). No asset can have one – an assignment, _Edit selected_, an import or a proposal naming it is refused (`PLANT_PROFILE_TEMPLATE`); a profile assets have cannot become one (`PLANT_PROFILE_TEMPLATE_IN_USE`). A badge and a _Kind_ filter on the profile list.
- **Units of KPI inputs are checked** (0.12.0, [docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#units-of-kpi-inputs)). A KPI input may state the unit it expects per data point (`units`; default: the data point's unit in the input's profile, the base's for an input naming a base). The new check `kpi_unit_mismatch` flags an asset that delivers another unit and says whether it converts by a pure factor (Wh ↔ kWh) or not – on a KPI job (a problem), in the coverage cell, on the KPI page's candidates and the asset's _KPIs it can have_.
- **KPI potential in the coverage** (0.12.0, [docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#kpi-coverage)): _Could have it_ is split into **Collected – ready** (a data collection of the asset maps every input) and **Device delivers, not collected**, with the totals (_N ready, M need a data collection_) and a table per site and area; the KPI page and the asset mark each candidate. The API stays compatible: the state remains `could`, the cell gets `collected`, the answer `potential` and `groups`, the site tiles `potential`.
- **The plant import's preview shows what differs** ([docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#modes)): a changed record lists its differences field by field, with the stored and the new value (the first 500 changed records; `diff` on each change in `POST /api/plant/import/preview`), and `drops` names the items a change drops from lists inside the record. On a phone the changes are cards.

### Changed

- **Not watched instead of Unknown** for an asset or device without linked checks ([docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#live-status)): a quiet badge, while _Unknown_ now means a linked check has no result. The site's counts show _Not watched_ apart from _Unknown_; when nothing on a site is watched, the overview and device lists leave out the _Live_ column and say where checks are linked. The API is unchanged (`state: unknown`, `watched: false`). A device without an address shows its address type alone (_Dynamic (DHCP)_).
- **Plant registry: collections have sinks; deleting what documents link to asks first** ([docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#sinks-and-transformations)). A collection's `destination` is now its first sink (`sinks` in the API and `/api/v1`); a Node-RED import names the broker or server of a sink node as its target (`destination.ref`) instead of the node type. `DELETE /api/plant/profiles/:profileId` refuses a profile that records link to (`PLANT_PROFILE_LINKED`), `DELETE …/collections/:collectionId` a collection (`PLANT_COLLECTION_LINKED`, `?cascade=1` takes the links along). **Check before updating:** API clients that write `destination` keep working (it becomes the one sink); clients that send a collection back as they read it must leave out `sinkRefs` as they leave out `destinationRef`.
- **Records and manual steps accept `.json`, `.yaml` and `.yml` files** ([docs/RECORDS.md](docs/RECORDS.md#attachments)), checked as text like `.csv` and always downloaded as attachments.
- **A derived device profile without a description of its own now shows its base's** (and the same for an inherited group or file set without one). **Check before updating:** where a derived profile left its description empty on purpose, give it its own text; the stored data is not changed, and the export is the same.
- **An asset's _Source_ folds a long list of import files**: with more than two files it reads _Import of N files, imported …_ with _Show all files_; an import keeps its files, not which file each entry came from, so no file is named as the entry's.
- **Long pages fold their secondary content** ([docs/reviews/UX-REVIEW.md](docs/reviews/UX-REVIEW.md#v0120-collapsible-content)). Sections are headings with a button that folds them; the folded header keeps the count and what needs attention, and actions such as _Add_ stay beside it. A section with entries or a problem starts open, an empty one folded; an error, a link to `#<section>` and printing open it; pages with many sections offer _Expand all_ / _Collapse all_. Where: the application page (each section's choice kept per user for every application page; on phones the lists start folded), the interfaces of a device profile (open up to three; `#iface-<id>`), the parts of an asset's data and KPIs, the site hierarchy (areas open, work centers folded; `#node-<id>` opens the path), links (the first 10, then _Show all N_) and _Linked from_ (folded with more than 10, the choice kept per user), the start page cards _Waiting for you_, _Credentials due_ and _Compliance_ (folded like the failed checks, opened again by new items), the firewall panel (a segment lists its first 10 systems), a credential's _Used by_ (first 5) and a data point's assets (first 20). The advanced settings of a step, of an SSO provider and the migration plan's details say how many settings are set or changed and open for a problem or settings that differ from the default. On phones the settings areas fold behind _Area: <current area>_. Long lists end in one _Show all N_ / _Show fewer_ button everywhere.
- **Folded sidebar areas, pinned entries, the failed-checks card and the getting-started checklist are kept with the other per-user preferences** (`sm.pref.<user>.…`). The keys of before are read until the person changes something, so nothing is lost. The getting-started checklist is now hidden per user instead of per browser. **Check before updating:** browser automation that sets the old keys (`sm.nav.pins.<user>`, `sm.nav.groups.<user>`, `sm.failedChecks.open.<user>`, `sm.gettingStarted.*`) still works until the person changes the setting; after that only the new keys count.

### Fixed

- **Insights Hub freshness checks no longer report fresh data as old when the look-back holds more than 2,000 records** ([docs/MONITORS.md](docs/MONITORS.md)). For variables missing from the newest record the check reads a time window, and the API answers its oldest records first: with dense data the newest value found was the last of the first 2,000 – up to an hour old. A full page now makes the check read the window again in slices from now backwards, each paged to its end; a slice too dense to page through makes the check _unknown_ with the reason, never _down_. The built-in Insights Hub simulator now answers oldest records first, like the real API.
- **Tab counts of two or more digits are shown in full** (they were cut to a round badge), also in the sidebar and the diagram's filter button; tab bars no longer show a stray vertical scrollbar.
- **Identifiers no longer break at a hyphen** in the site's device list and the asset's device cards (MAC and IP addresses, VLANs, switches and ports, host names); the table scrolls sideways instead.
- **Run steps open with the keyboard.** Each step of a run is a button with `aria-expanded`; failed steps start open, and a link to `#step-<id>` opens that step.
- **Audit log entries open with the keyboard.** The entry number is a button with `aria-expanded`; several entries can be open at once.
- **The process editor's problem list opens when errors block saving**, also when they appear while editing, and its toggles say whether it is open (`aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`).
- **The section titles of the application page are headings** (they were bold text), so screen reader users can jump between them.

### Security

- **Collector configurations are kept without their secrets** ([docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#gateway-configurations)). Before an imported gateway, Node-RED, connector or table file is stored, attached or audited, values of keys that look secret are removed at any depth (passwords, tokens, API and private keys, credentials, auth headers, connection strings, client secrets), as are passwords in URLs and CSV columns with a secret header; Node-RED `credentials` are dropped before the importer reads the flow. Only the paths of removed values are kept, never the values; of the original only its SHA-256 and size. The importers also no longer copy a password in a URL into a collection (a Node-RED HTTP node's URL was taken as written).

## [0.11.0] - 2026-11-09

Steward 0.11.0 consolidates. Administrators switch off the areas a team does not use, the sidebar shows what a role can use, _Catalogue_ and _Registers_ are separate and a glossary and a documentation index help new users. The plant registry gains id changes with aliases, changes to many entries at once, device profile inheritance with asset-level additions, data collections that document what a gateway or ETL job reads (gateway JSON, CSV/YAML, Node-RED, Industrial Edge as a preview) and an OT network diagram in bands by level.
