Operations get a `pg_dump` before every PostgreSQL update, backups past 4 GB, a real PostgreSQL restore test, metrics for backups and readiness with alert rules, and secrets from files. Privacy: a per-person data export and pseudonymising a leaver. Security: local passwords by length and a list of common passwords instead of composition rules, MCP OAuth grants that end and revoke on refresh-token reuse, and monitor checks and outputs that only use a platform of their kind. See _Check before updating_ under Changed and Security.

### Added

- **Device profiles extend a base profile; assets add their own data points** (plant registry, [docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#profile-inheritance-and-asset-additions), [ADR 0007 – Update 0.11](docs/adr/0007-plant-registry.md#update-011-profile-inheritance-and-asset-additions)). A device profile may **extend** one base profile (several levels, no cycles): it inherits the base's interfaces, data points and files, adds its own, changes inherited fields (address, unit, data type, update rate, access …) and marks inherited items as **not present**. Changes to a base flow down; the profile page shows the chain of bases and where each item comes from (_Inherited_, _Changed here_, _Added here_). A base that another profile extends cannot be deleted (`PLANT_PROFILE_EXTENDED`); _Duplicate_ keeps `extends`. One resolved model is used everywhere – data point pages, who delivers what, assignments, checks, KPI inputs (a KPI that reads a base reads its derived profiles), coverage, data flow, lineage and the API. An asset's assignment may **add data points** to an interface of its profile and **whole interfaces** for that one asset; they count as delivered. Data points get **known-as names** (`aliases`). Format 1 gets the optional keys `extends`, `present`, `aliases` and `additions`; a derived profile is written with only its own part and imports back unchanged. Migration `plant-005-profile-extends` (additive).
- **Data collections: what a gateway or ETL job reads from an asset** (plant registry, [docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#data-collections), [ADR 0019](docs/adr/0019-data-collections.md)). Asset page → **Collections**: per collector (an application, platform, process or a job elsewhere) what it reads – mode, interval, debounce, triggers, destination, its access patterns (**write, create and delete are shown in red**), settings – and its items: the address, the collector's name for the value and the data point of the asset it is, shown as _Spindle speed · as `Spindle_speedActSP1`_. **Imports** of a gateway JSON with datasets of data points, a CSV or YAML table, the Industrial Edge OPC UA connector export (_preview_) and Node-RED flows (never running code, never reading credentials), with a preview that suggests a data point for each item – a mapping file, a remembered pair, the asset's own address, a name or known-as name, or a fuzzy name that waits for confirmation – and bulk actions. Confirming can record the item's address as the asset's address of the data point, remember the name (on the profile or only for the collector) and add unknown addresses as data points; a re-import needs no new work. Coverage counts mapped, raw and not-collected data points; the data flow and lineage run through the collections; application, platform and data point pages list them; mappings export as CSV or YAML. Rights follow the asset's site; audited as `plant.collection_created|updated|deleted|imported`. API: `/api/plant/assets/:assetId/collections…`, `GET /api/v1/plant/assets/{assetId}/collections`. Invented examples in `examples/plant/collections/`. Migration `plant-006-collections` (additive).
- **Modules: switch off what your team does not use** ([docs/MODULES.md](docs/MODULES.md), [ADR 0016](docs/adr/0016-module-registry.md)). _Settings → Modules_ lists the optional areas – compliance and evidence, status pages, scorecard, costs, data contracts, AI models, approvals in Microsoft Teams, shared procedure libraries, version watch and end of life, changedetection.io, the plant registry and its map – each with a switch. Switched off, an area leaves the sidebar, its tabs, settings and search results; its pages say _This area is switched off_; its routes answer `404 MODULE_OFF` (also in `/api/v1`); its background jobs and connectors stop. Its data stays and is back when it is switched on. Everything is on by default; the plant registry and its map stay off until an administrator turns them on. Switching needs a step-up (control point `modules.switch`) and is audited (`module.enabled`, `module.disabled`). API: `GET /api/settings/modules`, `PUT /api/settings/modules/:id`.
- **Maturity labels** ([docs/MODULES.md](docs/MODULES.md#maturity)): every module and every built-in connector is _stable_, _beta_ or _preview_; connectors also say whether they were tested against a real system or only against Steward's own mock. _Settings → Modules_, _Add platform_ and the platform page show the label, with what it means in a tooltip.
- **A glossary** under _Help → Glossary_ (English and German) and in [docs/GLOSSARY.md](docs/GLOSSARY.md): service, application, system, component, platform, process, run, step, decision, monitor, check, objective, document, credentials register, duty, team and owning team.
- **`GET /api/me/visibility`**: per sidebar area, whether the signed-in person may see something there or add something, and whether entries exist they may not see – two booleans per area, never counts or names. `GET /api/datasets/:key/visibility` says the same for a dataset.
- **Documentation index and a getting-started guide.** [docs/README.md](docs/README.md) lists every page once, grouped by task; [docs/GETTING-STARTED.md](docs/GETTING-STARTED.md) takes an administrator from the installation through unlocking, single sign-on, teams, the first platform and process, monitors, notifications and backups.
- **Release and support policy** ([docs/RELEASES.md](docs/RELEASES.md)): versioning, which versions get fixes, how releases are tested and how to verify a download. [SECURITY.md](SECURITY.md) follows it.
- **ADR 0017: audit retention** ([docs/adr/0017-audit-retention.md](docs/adr/0017-audit-retention.md)) records that archived audit entries can leave the database while the archives keep the chain verifiable; ADR 0003 points to it.
- **Export a person's data, and replace a leaver's data by a pseudonym** (GDPR Art. 15, 17 and 20, [docs/PRIVACY.md](docs/PRIVACY.md)). _Profile → Your data_ downloads what Steward holds about the signed-in person as one JSON file (`GET /api/me/data-export`, session only): the account, teams, the metadata of API tokens, sessions, MCP clients and emergency access (no secrets), settings, notifications, where the person is named – as the one who started, decided or wrote something, and as owner or responsible – and the audit entries they wrote or that are about their account (2000 per file, `audit.next` names the next one). Administrators download it for anyone under _Users_ (`GET /api/users/:id/data-export`, with a step-up). For a **deactivated** account, administrators can replace the personal data by a pseudonym such as `former-user-7f3a2c` (_Users_ → mask icon, `POST /api/users/:id/pseudonymise`; the user name typed again, a step-up, audited as `user.pseudonymised`): names, e-mail, the single sign-on profile and identity link and the password are removed; settings, notifications, held messages, the Teams chat, team memberships, sessions, API tokens, emergency access and MCP connections are deleted. The account id and the user name stay – the HMAC-chained audit entries name the user name and are never changed, so the chain still verifies. It cannot be undone: the account cannot be activated or changed again. New control points `user.pseudonymise` (step-up, baseline) and `personalData.export` (step-up, on by default) in the assurance policy. Both are audited (`user.data_exported`).
- **Automatic `pg_dump` before a PostgreSQL update** ([docs/INSTALL.md](docs/INSTALL.md#6-updating)): when an update has migrations to apply, Steward first dumps its schema with `SM_PG_DUMP` to `<data folder>/backups/pre-<version>-<UTC time>.pgdump` (custom format, 0600), as it copies a SQLite database. `SM_UPGRADE_BACKUPS` keeps the newest copies of either kind (`0` = none), `SM_UPGRADE_BACKUP_DIR` puts them elsewhere, `SM_UPGRADE_BACKUP_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` limits the dump (default 3600). A failed copy stops the start without changing the database, and says why; `SM_UPGRADE_BACKUP_REQUIRED=0` migrates anyway, with a warning and a note in the administrators' update notice. `db:copy` dumps an older PostgreSQL source the same way.
- **Backups past 4 GB and 65,535 files** ([docs/BACKUPS.md](docs/BACKUPS.md#limits-and-notes)): the backup archive uses ZIP64 where it is needed (up to 2 million files); `unzip`, 7-Zip, Windows Explorer and Python read it. A backup within the old limits stays a plain ZIP, byte for byte as before, and old backups restore as before. Very large backups go to S3 in larger parts (S3 takes at most 10,000).
- **PostgreSQL restore test with a real restore** ([docs/BACKUPS.md](docs/BACKUPS.md#the-restore-test)): with `SM_BACKUP_RESTORE_TEST_DATABASE_URL` (a database of its own, never the live one) the weekly restore test restores the dump there, checks the tables and the audit chain of the restored data like a SQLite snapshot, and drops it again. Without it, the test reads the dump's table of contents as before. A `pg_restore` of PostgreSQL 17 or newer reports the setting `transaction_timeout` as unknown on a server of version 16 or older; the test accepts that one error and fails on any other.
- **Metrics for backups, the vault and readiness, and alert rules** ([docs/METRICS.md](docs/METRICS.md)): `sm_backup_last_success_timestamp_seconds`, `sm_backup_last_failure_timestamp_seconds`, `sm_backup_last_size_bytes`, the same for the restore test, `sm_backup_enabled`, `sm_vault_unlocked`, `sm_ready`, `sm_readiness_check{check}`, `sm_build_info`, `sm_db_size_bytes` and `sm_db_free_bytes`. `examples/prometheus/steward-alerts.yml` has rules for Steward down, vault locked, not ready, scheduler stalled, failing background jobs, no backup for 26 hours, a failed backup or restore test, event-loop lag, server errors and stale datasets. `/api/metrics` now also answers while the vault is locked (with the same sign-in rules).
- **Metrics and trace spans for Steward's own API**: `sm_http_server_request_duration_seconds{http_request_method,http_route,http_response_status_code}` after the OpenTelemetry HTTP conventions (route templates, never paths), and with tracing on a `SERVER` span per request, which is the parent of the run, steps and platform calls it starts and continues a caller's `traceparent`.
- **Database timing**: `sm_db_statement_duration_seconds{dialect,operation}`, `sm_db_transaction_duration_seconds` and `sm_db_slow_statements_total`; statements over `SM_DB_SLOW_MS` (default 200 ms) are logged as `slow database statement` with the start of their SQL (placeholders, no values). Every API request's log line has `dbStatements` and `dbMs`.
- **Secrets from files**: every secret setting can come from a file – `SM_DATABASE_URL_FILE`, `SM_BACKUP_PASSPHRASE_FILE`, `SM_TRANSFER_PASSPHRASE_FILE`, `SM_SENTRY_DSN_FILE`, `SM_BACKUP_RESTORE_TEST_DATABASE_URL_FILE` – as Docker and Kubernetes mount secrets ([docs/CONFIGURATION.md](docs/CONFIGURATION.md)).
- **Kubernetes example** ([examples/kubernetes](examples/kubernetes/README.md)): Deployment (one replica, `Recreate`, uid 1000 with `fsGroup: 1000`, read-only root, the probes of the installation guide, the master passphrase as a Secret file), volume, Service, optional Ingress and a PostgreSQL variant, checked by a test.
- **Warnings for unknown `SM_*` variables** at start-up, with the closest known name (`did you mean SM_DATABASE_URL?`); never with their values.
- **`SM_LOG_LEVEL_FORMAT=label`** writes `"level":"info"` instead of `"level":30`, for log pipelines that expect words.
- **Free disk space after the clean-up** ([docs/INSTALL.md](docs/INSTALL.md#disk-space-after-a-clean-up)): new SQLite databases use incremental auto-vacuum, and the daily clean-up gives the space of deleted runs back in small steps (`SM_SQLITE_AUTO_VACUUM`). For an older database, `SM_SQLITE_VACUUM_AT_START=1` for one start rewrites the file before the server listens.
- **The demo shows the service half** ([README.md](README.md#quick-start-demo-with-simulated-platforms)). _Load demo platforms & processes_ on Home also creates three services built from the sample applications (_Online shop_, _Customer care_, _Plant monitoring_) with the demo monitors linked, an availability objective, a cluster in two environments, a decision record, an approved change record, a reviewed operations manual, three credential register entries (metadata only, never a secret), a vendor ticket, duties, a planned maintenance window, a status page and a weekly report schedule. Scorecard, Costs per service, the service hub and the Home tiles have content. All names are invented. The API adds this part only with `POST /api/demo/setup` and `{ "services": true }`; without it the demo stays as before.
- **Notification centre: groups and filters** ([docs/NOTIFICATIONS.md](docs/NOTIFICATIONS.md#the-bell)). The bell groups notifications by event, decision requests first. _All_, _Decisions_, _Alerts_ and _Updates_ filter the list, _Unread only_ hides what was read, and _Mark read_ marks one group read (`POST /api/notifications/read-all` takes `{ "events": [...] }`). An unread decision request stays in the list however many alerts arrive after it.
- **The run list is sorted by the server** ([docs/reviews/UX-REVIEW.md](docs/reviews/UX-REVIEW.md#backlog)). Sorting the Runs page by start, process, status, person or duration sorts all runs, not only the loaded page. `GET /api/runs` takes `sort` and `dir`; ties are broken by the run id, and _Load more_ continues with `after=<run id>`. Lists remember their sort order per user in the browser.
- **A Steward monitor as the source of an availability objective** ([docs/SCORECARD.md](docs/SCORECARD.md#from-a-steward-monitor), [ADR 0018](docs/adr/0018-one-maintenance-window-concept.md)). An objective names a monitor instead of a Gatus endpoint or a PromQL query; its figures are the monitor's time-weighted uptime over 1 h, 24 h, 7 days and 30 days from its recorded states (maintenance time left out). The scorecard, the service page, the monitor and scheduled reports show the same number for the same window. A service watched only by Steward monitors now has availability on its scorecard. The demo adds one such objective.
- **Maintenance windows can list monitors** ([docs/APPLICATIONS.md](docs/APPLICATIONS.md#maintenance-windows)): a window on _Operate → Maintenance_ can name monitors next to platforms, systems, applications and services (the _acknowledge_ right on each). The monitor page plans and shows its windows with the same form.
- **Scheduled reports take each monitor's SLA target from its objective** ([docs/REPORTS.md](docs/REPORTS.md)): _Use the objective_ in the section _Availability of monitors_ (on for new schedules) judges each monitor by the target of the objective window closest to the report period.
- **ADR 0018: one maintenance-window concept; monitors as an objective source** ([docs/adr/0018-one-maintenance-window-concept.md](docs/adr/0018-one-maintenance-window-concept.md)).
- **Change the id of a plant registry entry** ([docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#changing-ids)): sites, buildings, areas, work centers, work units, assets, device profiles with their interfaces and data points, KPI definitions, VLANs, zones, conduits, devices and data paths get a new id through one rename core. Every reference follows in one transaction – the plant tables, links of records, services and tickets (also `<site>/<conduit>`), owning teams, credentials' _used by_, copies, earlier imports – and the old id stays an alias: reads of the internal API and `/api/v1/plant/…`, bookmarks in the web app (the address changes to the new id) and import files keep working; chains collapse. _More actions → Change id_ on the entry's page (and in the site tree for nodes and buildings) checks the new id while it is typed and lists what refers to the entry. A new entry cannot take an old id until an administrator removes it under _Settings → Modules → Plant registry → Old ids_. `POST /api/plant/ids/rename[/preview]`, `GET /api/plant/aliases`, `DELETE /api/plant/aliases/:kind/:oldId`; audited as `plant.id_changed` and `plant.id_alias_removed`; table `plant_id_aliases` (migration `plant-004-id-aliases`, additive).
- **The plant import maps ids** ([docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#renaming-many-entries-with-an-import)): an optional step with old → new ids (pasted or loaded as CSV `kind,old,new` / `old,new` or YAML) and rules (replace or add a prefix, add a suffix, lower case – per kind). The mapping applies to the files' ids and references; an entry whose mapped old id exists is renamed, not added again, so export → map → import renames many entries. The preview lists renames and old ids found in the files apart from the other changes, and refuses two old ids for one new id, chains and cycles, taken ids and old ids of other entries. `idMap` in `POST /api/plant/import[/preview]`; audited as `plant.ids_changed`.
- **Change or remove many plant entries at once** ([docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#changing-many-entries-at-once)): the asset, site, device profile and KPI definition lists have a checkbox per row with _Edit selected_ (status, tags, building, place, device profile, manufacturer, model, domain, process area, role, end of support; category) and _Remove selected_. The import removes entries too – `remove:` in a file (kind and id), or _Remove entries that are not in the files_ (only in the sites and kinds the files list) – and the preview lists removals apart from the other changes. An entry that still has assets, devices, KPI instances, links or other parts is refused unless _Also remove what belongs to it_ is chosen; references from entries that stay always refuse it. Every change shows a preview first, skips entries the person may not change and applies in one transaction, audited once (`plant.bulk_changed`, `plant.bulk_removed`). `POST /api/plant/bulk[/preview]`, `removeMissing` and `cascade` in the import; the import format and its JSON Schema have the optional key `remove`.
- **Control points `plant.renameId`** (one entry's id; off by default) **and `plant.bulkChange`** (renaming or removing more than one plant entry at once, or editing more than 25; on by default) in the assurance policy ([docs/ASSURANCE.md](docs/ASSURANCE.md)).

### Changed

- **Plant registry: profile filters include derived profiles.** `GET /api/plant/assets?profile=` (and `/api/v1`), the coverage filter by profile and a data point's page now also count assets whose profile extends the named one. A profile's `interfaces` in the API are the effective ones (for a profile without a base nothing changes); the editor's own part is `own`. In the import format an interface's `protocol` may be left out for an inherited interface; a new interface still needs it. **Check before updating:** nothing changes until a profile extends another; tools that read `interfaces` of a derived profile get the inherited items too.
- **A plant import that removes or renames more than one record asks for a step-up** (control point `plant.bulkChange`), also _Replace an earlier import_ with _remove_. **Check before updating:** a script that runs such an import with an API token now gets `403`; run it in the web app, or let the import mark records instead of removing them.
- **Development: faster server tests and CI** ([docs/TESTING.md](docs/TESTING.md#running-the-server-tests)): the server test files run in parallel (half of the cores locally, 3 workers in CI) instead of one after another; `startTestServer` closes its server and all of its connections after the file's last hook, so a test file needs no teardown of its own and a keep-alive socket can no longer hold up the close; time limits in tests stretch with `timeLimit()` under parallel load and coverage. In GitHub CI the build runs in its own job, the server suite runs in shards behind gate jobs that keep the required check names (`build-test (Node 24)`, `build-test (Node 26)`, `test-postgres`) (Node 24 with coverage in three, Node 26 in two, PostgreSQL in three), and a `coverage` job merges the shards' coverage, reports it as before and **fails below a minimum** (statements 84 %, branches 73 %, functions and lines 87.5 %, about one point below today). Branches are tested only through their pull request (pushes run for `main` only; tags are built by the release and container workflows), so a commit no longer runs twice, and a new push cancels the running checks of its pull request. Woodpecker CI installs dependencies like GitHub CI (`npm ci --ignore-scripts` and the reviewed install scripts – before, every install script ran), and gains `i18n:literals`, the coverage minimum and the database platform tests (`.woodpecker/databases.yaml`).
- **The sidebar shows what a role can use.** An area whose items the person has no right to, that holds nothing for them and where they cannot add anything is left out – for example AI requests, access changes, access reconciliation and person reviews for viewers. The demo roles now see 18 (viewer) to 27 (administrator) entries instead of 27 to 33.
- **Catalogue and Registers** ([docs/GLOSSARY.md](docs/GLOSSARY.md)): _Catalogue_ holds Services, Applications, Systems, Platforms and Data contracts; Documents, Credentials, Ticket references and Duties are in the new area _Registers_; _Maintenance_ moved to _Operate_. _Scorecard_ and _Costs_ are tabs of _Services_. All addresses stay; `/registers` leads to Documents. The descriptions of Platforms, Applications, Systems and Components now say how they differ. **Check before updating:** tell your users where Documents, Credentials, Ticket references, Duties, Maintenance, Scorecard and Costs went.
- **Honest empty states**: a list that is empty for the person but holds entries they may not see says _Nothing here you may see_ and who to ask, instead of _No … yet_; a dashboard widget whose data is not theirs says so instead of _No data yet_.
- **The plant registry's own switch asks for a step-up** when it turns the module on or off (`PUT /api/settings/plant`, control point `modules.switch`); other plant settings do not. **Check before updating:** a script that turns the plant registry on or off with an API token now gets `403`; switch it in the web app.
- **Sidebar pin buttons are no longer tab stops**: press _P_ on a focused entry to pin or unpin it (one tab stop per entry). The shortcut list names it.
- **Home on desktop** folds two or more _Not set up yet_ tiles into one line, as on phones.
- **Internal: step types are modules behind a registry** (`server/src/engine/steps/`, [ADR 0011](docs/adr/0011-engine-boundary.md#update-011-step-types-behind-a-registry)). The engine's `runner.ts` keeps the run loop; nothing changes for runs or their protocols.
- **Internal: one composition root, a lighter PDF worker and an import check** ([docs/ARCHITECTURE.md](docs/ARCHITECTURE.md#how-features-are-wired)). Each feature has a manifest in `server/src/features/` (routes, public API, jobs, report sections, datasets, MCP tools, link kinds, migration lists) and `features/index.ts` is the one list that wires them; the PDF worker loads drawing code only (33 instead of 265 server modules, about 18 instead of 46 MB heap); `npm run check:imports` fails CI on a new import cycle or a broken layer rule. Nothing changes for users; in `docs/openapi.json` the ticket paths come before those of duties and the scorecard.
- **Internal: one descriptor per platform kind and per check type** ([docs/ARCHITECTURE.md](docs/ARCHITECTURE.md#platform-kinds-and-check-types)): `shared/src/platformKinds.ts` and `shared/src/checkTypes.ts` replace the check-type and kind lists kept in three places, the connection tests and access sources run through maps, and the monitor editor has one form per check type. Nothing changes for users, except the two fixes below.
- **Internal: one copy of each route helper.** `params()`, `requireProcess`, `requireRun(View)` and the role check `hasRole` each live in one module (`server/src/routes/params.ts`, `server/src/routes/helpers.ts`, `server/src/auth/permissions.ts`); an ESLint rule refuses new copies. Answers are unchanged.
- **Internal: checks for contributors.** A test matches every URL the web app calls against the server's routes and names the internal routes only scripts use (`server/test/web-route-contract.test.ts`); the web app's pure helpers have unit tests (`server/test/web-helpers.test.ts`); `Db.tx()` refuses a callback that returns a promise; `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` is on for `server/src`, `shared` and `web` (the server tests keep it off in `server/test/tsconfig.json`, which `npm run typecheck` now checks as well).
- **Docs.** The README no longer calls the MCP server read-only: since 0.2.10 an assistant can ask for changes that a person approves. Its node type table now lists all step types. `NOTICE` names the bundled endoflife.date data (MIT), with its licence text next to the files. `docs/INSTALL.md` and `docs/SECURITY-HARDENING.md` show image tags as `<version>` instead of 0.2.1, and INSTALL.md says that backups of PostgreSQL 18 or later need a matching `pg_dump` (`SM_PG_DUMP`).
- **The README is an overview now.** Writing processes and the node types, dashboards, platforms, users and roles, and the security model have their own pages ([PROCESSES.md](docs/PROCESSES.md), [DASHBOARDS.md](docs/DASHBOARDS.md), [PLATFORMS.md](docs/PLATFORMS.md), [USERS.md](docs/USERS.md), [SECURITY-MODEL.md](docs/SECURITY-MODEL.md)); PDF reports are in [REPORTS.md](docs/REPORTS.md). The dated reviews moved to `docs/reviews/`, the CI guides to `docs/ci/`. **Check before updating:** bookmarks to README sections or to those files need the new pages.
- **`npm run config:docs` writes only [docs/CONFIGURATION.md](docs/CONFIGURATION.md).** The README no longer repeats the variable table, and the `readme` flag of the configuration schema is gone.
- **Help → Documentation** offers _Getting started_ and _All documentation_ (the index); _About_ and the _Unattended operation_ card link the index and the guide.
- **User docs say what a feature does, not when it arrived.** Version and issue tags remain where an upgrade depends on them.
- **Local passwords: a length rule and a list of common passwords instead of composition rules** (NIST SP 800-63B, OWASP ASVS 5.0 V6.2, [docs/SECURITY-HARDENING.md](docs/SECURITY-HARDENING.md#sign-in)). A new password needs 12 to 256 characters – any characters, spaces too, no rules about digits or symbols – compared in Unicode NFKC form. It is refused when it is on the bundled list of common passwords (`server/assets/passwords/common-passwords.txt.gz`, compared without case; nothing is sent anywhere) or is one character or a short block repeated, or a run such as `abcdefghijkl`. The password fields explain the rule and the reason for a refusal (_This password is too common_). The set-up checks the administrator's password before it creates the vault. **Check before updating:** existing passwords keep working, also shorter ones; the rule applies when a password is set or changed. Scripts that create accounts with a password of 10 or 11 characters now get `400 PASSWORD_POLICY`.
- **Password hashes use scrypt N=2^17, r=8, p=1** (the OWASP minimum, as the vault key). Hashes from before (N=2^15) keep working and are replaced at the next successful sign-in or step-up; one-time passwords from a reset or `steward admin reset-password` get the new cost too. **Check before updating:** a password check now takes about four times as long (around 0.4 s) and about 128 MiB of memory while it runs, outside the JavaScript heap; `SM_PASSWORD_HASH_CONCURRENCY` (default 2) limits how many run at once – keep it low on small hosts. Going back to 0.10 afterwards keeps passwords of plain ASCII characters working; a password with characters that Unicode NFKC changes (ligatures, full-width letters) then needs a reset.
- **OAuth sign-in for MCP clients** ([docs/MCP.md](docs/MCP.md#sign-in-with-oauth-optional)). Self-registration of clients (RFC 7591) is off unless an administrator turns it on; installations that saved their OAuth settings keep their choice. New loopback redirect addresses must use `127.0.0.1` or `[::1]` (RFC 8252 §8.3); clients registered with `http://localhost` before keep working with their exact registered address, not with another port. `POST /oauth/revoke` needs the client's `client_id` and revokes only that client's tokens (RFC 7009 §2.1). `/.well-known/openid-configuration` answers `404`: Steward is no OpenID provider; the RFC 8414 document at `/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server` lists `response_modes_supported` and `service_documentation`. A connection now ends 90 days after the person allowed it, however often the client refreshes (_Sign in again after_, 1–730 days). **Check before updating:** connections older than 90 days end at their next refresh and the person allows the client again; an assistant that registers itself on a new installation needs self-registration turned on; a client that revokes without `client_id` gets `401 invalid_client`; a client that only reads the OpenID Connect discovery address needs to read the OAuth one.
- **Single sign-on errors on the sign-in page.** The callback redirects with a code (`?sso_error=SSO_STATE_UNKNOWN`, `SSO_IDP_ERROR`, `SSO_SIGN_IN_REFUSED&sso_ref=…` …) instead of the error's text, and the page shows a fixed, translated text (see _Security_). An identity provider's `error_description` is written to the operational log.
- **Release SBOM.** The CycloneDX SBOM of the npm production dependencies comes from npm's own `npm sbom` command (GitHub and Woodpecker), so no SBOM tool and no unpinned dependencies of it are downloaded at release time. The `Dockerfile` has a build argument `APT_UPGRADE` (default `1`): `0` builds the image from the pinned base image's packages alone. [docs/ci/SECURITY-PIPELINE.md](docs/ci/SECURITY-PIPELINE.md) states SLSA Build L2 (not L3), explains the `apt-get upgrade` trade-off and drops the outdated Vitest ignores (also from `osv-scanner.toml`). `SECURITY.md` lists the built-in library publisher key as an open maintainer step.
- **Metric names start with `sm_`** ([docs/METRICS.md](docs/METRICS.md#renamed-in-0110)): `steward_mcp_*` are now `sm_mcp_*`, `steward_monitor_status` and `steward_monitor_evaluations_total` are `sm_monitor_status` and `sm_monitor_evaluations_total`, `steward_monitor_last_ok_timestamp` is `sm_monitor_last_ok_timestamp_seconds`, and the platform-call metrics `sm_http_requests_total` and `sm_http_request_duration_seconds` are `sm_http_client_requests_total` and `sm_http_client_request_duration_seconds` (next to the new server metrics). **Check before updating:** change dashboards and alert rules to the new names during 0.11; the old names are still served, see _Deprecated_.
- **PostgreSQL updates make a `pg_dump` first** (see _Added_). **Check before updating:** the dump needs a `pg_dump` of the server's version or newer and free space of about the size of the database in `<data folder>/backups` (or `SM_UPGRADE_BACKUP_DIR`). The container image has PostgreSQL 17's: with PostgreSQL 18 or later, set `SM_PG_DUMP` to a matching program, or make your own backup and set `SM_UPGRADE_BACKUPS=0` – otherwise the update stops before it migrates. A large database makes the first start after an update take longer; give a Kubernetes `startupProbe` time for it.
- **The SQLite backup snapshot is made in a worker thread**, on a connection of its own: requests and health probes no longer wait for the nightly backup.
- **Source maps are off in the container image**: they cost about 80 MB of memory (measured: 340 MB instead of 262 MB after start-up). `NODE_OPTIONS=--enable-source-maps` turns them on again, so stack traces in the log point at the TypeScript sources.
- **`SM_SENTRY_DSN` counts as a secret** (it can come from `SM_SENTRY_DSN_FILE`).
- **Processes, Platforms and Dashboards are sortable tables** with filters in the address (`?q=`, `?tag=`, `?last=`, `?scheduled=` on Processes; `?kind=`, `?env=`, `?test=` on Platforms; `?q=` on Dashboards) and cards on phones. The process table shows the last run, the next scheduled run, the owner and the version; the 54 demo processes no longer make a page of 4,400 px. The card view stays available and is remembered per user, like the table/card choice of every list (the earlier browser-wide choice is still read). **Check before updating:** UI tests or scripts that look for the process, platform or dashboard cards find a table on wide screens.
- **"New process" asks first.** A dialog asks for the name, the id (derived from the name, editable), a description, the start (blank, manual procedure or a template) and the owning teams. Nothing is saved before _Create and edit_; an id that exists is refused (`409 PROCESS_ID_TAKEN`). _More → New manual procedure_ opens the same dialog. `POST /api/processes` takes an optional `id`. **Check before updating:** UI tests or scripts that relied on one click creating a process named "New process" must fill in the dialog.
- **A wider process canvas.** The steps panel folds to a thin strip, and the properties panel stays folded while no step is selected (it opens with a selection). Both choices are remembered per user. At 1440 × 900 the canvas grows from about 580 px to over 1,000 px.
- **Forms say what is missing instead of disabling the button.** The first-run setup, _New process_, the link dialog, the API specification import, status page, report schedule, access change entry, evidence pack, CIA scale, budget, team, API token, quick platform, monitor and record editor forms keep their button enabled; on submit the fields show what is wrong and the first one gets the focus. The ticket source, credential reminder and environment settings stop with the field's message.
- **Docs:** [docs/I18N.md](docs/I18N.md#what-stays-english-and-why) explains why the demo data and the titles of the built-in control catalogues stay English.
- **A monitor's own maintenance windows became catalogue maintenance windows** ([docs/MONITORS.md](docs/MONITORS.md#concepts), [ADR 0018](docs/adr/0018-one-maintenance-window-concept.md)). The update copies each existing monitor window (table `monitor_maintenance`) into a maintenance window that lists the monitor, once the vault is open – one audit entry `maintenance.migrated` per copy; the old row keeps a reference (`migrated_to`). The copies keep their times and reason, repeat never and announce nothing, as before. The monitor engine, notifications, objectives and status pages now read only the catalogue windows. **Check before updating:** monitor windows now show on _Operate → Maintenance_ to everyone who may see the monitor, and they are no longer removed by the monitor-history retention (cancel them, or they go with the monitor). A window that lists other items as well can no longer be ended through the monitor API (`409 MAINTENANCE_SHARED`); change it under Maintenance.
- **Availability objectives in the API** (`GET /api/objectives`, `/api/v1/objectives`) have `sourceKind`, `sourceVisible`, `monitorId` and `monitorName`; `platformVisible` stays and means the same as `sourceVisible`. A new no-data reason `monitorOff` says the source monitor is disabled or was deleted.
- **Docs: Steward's monitors next to Gatus and Prometheus** ([docs/MONITORS.md](docs/MONITORS.md#steward-monitors-gatus-and-prometheus), [docs/GATUS.md](docs/GATUS.md), [docs/ROADMAP.md](docs/ROADMAP.md#non-goals)). They no longer say Steward does not re-implement monitoring: its monitors cover the checks tied to procedures and services; Gatus and Prometheus remain good choices for broad infrastructure monitoring and can feed objectives.
- **Report schedules: the section's own SLA target applies only where no objective gives one** once _Use the objective_ is on. **Check before updating:** schedules saved before 0.11 keep the single target until you turn _Use the objective_ on; then monitors with an objective are judged by its target, so a report can show _missed_ where it showed _met_ before (or the other way round).
- **The OT network diagram in bands by level** ([docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#ot-network-diagram)): zones stand in horizontal bands by their level in the Purdue model – cells at the bottom, supervisory, operations, the DMZ, the enterprise network and external parties above – so data paths run upwards; a conduit is a gate on the boundary between its two zones, not a node in the flow. Zones get an optional **level** (`field`, `control`, `supervisory`, `operations`, `dmz`, `enterprise`, `external`) in the zone form, the YAML (`level`) and the API; without one the diagram derives it from the name, a DMZ VLAN, the assets and the conduits (nothing is stored). Parts are small chips with their details when zoomed in; the labels of the ways show on pointing at them, a part or a path. The picture opens fitted to the screen, with full screen and a mini map; a search finds and centres a part or data path; filters by zone, protocol, live status, paths across zones and paths without a documented conduit; the data path panel folds (kept per user) and the legend folds into a button. At 1440 px the picture uses 78 % of the width instead of 54 %, and on a site with 40 assets every part is in view at once instead of 5 of 67. **Check before updating:** a click on a part now selects it (and shows _Open page_); a double click or Enter opens its page as a click did before.

### Deprecated

- **The old metric names** `steward_mcp_requests_total`, `steward_mcp_tool_calls_total`, `steward_mcp_gateway_calls_total`, `steward_monitor_status`, `steward_monitor_last_ok_timestamp`, `steward_monitor_evaluations_total`, `sm_http_requests_total` and `sm_http_request_duration_seconds` are served in 0.11 as copies of the new metrics (help text _Deprecated: use …_) and removed in 0.12.
- **The monitor API's maintenance routes** `GET /api/monitors/{id}/maintenance`, `POST /api/monitors/{id}/maintenance` and `DELETE /api/monitors/{id}/maintenance/{windowId}` ([docs/MONITORS.md](docs/MONITORS.md#concepts)). They still work in 0.11 and read and write catalogue maintenance windows that list the monitor (ids now start with `mtw_`; the old `mw_` id of a copied window still ends it). They will be removed in a later release; use `/api/maintenance-windows` with a target `{ "kind": "monitor", "id": "…" }`. **Check before updating:** scripts that call them keep working, but the window ids they get back change.
- **The separate SLA target of the report section _Availability of monitors_** (`slaTarget` of the `availability` section). Set targets as availability objectives with the monitor as source and turn on _Use the objective_ (`slaFromObjectives`). The field will be removed in a later release. **Check before updating:** nothing changes for existing schedules in 0.11.

### Fixed

- **Docs that no longer matched the code:** the module map in [ARCHITECTURE.md](docs/ARCHITECTURE.md) names every part of `server/src`, and [NOTIFICATIONS.md](docs/NOTIFICATIONS.md) lists all its sections and every event with its key (the AI budget, AI requests, data contract tests, undocumented access, failed backups and emergency access were missing). A test keeps both, the docs index, the ADR index and every link in the docs and from the code to the docs complete.
- **Phone Home: the _Check findings_ header** no longer squeezes its title into a narrow column; the counts wrap below the title.
- **OT network diagram: the label of a busy way no longer grows across the picture.** A way that many data paths share got every path's transfer appended to its label; it names the first two now.
- **Importing a freshness check that reads an API** (`source: platform`) from a monitor file or Git sync only offered Insights Hub platforms; it now offers every platform, as the monitor editor does.
- **The _Get started_ panel of a database platform** now lists the schema change check and its example monitors.
- The map's site pins and clusters no longer lag behind when the map moves.

### Security

- **MCP OAuth refresh tokens: reuse of any used token revokes the grant** (RFC 9700 §4.14.2, ASVS 5.0 V10.4.5/V10.4.8). Only the token one rotation old was recognised: a thief who refreshed twice kept a working grant after the real client came back with an older token, and the sliding lifetime had no end. Steward now keeps the hashes of every refresh token a grant used (new table `oauth_used_refresh`); presenting any of them again ends the grant for both holders and is audited (`mcp.oauth_refresh_reused`). Grants also end after `grantMaxDays` (default 90).
- **`X-Forwarded-Host` is checked against the host allow-list** ([docs/MCP.md](docs/MCP.md#sign-in-with-oauth-optional)). Behind a trusted proxy (`SM_TRUST_PROXY`) without `SM_PUBLIC_URL`, the OAuth issuer and the single sign-on redirect address are built from the forwarded host, but only the `Host` header was checked: a proxy that passed a client's `X-Forwarded-Host` through made it the issuer of discovery documents that could be cached for 5 minutes. Both headers must now be allowed, the documents are cached only when they come from `SM_PUBLIC_URL`, and start-up warns when `SM_TRUST_PROXY` is set without `SM_PUBLIC_URL`.
- **The Teams-bot listener uses the main server's TLS settings**: TLS 1.2 or newer, forward-secret AEAD suites only, `SM_TLS_CIPHERS`. It accepted Node's default list (CBC suites, and RSA key exchange without forward secrecy with an RSA key). So does the small server that answers health probes while Steward waits for the database.
- **No text from a link on the sign-in page.** `?sso_error=` was shown as it was, then removed from the address bar, so a link – or an identity provider description sent with a stolen sign-in state – could put its own words (e.g. a fake service desk number) on Steward's real sign-in page. Only known codes are shown now, as translated texts; anything else shows a generic text.
- **Monitor checks and outputs only use a platform of the kind they are made for** ([docs/MONITORS.md](docs/MONITORS.md#check-types), [outputs](docs/MONITORS.md#outputs)). Through the API, an import or Git sync, a Gatus, CheckMk, Prometheus, Kubernetes, database schema or Insights Hub freshness check – every check type tied to platform kinds – and a Gatus or Zabbix output – could be pointed at a platform of any kind; the request still went only to that platform's own address with its own credentials. Saving now refuses another kind with `400 CHECK_PLATFORM_KIND` (create, update, duplicate, template instances, import, Git sync; it replaces `CHECK_GIT_PLATFORM` of the repository check), and an output with `400 OUTPUT_PLATFORM_KIND`. The import and Git sync dialogs offer only platforms of a fitting kind and refuse another choice before saving (`PLATFORM_CHOICE_KIND`). A stored check whose platform is of another kind reports _unknown_ with the reason and sends no request; a stored output sends nothing, reads no credential and records the failure; the Insights Hub access source checks its platform's kind when it runs, as the other sources do. A freshness check with `source: platform` still reads any platform. **Check before updating:** such checks show _unknown_ – "… is a platform of kind …, but this check reads …" – on the monitor page; such outputs log `monitor output failed` at each attempt. At start the server logs one warning (`monitor checks and outputs whose platform is of a kind they do not read or write to`) that lists them by monitor id, key, check or output id and platform id. Choose a platform of the right kind; until then, saving that monitor or duplicating it is refused.
- **Browser steps no longer store a secret in their step input.** The list of done actions a browser step keeps as its input (`run_steps.input`: `goto` addresses with the rendered value, selectors) was stored without the run's redaction, so a `$secret()` rendered into a `goto` address was readable in the run's step details. It is now redacted like the input of every other step. Runs from before keep what they stored.

## [0.10.1] - 2026-11-08

Steward 0.10.1 fixes what a whole-application review of 0.10.0 found. A single sign-on is now bound to the browser that started it (login CSRF), and secrets that would outlive an API token – webhook trigger secrets, push tokens, monitor gateways, heartbeat secrets, status page links – and the MCP server switch need a signed-in session. SQLite gets the single-instance guard PostgreSQL already had, and a new readiness probe for routing, `/api/ready/traffic`, keeps a locked or new instance reachable behind Kubernetes and load balancers. The web app no longer crashes for browser locales such as `en-US@posix`. See _Check before updating_ under Changed.

### Added

- **Readiness probe for routing**: `GET /api/ready/traffic` answers `200` whenever Steward serves the web UI and the API, also while the vault is locked and before the first set-up. It answers `503` only while Steward shuts down or waits for another instance to release the database. Use it as the Kubernetes `readinessProbe` and for load balancer checks; `/api/ready` stays as it is, for alerts ([docs/INSTALL.md](docs/INSTALL.md#health-and-readiness)). _System status_ lists it with the other health-check addresses.

### Changed

- **Creating or rotating a secret and switching the MCP server need a signed-in session** ([docs/API.md](docs/API.md#security-notes), [docs/SECURITY-HARDENING.md](docs/SECURITY-HARDENING.md)). API tokens of any scope now get `403 SESSION_REQUIRED` (problem details, like the other session-only routes) on `POST /api/triggers`, `POST /api/triggers/:id/rotate`, the cost and schema push tokens (`POST /api/costs/platform/:id/push-tokens[/:tokenId/rotate]`, `POST /api/db-schemas/:kind/:id/push-tokens[/:tokenId/rotate]`), `POST /api/monitors/:id/checks/:checkId/rotate`, `POST /api/monitor-gateways`, `POST /api/monitor-gateways/:id/rotate`, `POST /api/status-pages/:pageId/link` and `PUT /api/mcp-server`. A monitor saved with an API token gets no heartbeat secrets in its answer (`secrets: []`); the checks are created as before. Changing, pausing, deleting and revoking stay open to tokens. **Check before updating:** a script or CI job that creates webhook triggers, push tokens, monitor gateways or status page links with an API token, rotates their secrets, reads heartbeat secrets from a monitor save or turns the MCP server on or off now fails with "API tokens cannot do this – sign in to the web UI". Do these steps signed in to the web app (they are one-off set-up steps) and keep only the resulting secret in the CI job.
- **Kubernetes rollouts**: the installation guide now recommends `strategy: Recreate` and `replicas: 1` (SQLite and PostgreSQL). With `RollingUpdate`, the new pod waits for the old one and never becomes ready, so the rollout would hang. It also warns against network file systems (NFS, SMB, many `ReadWriteMany` volumes) for SQLite.
- **Docs.** The README no longer calls the MCP server read-only: since 0.2.10 an assistant can ask for changes that a person approves. Its node type table now lists all step types. `NOTICE` names the bundled endoflife.date data (MIT), with its licence text next to the files. `docs/INSTALL.md` and `docs/SECURITY-HARDENING.md` show image tags as `<version>` instead of 0.2.1, and INSTALL.md says that backups of PostgreSQL 18 or later need a matching `pg_dump` (`SM_PG_DUMP`).

### Fixed

- **docs/PRIVACY.md described an account deletion and a profile export that do not exist.** It now says what Steward does today: an administrator deactivates a leaver, which ends their sessions, tokens and what runs in their name; the user name, roles and team memberships stay because the audit chain names people by their user name; display name, names and e-mail can be replaced by hand. Pseudonymising a person's data and a per-person export are planned for 0.11.
- **Kubernetes readiness probe**: the installation guide recommended `/api/ready` as the `readinessProbe`. It answers `503` while the vault is locked or Steward is not set up yet, so Kubernetes sent nobody to the pod and nobody could unlock or set up Steward through its address. **Check before updating:** change the `readinessProbe` to `/api/ready/traffic`. The guide's probes now also send `Host: localhost`: Kubernetes sends the pod's IP address as the host name, which Steward refuses with `403 Host not allowed` unless it is in `SM_ALLOWED_HOSTS`.
- **Only one instance per SQLite database** ([ADR 0002, update 0.10.1](docs/adr/0002-single-active-instance-with-lease-lock.md), [docs/INSTALL.md](docs/INSTALL.md#one-instance-per-data-folder-sqlite)): Steward holds an exclusive file lock on `service-manager.db.lock` before it opens the database. A second instance on the same data folder (a Kubernetes rolling update, a second container on the same volume) no longer runs every schedule a second time and races on the audit chain: it does not open the database, waits (`/api/health` 200, `/api/ready` and `/api/ready/traffic` 503, the log line `waiting for the database lock`) and takes over when the first one stops. A crash or `kill -9` leaves no stale lock. `admin`, `migrate` and `db:copy` hold the lock while they work and refuse while Steward runs, also in another container; the desktop launcher opens the running Steward instead of starting a second one. The lock files are not part of backups. **Check before updating:** on Kubernetes with SQLite, set `strategy: Recreate` in the Deployment. With `RollingUpdate`, the new pod now waits for the old one, never becomes ready, and the rollout hangs. (The update to 0.10.1 itself still starts beside the old pod, because 0.10.0 does not take the lock.)
- **Browser locales that are not BCP 47 tags no longer break the UI.** A browser that reports `en-US@posix` (Chromium on Linux with `LANG` unset or `C`/`POSIX`, common on kiosks and wall boards) made every page show an error, the sign-in page included. Such locales are now mapped (`en-US@posix` → `en-US`, `de_AT.UTF-8` → `de-AT`) or skipped, and numbers and dates fall back to the UI language.
- **"Load more" on the runs list no longer skips runs.** Runs that started in the same millisecond as the last run of a page (for-each children, a burst of webhook triggers) were left out. The list now pages by start time and id, like the public API. The old `before=<start time>` still works.
- **Contrast and target size.** In dark mode, filled status badges in green, teal, cyan, lime and yellow (e.g. the _Up_ and _Degraded_ monitor badges) now reach 4.5:1 with their white text. The application name link in the applications list is at least 24 px high.

### Security

- **A single sign-on is bound to the browser that started it** (login CSRF, RFC 9700 §4.7.1, [docs/SSO.md](docs/SSO.md)). Until now the `state` and the PKCE verifier lived only on the server, so someone with an account at the identity provider could start a sign-in, stop before the redirect back and send the callback link to another person: whoever opened it was signed in to the sender's account and could type their data into it. The start now sets a short-lived cookie (`__Host-sm_sso_…`, `__Secure-sm_sso_…` under `SM_BASE_PATH`; HttpOnly, SameSite=Lax, 10 minutes) whose hash is kept with the sign-in. The callback refuses a sign-in without the matching cookie with the same message as an unknown state, audits it (`auth.login_failed`, reason `SSO_BROWSER_MISMATCH`) and clears the cookie. The step-up and the administrator's test sign-in through single sign-on are bound the same way (`auth.step_up_failed`, `sso.test_sign_in`). Each sign-in in progress has its own cookie, so sign-ins in several tabs still work.
- **A leaked API token could leave credentials behind that outlived its revocation** ([docs/API.md](docs/API.md#security-notes)). A full-access token could create a webhook trigger, a cost or schema push token, a monitor gateway or heartbeat secret, or a status page link without sign-in, and could switch the MCP server on with AI run requests and control proposals. Revoking the token – the documented answer to a leak – did not end that access: the trigger's secret still started the owner's processes, a push token still rewrote cost data. These routes are now for signed-in sessions only (see _Changed_), as rotating a Git sync source's webhook secret and the MCP OAuth settings already were. The token runbook in docs/API.md now says to check what a leaked token set up.

## [0.10.0] - 2026-11-07

Steward 0.10.0 puts the process engine behind **one interface** and builds **debugging** on it, adds **quiet hours and digests** for notifications, **approvals from Microsoft Teams**, **shared procedure libraries** and a **map of sites**. Every start, decision and cancel of a run now goes through one rights check; a single node can be run with sample data, a run stepped through and two runs compared, without debug runs ever counting anywhere. Messages wait for the end of quiet hours instead of being dropped; Teams cards open Steward's decision dialog, and a preview bot decides in the card where the assurance policy allows it. Libraries are signed releases installed after a full review; the plant registry's map fetches its tiles only through Steward and stays off until an administrator turns it on. Three security reviews of the new features found and fixed, among others, a debug path around the AI allowed-data rule, Teams decisions on stale rights, a Hold that a designer could lift and a map check that could send the stored key elsewhere; see _Check before updating_ under Changed.

### Added

- **Quiet hours and digests (#236, [ADR 0012](docs/adr/0012-quiet-hours-and-digests.md), [docs/NOTIFICATIONS.md](docs/NOTIFICATIONS.md#quiet-hours-and-digests))**: e-mails and Teams posts wait at night and arrive together – nothing is dropped, the app gets everything at once.
  - **Quiet hours** per person (_Profile → Quiet hours and digests_), with a team default, and per channel: the instance Teams channel, the instance webhook in Teams format, team channels in Teams format and monitor outputs in Teams format. IANA time zones, times across midnight and the weekend, daylight-saving nights handled on the wall clock (tested for Europe/Berlin, America/New_York and Australia/Lord_Howe). A week grid shows the result; the bell says _Quiet hours until 07:30 · 4 messages held_.
  - **One catch-up message** when they end: decisions waiting, still failing, failed and passed again, other – each item once with its start and latest state; decisions taken meanwhile are left out and counted. When the daily summary is due within 60 minutes, it becomes its first section, _While you were away_.
  - **Break-throughs**: emergency access always; a decision that would expire inside the quiet hours (AI requests) when no other decider is reachable; sources marked **Urgent – interrupts quiet hours** in their notification policy (schedules, webhook triggers, monitor outputs), unless a person or channel turned that off. The footer says why.
  - **Digests** for e-mail per event group (decisions, runs and checks, monitors, reminders, reports, costs): hourly, daily or weekly; decisions at most hourly.
  - **JSON webhooks are never held**; there is no escalation and no on-call rota – an on-call tool pages from a JSON webhook.
  - Rights are checked again at release for every kind of item; a waiting decision is never folded into the count of the 500-item limit; the members' default quiet hours of a team need a step-up like channel quiet hours. Held messages are the rendered text in `notification_outbox` (never URLs or secrets), at most 500 open items per target, deleted 7 days after release. Administrators switch quiet hours off for everyone and see who uses them (_Settings → Notifications_). Audited: `notification.quiet_hours_changed`, `notification.digests_changed`, `notification.quiet_hours_switch`. API: `/api/me/notification-prefs`, `/api/settings/quiet-hours`.
  - **Daily summary once a week**: _Days → Once a week_ on a chosen day.
- **Decisions from Microsoft Teams (#237, [ADR 0013](docs/adr/0013-teams-approvals.md), [docs/TEAMS-APPROVALS.md](docs/TEAMS-APPROVALS.md))**:
  - **Cards that lead to the decision**: process, step, platform, the change as `METHOD path_template`, who asked, since when and the expiry – never bodies, inputs or values. **Approve** / **Reject** open the run or AI request with the decision dialog pre-selected (`?decide=approve|reject`); nothing is decided until the person confirms. A **follow-up card** with the outcome goes to the channels the card went to (`approval_posts`). E-mails carry the same facts and links.
  - **Approve inside the card (preview, off by default)**: a Teams bot on its own listener (`SM_TEAMS_BOT_PORT`) that serves only `POST /teams/bot/messages`, with full Bot Framework token validation (signature, endorsement, issuer, audience, expiry, `serviceUrl` on a Teams host), allowed tenants, replay protection and rate limits. Only accounts linked by Entra `tid` + `oid` decide; decisions run the same code as in Steward and read _via Microsoft Teams_ in audit entries, run protocols and reports. Anything with a step-up, select decisions, manual steps, verifications and proposals open Steward. Card data carries a version and an HMAC. _Settings → Notifications → Teams bot_ (with a downloadable Teams app package), audited as `teams.bot_settings_changed`, `teams.conversation_added`/`_removed` and `teams.action_refused`.
  - **Assurance policy**: a new switch per control point, **May be decided in Microsoft Teams** – on by default for `run.decide`, off for `mcp.write` (the full request is shown in Steward), impossible where a step-up applies; and a new point **Decide with the Teams bot** (`teams.bot`): switching the bot on asks a second administrator ([docs/ASSURANCE.md](docs/ASSURANCE.md)).
  - **Safeguards**: a Teams decision needs a Steward sign-in within the last 7 days (a setting, 1–30); personal chats show the step's instructions and items or the concrete AI request, channel cards stay minimal; bot calls follow no redirects; card data expires after 30 days; refreshes check the one item and its visibility.
- **Map of sites (plant registry, #234, [ADR 0015](docs/adr/0015-map-of-sites.md), [docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#map))**: _Plant → Map_ shows the sites the reader may see as a list and on a map side by side (phones start with the list), with their state.
  - **Off until an administrator turns it on** (_Settings → Modules → Map of sites_): _OpenStreetMap_ is preselected – one click, with what each choice sends where; _Light and dark_ (vector tiles of OpenFreeMap in Steward's own styles that follow the theme), an **own tile source** (a raster URL template or a MapLibre style URL, with an optional API key kept in the vault) or a **self-hosted PMTiles file** in `<data>/maps/` for networks without internet access. Changing where tiles come from – also the own source while another one is chosen, and checking a new style address – needs a step-up (new control point `plant.mapSource`, on by default) and is audited (`plant.map_settings_changed`, `plant.map_source_checked`, with the old and new origins, never the key); turning the map off keeps the stored own source. A stored API key is only ever sent to its own source's origins; a new address on another origin needs it entered again.
  - **Tiles only through Steward**: the browser loads style, tiles, glyphs and sprites from `/api/plant/map/…`; Steward's server fetches them from the source's origins only (GET, address safety, no redirects elsewhere, content type and size checked, 10 s), with its own `User-Agent`, caches them in `<data>/cache/map/` (`SM_MAP_CACHE_MB`, default 256; per user above zoom 12) and limits each user to 600 tiles a minute. Browser sessions only; the content security policy is unchanged. A failing source tells readers only that it failed; administrators see the address and reason. The OpenStreetMap tile usage policy is met and its attribution is always on the map.
  - **Pins and clusters are buttons** in list order with an accessible name that says the state (worst of the linked monitors and failing plant checks; _Not watched_ when nothing watches a site), a card with assets by status, KPI coverage, open checks and links into the site; **Show on the map** on the site page; a **position picker** in the site form; the dashboard widget **Sites map**. Without WebGL 2, or when the source fails, the list stays with the reason. Light and dark, keyboard, axe clean, `prefers-reduced-motion` respected.
  - **Rights**: who sees a site sees its position, as before; the sites answer is filtered by the site rights (a hidden site gives no pin and no count). The Plant viewer role has the map too.
  - **Libraries**: MapLibre GL JS 6.15.0 (BSD-3-Clause, its own chunk, loaded only with a map) and pmtiles 4.5.0 (BSD-3-Clause, server); the Protomaps-schema styles are generated with @protomaps/basemaps 5.7.2 (development only, `npm run map:styles`). Backups and migration packages leave `<data>/cache/` and `<data>/maps/` out. The demo has a made-up tile source and a small PMTiles file, so it fetches nothing real.
- **Shared procedure libraries (#238, [ADR 0014](docs/adr/0014-shared-procedure-libraries.md), [docs/LIBRARIES.md](docs/LIBRARIES.md))**: share tested processes, dashboards, monitors, record types and connector types between installations as a signed, versioned **library**, reviewed as a whole and installed in one transaction under _Automate → Libraries_.
  - **Signed releases**: `steward library build` writes `release.json` (every file with its SHA-256); the publisher signs it with `ssh-keygen -Y sign -n steward-library` (ed25519 or a hardware key, sk-ed25519 with a touch). Steward verifies the SSHSIG signature itself with Node's crypto – no new dependency – and refuses extra, missing or changed files, downgrades, replays and releases of another publisher. CLI: `npm run library -- build|sign|verify|pack|schema`, in the desktop app `steward library …`.
  - **Trusted publishers** (_Settings → Integrations → Libraries_): administrators pin publishers' keys (shown as `SHA256:…`, sealed with the vault's integrity key), limit them to library ids, set expiries, retire and revoke keys. Trust changes need a step-up (new control point `library.publishers`). This version has **no built-in publisher key**.
  - **Distribution**: a Git sync source in the new **library mode** follows version tags (`v{version}`, pre-releases on request; Forgejo/Gitea, GitHub, GitLab), or a `.zip` is uploaded. A verified release waits for review; nothing installs by itself.
  - **Review and install**: every item as new, changed (with the diff), unchanged, no longer in the library or a conflict (changed locally, exists but not from this library – never overwritten silently); **what each process can do**, computed from its steps (writes counted by HTTP method, steps marked as not changing anything – which also run in dry runs – shown separately); every monitor output (webhooks, pushes, processes it starts); references mapped to local platforms and processes; connector types with their base URL, token URL, trusted origins and HTTP settings in full (administrators only). Monitors arrive switched off; a library's sub-processes are pinned to the versions the install created. **Rollback** from Steward's stored copy, **uninstall** detaches the items, four-eyes on install through the new control point `library.install` (off by default): the approver sees exactly the requester's decisions and approves exactly those, with their own rights.
  - **Revoked keys and Hold**: releases of a revoked key are flagged on the library, its items, runs and run reports, and their updates are refused; one action **Hold** pauses the schedules, webhook triggers and monitors of the library and refuses its runs until an administrator lifts it – a held library cannot be uninstalled and what it paused cannot be switched on again by hand. After a key's expiry date no release signed with it is installed or updated (a rollback to the release installed before stays possible).
  - **Provenance**: _From library_ badges on processes, monitors, dashboards and record types; the library release, key fingerprint and release SHA-256 in the version history, run details, run protocols (and so in evidence packs) and the audit log.
  - **Example**: `examples/libraries/example-access-reviews`, signed with a test key that is never trusted by default; `npm run validate` checks libraries.
  - **Update**: migrations `libraries-001-publishers-releases` (new tables) and `gitsync-004-library-mode` (new columns `sync_sources.mode`, `allow_prereleases`, `newest_seen`, `sync_items.release_id`, `process_versions.origin`) – additive. New internal routes below `/api/libraries`, `/api/library-releases`, `/api/library-publishers`, `/api/library-keys`, `/api/library-origins`; `/api/v1` is unchanged.
- **Debugging procedures: run one node, step through a run, compare two runs** (#235, [ADR 0010](docs/adr/0010-debugging-procedures.md), [docs/DEBUGGING.md](docs/DEBUGGING.md)): find a fault without blind edits and full runs.
  - **Run this node** (a tab beside a node's properties in the editor): one node with the variables just before it in an earlier run, with sample data (JSON, up to 1 MB) or with run inputs; **offline** (nothing contacted; a request needs a pasted answer), with **recorded** answers (the default for a node that calls a platform) or as a **live read** (only `GET`/`HEAD`, database queries, AI calls and browser steps without clicks – a step's `mutating: false` does not make a `POST` live). Sample data that looks like a credential is refused (`DEBUG_SAMPLE_SECRET`). The editor's unsaved node may run: it is checked like a saved definition and the debug run's definition hash covers it. A decision or manual step is answered in the form, marked _Simulated_. Variables copied from a run keep that run's data sources, so an AI gateway's _allowed data_ rule applies to them as in the run (typed or pasted sample data counts as run input). At most two single-node runs per person run at the same time (`DEBUG_NODE_BUSY`); they do not wait for a slot of `SM_MAX_CONCURRENT_RUNS`.
  - **Step through a run** (_Debug_ in the editor): a debug run of the saved version (test run or dry run, never a live run) that pauses before each node or at breakpoints, shows the variables and the node's resolved inputs (redacted), and is driven with _Continue_, _Step_, _Skip this node_ and _Stop_. Decisions and manual steps are answered in the panel, marked _Simulated_; from the first simulated or skipped decision on – also one inside a sub-process – the whole run and its sub-process runs answer from recordings only. Each _Continue_, _Step_, _Skip_ or answer checks the person's rights again (`run` on the process, `use` on the paused node's platforms); _Stop_ always works. One step-through run per person; a pause times out after `debugPauseMinutes` (default 30); a restart cancels open debug runs. On a phone the panel is a bottom sheet.
  - **Compare two runs** (`/runs/compare`, _Compare with the last good run_ on a failed run, _Compare with another run_ in a run's protocol menu): the path, decisions, checks, requests, errors, durations and the variables before each step side by side, from the stored, redacted protocol only; masked values stay masked, secret inputs are not compared. A comparison writes no audit entry.
  - **Debug runs** are runs like any other – journal, protocol, seal, audit – but never publish datasets, raise check alerts, document access changes, create tasks or notify, and run metrics, the daily summary, reports, evidence packs, compliance controls, duties, scorecards and objectives leave them out. Only their starter, the process's managers, administrators and auditors see them – also node inspection, the expression assistant, run reports and record links; run lists show them with the _Debug runs_ filter; `/api/v1` and MCP never serve them. AI calls that are really sent count against the budgets and are marked in the usage log. Finished debug runs are deleted after `retentionDebugRunDays` (default 7, _Settings → Retention_).
  - **Rights**: `edit` and `run` on the process and `use` on the platforms; an offline single node needs only `edit`; comparing needs `view` on both runs.
  - **API and migration**: `POST /api/processes/{id}/debug/node`, `POST /api/processes/{id}/debug/runs`, `GET`/`POST /api/runs/{id}/debug`, `GET /api/runs/compare?a=&b=`, `GET /api/runs/{id}/last-good`, `GET /api/runs?debug=1`; settings `retentionDebugRunDays` and `debugPauseMinutes`. Migration `engine-004-debug-runs` (additive: column `runs.debug` and a partial index).
- **The engine boundary: start, signal, cancel and query runs through one interface** (#239, [ADR 0011](docs/adr/0011-engine-boundary.md), [docs/ARCHITECTURE.md](docs/ARCHITECTURE.md#the-engine-boundary)): today's runner is the built-in engine behind `engine/engine.ts`; every caller that starts, decides, resumes or cancels runs goes through one command layer (`engine/commands.ts`), so the rights checks of the routes, the scheduler, webhook triggers, monitor outputs, MCP run requests, the dashboard refresh, template bundles, the demo setup and sub-process steps cannot drift apart. `run.started` and `run.finished` events carry the run's mode and whether it is a debug run. Contract tests check the engine's invariants, with golden protocol hashes of example processes on SQLite and PostgreSQL. Nothing changes for users except the reason in `run.cancelled` (below).

### Changed

- **`run.cancelled` says who and why**: the summary names who cancelled the run (_Run of "…" cancelled by olivia_), followed by the reason when one was given (_– reason: …_, one line of plain text, at most 500 characters), and `details.reason` and `details.cancelledBy` hold both – for example _Parent run … was cancelled_, _Stopped while debugging_, _debug pause timed out_ or _Steward restarted_ (cancelled by _system_). `POST /api/runs/{id}/cancel` takes an optional `reason`.
- **One rights check for every way a run starts** (ADR 0011). **Check before updating:** two callers check more than before – **monitor outputs** that start a run now also need `use` on every platform the output binds, for the monitor's owner (before: only `run` on the process); a refused start is reported like any other failed output. **Sub-process steps** now also need `use` on the platforms the child run is bound to, for the run's actor (before: only `run` on the child process) – it matters only when that right was taken away while the parent run was waiting. The demo setup checks the administrator's rights like any start. A schedule whose bound platform was deleted is now skipped (_may not use platform_) instead of failing.

### Fixed

- **Decision steps are decided exactly once**: `store.decideTask` writes only while the step is open; a second decision (from another session or a Teams card) gets 409 and changes nothing.

## [0.9.0] - 2026-11-07

Steward 0.9.0 adds **data contracts**, **platform costs**, **Kafka Connect**, a **version watch** of the tech stack and **changedetection.io**, checks the outbound policy on **every redirect**, and lays the groundwork for several **identities per platform**. Data contracts in ODCS v3 are tested through the data contract service and checked by Steward itself, with breaking changes tied to change records. Costs from AWS, Snowflake, Confluent Cloud, the AI gateways and FOCUS files show what services, applications and teams cost, with budgets. Kafka Connect clusters, the newest versions of tech stack entries and changed vendor pages are watched read-only. Three security reviews of the new features found and fixed, among others, credentials that could be sent through another platform's connection, read-only rules that another platform could get around, and rights of the reader that costs and the version watch did not respect throughout; see _Check before updating_ under Changed and Security.

### Added

- **Platform identities, step 1: one default identity per platform (#228, [ADR 0009](docs/adr/0009-platform-identities.md), [docs/PLATFORM-SIGN-IN.md](docs/PLATFORM-SIGN-IN.md#identities))**: the groundwork for several sets of credentials under one platform. Nothing changes for users yet.
  - **Default identity**: every platform has exactly one, holding its credentials and its MCP mode; it signs in with the platform's settings and is open to everybody with the _use_ right, as before. Saving credentials or the MCP gateway writes it. A new **Identities** tab on the platform page shows it read-only.
  - **Update**: migration `platforms-005-identities` (additive: a new table, `platforms.secrets` stays and is emptied) creates the default identities; at the first unlock each intact platform's credentials and MCP mode move to its sealed default identity (audited as `platform.identities_created`). A platform whose settings were changed outside Steward stays refused until an administrator confirms them, as before.
  - **Seals and caches**: identities are sealed like platforms and bound to their platform; key rotation, backups and migration packages cover their credentials; tokens, assumed AWS roles and database connection pools are kept per identity.
  - **API**: `GET /api/v1/platforms` and `/platforms/{id}` list the identities with names and flags only – never credentials, sign-in settings or variables.
- **Data contracts (ODCS v3, #221, [docs/DATA-CONTRACTS.md](docs/DATA-CONTRACTS.md))**: what a data source promises – schema, quality rules, owner, servers – as an [Open Data Contract Standard](https://bitol-io.github.io/open-data-contract-standard/) v3 contract, under _Catalogue → Data contracts_.
  - **Contracts with versions**: every saved YAML is kept as a version; **lint on save** against the official JSON Schema of ODCS v3.0.2 (vendored with its licence in `server/assets/odcs/`), live in the editor; **Lint with the service** runs the datacontract CLI's own lint. YAML is read within the limits of the plant import (1 MB, keys, 32 levels, aliases – a YAML bomb is refused).
  - **Breaking changes**: the comparison of two versions marks removed fields and schema objects, changed or narrowed types, fields that become required or unique, added or tightened quality rules, changed physical names and servers that point elsewhere; **Copy as Markdown** for a change record. Per contract, **breaking changes can require an approved change record** – one record per version; switching the rule off takes the new contract right _manage_ (approvers and administrators).
  - **Links** to database tables (one contract can cover several tables or datasets, each linked to its schema object), datasets, applications, systems, API specifications, and the database platform that answers for each server.
  - **Drafts** from the tables of a database schema snapshot (#222), a dataset's columns, an OpenAPI response schema or a recorded platform response (#105).
  - **Platform kind _Data contract service_**: the datacontract CLI's API (`datacontract api`) as its own container. **Test now**, the monitor check and the process step send `POST /test` with the contract; the tested server's host and credentials come from its database platform in the vault and travel per request in the CLI's `datacontract-*` headers – never in the contract. The outbound policy applies to the service and to the tested database (Steward's own database is refused). The full report is stored as evidence, secrets masked.
  - **Monitor check _Data contract_** (the test, or Steward's own checks without the service), **process step _Test a data contract_**, a **scorecard part _Data contracts_** and a **report section _Data contracts_**.
  - **Steward's own checks**: no owner, not tested for N days, last test failed, columns of linked datasets and tables that no longer match the contract, lint errors.
  - **Notifications**: a failing test notifies the contract's owning team (event `contract.failed`, through the routes or the notification policy of the schedule or trigger that ran it); opening a vendor ticket stays a person's decision.
  - **Import and export** of contract YAML; examples in `examples/data-contracts/` (checked by `npm run validate`); the demo has a simulated data contract service and two contracts.
  - **API**: internal routes below `/api/data-contracts`; the `/api/v1` scorecard carries the new part `contracts`. Migrations `datacontracts-001-contracts`, `datacontracts-002-scorecard` and `datacontracts-003-history-and-rules` (additive: new tables, new columns `contracts` and `contract_ids` of `scorecard_snapshots`). Database platforms have the new setting _Allow the data contract service to connect without certificate verification_ (`tls.contractServiceUnverified`). Owning teams have the new resource kind `contract`.
- **Platform costs – showback in one cost model (FinOps FOCUS)** (#226, [docs/COSTS.md](docs/COSTS.md)): what services, applications, teams and platforms cost per month, from the platforms Steward reads – not a FinOps suite.
  - **Cost model**: cost lines with FOCUS columns (`BillingPeriodStart/End`, `ChargePeriodStart/End`, `BilledCost`, `EffectiveCost`, `BillingCurrency`, `ProviderName`, `ServiceName`, `ServiceCategory`, `ResourceId`, `SubAccountId`, `ChargeCategory`, `Tags`) and Steward's links to platform, component and application; amounts as exact decimals (whole millionths, never floats) with their currency – **no conversion**, totals per currency.
  - **Sources**: AWS Cost Explorer by the application tag and by service; Snowflake `ORGANIZATION_USAGE.USAGE_IN_CURRENCY_DAILY`, else `METERING_DAILY_HISTORY` credits × a price per credit; Confluent Cloud's Costs API `GET /billing/v1/costs`, clusters linked to their Kafka platforms (variable `confluentClusterId`); the AI gateways' usage log; **FOCUS CSV files** uploaded with a preview (size limits, SHA-256, duplicates refused, replace or add) or **pushed from CI** with a per-platform push token; a file's tag links by name only to an application the importer (for a push: the token's creator) may change or one tied to the platform, and a large file is read in chunks without holding up the server. Read once a day (AI hourly), re-reading the last 35 days; late and corrected figures of closed periods are audited as corrections (`cost.corrected`).
  - **Mapping**: by the tag (a component on the platform or an application by name), the cluster, or the platform's own application; everything else is shown per platform as **unassigned**.
  - **Costs pages** (_Catalogue → Costs_, and _Costs_ on services, applications and platforms): per service, application, team and platform the last 12 months, the current month so far, a forecast and the trend (Theil–Sen, #145), breakdowns by provider and service, a chart with its table twin; a platform's _Source_ tab sets up the reading, imports files and manages push tokens.
  - **Budgets** per service, application or team and month, with notices at 80 % and 100 % (new event _Cost budget_, `cost.budget`); raising one is audited and needs a step-up at the new assurance control point `cost.budget`.
