  - **Monitor check _Cost change_** (`type: cost`), the scorecard part **Costs**, the report section **Costs**, the built-in dataset **Costs** (`costs.overview`, administrators and auditors) and the read endpoints `GET /api/v1/costs` and `GET /api/v1/costs/{scope}/{id}`.
  - **Who sees costs**: owners and owning teams, administrators and auditors – and everyone who may see the scope unless _Hide amounts from viewers_ is on; hidden scopes are neither named nor counted. A budget covers the whole service or team, so whoever does not see all its applications sees only its state (within budget, 80 % reached, reached) – on the costs pages, the scorecard, the report section, `/api/v1` (`stateOnly`) and in the notice. The check _Cost change_ counts what its owner may see, also on its schedule, and reports percentages only while _Hide amounts from viewers_ is on.
  - Migration `costs-001-records-budgets-imports` (additive: new tables, and a column `costs` on `scorecard_snapshots`). The demo gets _Confluent Cloud (demo)_ and reads every source once.
- **changedetection.io: platform, page watches, monitor check and a review from its notifications** (#242, [docs/CHANGEDETECTION.md](docs/CHANGEDETECTION.md)): for vendor pages without an API – changelogs and release notes, terms and pricing pages, status pages, documentation. changedetection.io runs separately (its own container and browser); Steward only connects to it.
  - **Preset _changedetection.io (page watches, read-only)_** (group _Monitoring_): base URL, the API key in `x-api-key` from the vault, the variable `uiUrl` for links, an OpenAPI description of the read endpoints. **Read-only and without page contents by rule of the platform kind**: only `GET`/`HEAD` (other methods: `PLATFORM_METHOD_DENIED`, the same rule as Kafka Connect, also on redirect hops), no `recheck`/`paused`/`muted` parameters, no snapshots, diffs, `/changes`, `/notifications` or pages outside `/api/v1` – for processes, the explorer, the MCP gateway and monitors alike; credentials in a watch's settings (notification URLs, request headers, browser steps) are removed from every answer. Requests go through the outbound policy.
  - **Watches as components of the platform** (tab _Watches_): title, page, tags (by name), state, last check, last change, last error and snapshot times – metadata only, never contents –, a link to each diff in changedetection.io, search and filters by tag and state, cards on phones. _Read now_, then every hour in the background as the person who read it last. A watch links to an **application**, a **service** and a **vendor** (platform managers; audited `changedetection.watch_linked`) and then shows under _Linked from_ on the application or service for people who may see the platform. A linked watch deleted in changedetection.io stays as _Removed_.
  - **Monitor check _Page watch_** (`page-watch`): one watch, the watches of a tag or all – a change within N hours as information or degraded, a failing watch (or one not checked for N hours) degraded or down, paused ones left out or degraded. Example monitor _Vendor page watches_; `/api/v1` lists the check type.
  - **A notification starts a review**: the process _changedetection.io – Review a changed page (from a notification)_ checks the payload, looks the watch up and gives a person the task "… changed" with the diff link; the first **example trigger** (`examples/triggers/`, new file kind `webhookTrigger`, checked by `npm run validate`) fills the trigger's mapping (_Map: changedetection.io notification_) and shows the notification URL (`posts://…?+X-Steward-Token=…`) and JSON body to paste.
  - The demo has a simulated changedetection.io with six watches of invented vendor pages. Internal routes `GET /api/changedetection/:platformId/watches`, `POST …/watches/read`, `PUT …/watches/:uuid/link`, `GET …/watches/:uuid/history`, `GET /api/triggers/examples`; job `changedetection`. Migration `changedetection-001-watches` (additive: new tables only).
- **Kafka Connect and Debezium** (#223, [docs/KAFKA.md](docs/KAFKA.md#kafka-connect-and-debezium)): Steward watches the connectors and tasks of a Kafka Connect cluster, read-only.
  - **Platform kind _Kafka Connect_**: the REST API as base URL, sign-in with basic, a bearer token or a client certificate, and its Kafka platform (variable `kafkaPlatform`). Steward only sends `GET`: changing requests – create, restart, pause, resume, delete – are refused for the kind, whatever asks (`PLATFORM_METHOD_DENIED`).
  - **Connectors tab** on the platform page: connectors with type, class, plugin version, state, worker and topics; their tasks, and the error of a failed one – shortened, with secret-looking values removed; the installed plugins. Read every hour and with _Read now_; connectors that come and go are recorded in the platform's history.
  - **Links**: a connector's topics link to the Kafka topic components of applications, and the topic component lists its connectors; a Debezium source connector links to the database platform and the system it reads (by host, port and database – the rule is in the docs). Only what the reader may see is named.
  - **Configurations without secrets**: every answer of the kind has values under secret-looking keys (password, secret, token, key, credentials, `sasl.jaas.config` …) and secret-looking values (private keys, tokens, passwords in URLs) removed – in the readout, the explorer, process steps and the MCP gateway alike. A connector's configuration is read on request, never stored, and the read is audited.
  - **Monitor check _Kafka Connect connectors_** (`kafka-connect`): a connector or task not running, a task failed for N minutes, the consumer lag of a sink connector and a Debezium connector whose topics got no message for N minutes; the example monitor `examples/monitors/63-kafka-connect.yaml`.
  - **Demo**: _Kafka Connect (demo)_ with a Debezium PostgreSQL source of the demo database and a sink with a failed task; the demo Kafka cluster has the two CDC topics and the sink's consumer group.
  - Internal routes `GET /api/platforms/:id/kafka-connect`, `POST …/kafka-connect/read`, `GET …/kafka-connect/connectors/:name/config`; migration `platforms-004-kafka-connect` (additive: a new table). No new dependency.
- **Client certificates for platforms (mutual TLS)** ([docs/PLATFORM-SIGN-IN.md](docs/PLATFORM-SIGN-IN.md#client-certificate-mutual-tls)): a platform whose credentials hold `tlsCertificate` and `tlsKey` (PEM, optional `tlsKeyPassphrase`) presents that certificate to its own origins, with any authentication type.
- **Version watch of the tech stack** (#241, [docs/APPLICATIONS.md](docs/APPLICATIONS.md#version-watch)): the newest version of every tech stack entry – from SBOMs, cluster images, repository files or entered by hand –, how far behind the installed one is and whether its cycle is still supported. Read-only: Steward shows and reminds, Renovate or Dependabot change the code.
  - **Sources**, tried in a configurable order (the first that answers wins, or one chosen per entry): Dependency-Track's latest version of a package URL, the public package registries by package URL – npm, PyPI, Maven Central, NuGet, the Go module proxy, crates.io (or a mirror) –, image tags on a container registry platform (OCI distribution API with the registry's token flow; new platform types _Container registry_ and _Docker Hub_), releases or tags on GitHub, GitLab and Forgejo/Gitea, and endoflife.date.
  - **Comparison by the ecosystem's rules**: Semantic Versioning 2.0.0 (also NuGet, Go, Cargo), PEP 440, Maven's version order; pre-releases left out unless chosen; image tags keep their shape (`-alpine`), a tag pattern with `{version}` picks others.
  - **On the component, the system and the application's tech stack**: installed → newest, behind by major / minor / patch and for how many days, the end of life of the cycle and the component's open findings from Dependency-Track; the dialog of an entry shows what each source said and lets editors choose where to look. `GET /api/v1/applications/{id}` carries `watch` on every stack entry.
  - **Monitor check _Version behind_** (degraded above N minor releases or D days, down a major behind or at the end of life – per check), the dataset **Component versions** (`catalogue.versions`, administrators and auditors), the optional scorecard part **Up to date**, and the message **New major release** (`stack.release`) through the usual notification policy.
  - **Safe**: cached once a day per lookup by the job `version-watch`, at most 200 lookups per run; the outbound policy decides which registries may be reached (allow their hosts under _Settings → Outbound access_); no credentials, cookies or redirects to public registries, answers up to 16 MB; package URLs and image names are checked segment by segment. Entries of applications or systems the reader may not see answer like missing ones; a hidden system is neither named nor counted in the check _Version behind_ and the scorecard parts _Up to date_ and _Tech stack_; a platform the reader may not see is named by its kind only. The registry addresses and the chosen platforms of the settings are shown to administrators only. Every change of a setting is audited.
  - Internal routes `/api/catalogue/versions`, `/api/catalogue/versions/refresh`, `/api/catalogue/versions/entry` and `/api/catalogue/versions/entry/read`; migrations `catalogue-013-version-watch` and `scorecard-005-up-to-date` (additive: new tables and columns). The demo has a simulated container registry and simulated package registries. No new dependency.

### Changed

- **The outbound policy is checked on every redirect hop** (#243). Steward follows up to 5 redirects of a platform request itself; until now only the first URL went through the outbound policy, so a platform – or whoever controls an allowed host – could redirect the request (without credentials) to a host the policy refuses, internal hosts included. Every redirect target is now checked with the same rules as the first request: a redirect on the same origin, from `http` to `https` on the same host, or to the platform's own origins (base URL, trusted origins, an AWS account's endpoints) passes as before; any other host must be another platform's origin (policy _platforms_) or on the allow-list. A refused redirect fails the call at once (no retry) and names both hosts and how to allow the target. This covers every platform request – process http steps and pagination, monitor and freshness checks, _Check now_, _Test connection_, ad-hoc requests, the built-in readers (endoflife.date, Dependency-Track, AWS, Kubernetes, Prometheus, Gatus, AI gateways, S3 backups …) – and the requests to one URL that followed redirects without a platform: monitor HTTP checks, monitor webhook outputs and Teams cards, the OpenAPI import by URL, and notification webhooks and Teams channels (_Settings → Notifications_, a team's own channel; at most 5 redirects now). A `307`/`308` redirect to a host outside the platform's own origins is refused when the body it would send again contains one of the platform's credentials (stored or obtained for the request, also Base64-, URL- or JSON-encoded) or, for a webhook, the credential in its URL; a `301`–`303` drops the body as before. The MCP gateway, Git sync, SBOM URLs and the Entra ID, IBM PAM, Kafka and Confluent reads do not follow redirects at all. **Check before updating:** a request whose redirect leaves the allowed hosts – for example a portal that sends API calls to a separate sign-in or storage host – now fails with "Blocked by the outbound policy: … redirected to …, which is not a configured platform or on the allow-list" until the target is allowed: add it under _Settings → Outbound access_ (`outboundAllowList`), or to the platform's trusted origins (its credentials are then sent there too); a monitor HTTP check whose redirect is refused reports _unknown_ ("Target not allowed"). Notification webhooks and Teams channels: their stored URL is still not checked by the policy, but a redirect to another host now needs the policy (allow-list), and a URL or redirect to a cloud-metadata or link-local address or to Steward's own API (`/api/…` on its own port) is refused – check _Send test_ under _Settings → Notifications_ and on teams with their own channel.
- **AWS cost reading** (#226): Cost Explorer is asked once a day in **one** request series grouped by the application tag and by service (before: two), for 12 months and the current one (before: six) – the component readout still shows six months, and the costs go into the cost model ([docs/AWS.md](docs/AWS.md)).
- **Dependencies**: `ajv` 8.20.0 is now a direct dependency of the server (the JSON Schema check of data contracts); it was already installed as a dependency of Fastify.

### Security

- **Platform costs and the version watch respect the reader's rights throughout** (#226, #241): costs, budgets and imported cost lines no longer reveal or touch applications the reader may not see, cost check results follow _Hide amounts from viewers_, and the version watch no longer names hidden systems or platforms or shows its settings to everybody. The scorecard part _Tech stack_ (0.8.0) leaves out systems the reader may not see as well.
- **Data contracts: checking and comparing contracts is bounded** (#221): the JSON Schema check and the comparison of versions run in linear time, documents are capped at 10,000 quality rules and 250,000 values, and creating, checking, importing, comparing and saving contracts count against the heavy rate limit.
- **Data contracts: SQL quality rules follow Steward's database rules** (#221): saving a contract with SQL rules needs the _use_ right on the linked database platform, every test checks _use_ again for whoever runs it (by hand, a monitor's owner, a run's actor, a schedule's owner), and every rule must pass Steward's statement check on save and before each test. The documentation says that these rules run in the data contract service with the platform's read-only account, outside Steward's read-only session.
- **Data contracts: only Steward's own tests and lint call the data contract service** (#221): process steps, the explorer, ad-hoc requests, the MCP gateway and monitors can only read it.
- **Data contracts: the tested database's TLS setting is honoured** (#221): the service cannot verify PostgreSQL and MySQL certificates, so their tests are refused while TLS is on unless the platform allows it (off by default, audited); SQL Server gets its TLS settings.
- **Data contracts: Steward resolves and checks the tested host** (#221) and sends the service the address it checked; a name it cannot resolve is refused.
- **Data contracts: the change record rule holds** (#221): switching it off needs the _manage_ right, a change record covers one version of one contract, the rule outlives deleting the contract, and duplicate names and values of the wrong type in compared fields are refused on save.
- **Data contracts: scorecard history hides contracts the reader may not see** (#221): history, trend and `/api/v1` history show the part as hidden, like documentation.
- **Data contracts: test evidence is kept** (#221): failed test reports stay beyond the newest 500 runs, and dry runs are kept on their own, so they cannot push out real ones.
- **A redirect can no longer lead a request past the outbound policy** (#243): every redirect target of a platform request, a monitor HTTP check, a monitor webhook or an OpenAPI import by URL is checked like the first URL – also under the policy _allowList_, where a redirect to a private address was followed before. Address safety (cloud-metadata and link-local addresses, Steward's own API) applies to every hop as before.
- **Notification webhooks and Teams channels go through address safety** (#243): they were sent without the address guard and followed any redirect – to a cloud-metadata or link-local address, Steward's own API or any internal host. Now the URL and every redirect resolve through the guard, and a redirect to another host follows the outbound policy.
- **A 307/308 redirect no longer re-sends a credential in the body to another host** (#243): Steward drops secret-carrying headers on such a hop, but sent the body again unscanned. A body with one of the platform's credentials (or a webhook URL's) is now refused before it is sent there.
- **A platform's credentials only travel through its own connection** ([docs/SECURITY-HARDENING.md](docs/SECURITY-HARDENING.md#which-rule-checks-which-call)): in a process, `$secret('<slot>', …)` – in the URL, query, headers or body, a browser step's `fill` or `evaluate` value, a database query's parameters – may only be used in a step on that same slot. A step on another slot now fails before anything is sent, also in dry runs and test runs (`SECRET_OTHER_SLOT`), and the refusal is audited (`run.secret_refused`, without the value). **Check before updating:** a process that sends one platform's credential through another connection – for example `headers: { x-api-key: "{{ $secret('a', 'apiKey') }}" }` in a step on connection `b`, or a browser step without a connection that fills a platform's password – now fails with "Blocked: this request on connection … contains a credential of connection …". Send the request on the platform's own connection (bind the browser step to it), or store the credential on the platform that needs it.
- **Read-only kinds and the vault's refused paths apply whichever platform sends the request**: a request that reaches a Kafka Connect, changedetection.io, container registry, data contract service or IBM Verify Privilege Vault platform – the origin of its base URL below its path, or one of its trusted origins – gets that kind's method and path rules and has the kind's values removed from its answer, also when another platform sends it (an absolute URL, a redirect hop) and on every hop. Browser steps never open such a platform. **Container registries are read-only** now too (only `GET` and `HEAD`), as their presets say. **Check before updating:** a request through another platform to such a platform – a generic platform on the same host and path as a registry (Harbor, Artifactory, Nexus), a step that changes a changedetection.io watch through a generic platform, a browser step on changedetection.io's or the vault's web pages – now fails with `PLATFORM_METHOD_DENIED`, `PLATFORM_PATH_DENIED` or `BROWSER_RESTRICTED_ORIGIN`; give the other system its own base URL (another host or path) if it is not the restricted one.
- **Confirming settings changed outside Steward shows and resets what the platform page does not show** ([docs/PLATFORM-SIGN-IN.md](docs/PLATFORM-SIGN-IN.md#identities)): the confirmation dialog now lists each identity's MCP mode, credential names, variables and sign-in settings; confirming resets the default identity's variables, own sign-in, flags, operation filter and credential source (none of which exist in this release) and removes any other identity, and the audit entry records them. Variables used in the base URL, a token URL, the trusted origins or the portal URL always stay the platform's when a request is sent (`IDENTITY_VARIABLE_FIXED`). New route `GET /api/platforms/{id}/confirm-config` (administrators) for the dialog.
- **Address safety covers every form of an IP address**: IPv6 literals in URLs, IPv4-mapped (dotted and hex), NAT64 and IPv4-compatible addresses are classified like their IPv4 form, link-local IPv6 and the cloud-metadata addresses are refused in every form, and the guard for Steward's own API also covers `[::1]`, IPv4-mapped loopback, `0.0.0.0`, `[::]`, this machine's addresses and host names that resolve to them – for the first request and every redirect hop, platform requests (an IP literal in a base URL is checked before connecting), notification channels and monitor HTTP checks. Allow-list CIDRs may be IPv6.
- **Monitor HTTP checks keep the body rule on 307/308 redirects** (#243): a body containing one of the check's header values is not sent again to another host (the check reports _unknown_, "Target not allowed").
- **Kafka Connect configurations lose more credential shapes** ([docs/KAFKA.md](docs/KAFKA.md#configurations-and-secrets)): `Authorization`, `Bearer` and `Basic` values (an HTTP sink's `headers`), JDBC URLs with `user/password@` or `user:password@`, keys ending in `auth` or `headers`, and private keys and JSON Web Tokens inside lists and nested objects.
- **changedetection.io watch URLs are kept without credentials**: user info, the fragment and the values of secret-looking query parameters are removed before a watch is stored or shown (also in explorer answers); only `http(s)` URLs are kept.
- **Example process _changedetection.io – Review a changed page_** puts the notification's title and tags (and the watch's page) into the review task as code, so a forged notification cannot add links or formatting.
- **Smaller hardening**: client-certificate connections are kept per identity; the OpenAPI import by URL allows plain `http` only to `localhost` and `127.0.0.1` themselves (not to host names that start with them); changedetection.io answers never get a `__proto__` key in Steward's copy.

## [0.8.0] - 2026-11-06

Steward 0.8.0 completes the **plant registry** with the OT network and adds **pictures** of it, **database schemas** and a **DynamoDB freshness check**. The plant registry (opt-in, preview) now documents VLANs, IEC 62443-3-2 zones and conduits, devices and data paths, with live status only where a Steward check is linked, and draws site structures, data flows, data point lineage, KPI coverage and the OT network – animated where it shows direction, with a table for every picture. Database platforms read their schema (or take a tbls `schema.json` pushed from CI with a push token bound to one database), keep snapshots, mark breaking changes and export DBML or SQL DDL. Two security reviews found and fixed leaks of hidden datasets and monitors in live status, a runnable-statement path in the SQL export, and – older – a full URL in platform and freshness checks that skipped the outbound allow-list; see _Check before updating_ under Changed.

### Added

- **Plant registry, part 3: the OT network and live status (preview, #220, [docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#part-3-ot-network), [ADR 0007 – Part 3](docs/adr/0007-plant-registry.md#part-3-ot-network-080))**: how a plant's network looks and whether an asset is online.
  - **OT network of a site** (site page → _Open the OT network_, tabs _Status_, _VLANs_, _Zones and conduits_, _Devices_, _Data paths_): **VLANs** after IEEE 802.1Q (VID 1–4094, type, owner, CIDR subnets with blocked, dynamic and static ranges, restricted, who manages it, where its rules are kept, other sites, location); **IEC 62443-3-2 zones** (criticality, VLANs, owner, target security level SL-T – open until a risk assessment sets it) and **conduits** between two zones (direction, protocols, controls).
  - **Network segments**: a VLAN links to a Steward network segment – picked, or **Create segment from this VLAN**; a linked pair whose VID or subnets differ shows a hint. Nothing becomes a segment on its own.
  - **Firewall changes link to a conduit**: `conduit` is a new link kind; the conduit lists the records that link to it.
  - **On the asset page – OT network**: its **devices** (host name and domain, purpose, MAC as EUI-48 in any usual spelling, serial number, VLAN, address type and IPv4/IPv6 address, switch and port, wall outlet, CMDB port, state, flags, confidence, owner, monitoring) and **data paths** (subject, devices, collector – a Steward application, platform or process, or a job –, transfers with protocol, push or pull and peer, destinations); hosts are Steward systems, so chains can be followed link by link.
  - **Live status**: an asset or device is _online_, _offline_ or _unknown_ only from its linked checks – monitors or one of their checks (Gatus, CheckMk, the MQTT silent-device check, Kafka …) and dataset freshness. Unknown is never shown as online. Shown on the asset page, the site page and the site's status overview.
  - **YAML import and export** in format 1 with new optional keys `ot_network(s)` and `asset_network(s)`, the same modes and preview; the JSON Schema is regenerated; an invented example `examples/plant/brightwater-ot-network.plant.yaml`.
  - **Rights**: the OT network follows its site (the Plant viewer sees it; status viewers and status pages never); a monitor or dataset the reader may not see is not named – it counts towards the status as "a check you may not see" with its state only. Every change is audited.
  - **API**: internal routes below `/api/plant/sites/:siteId/network|vlans|zones|conduits` and `/api/plant/assets/:assetId/network|devices|paths|checks`; read endpoints `GET /api/v1/plant/sites/{siteId}/network` and `GET /api/v1/plant/assets/{assetId}/network`; the export carries the OT network. Migration `plant-003-ot-network` (additive: new tables only).
- **Plant registry visualizations (preview, #230, [docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#visualisations))**: pictures of what the registry documents, with motion that shows direction – status only where a Steward item is linked.
  - **Site structure** (site page → _Structure_): the ISA-95 hierarchy as a zoomable treemap – tiles sized by their assets, a status ring (documented lifecycle status, an inner ring for linked monitors), a square per asset, drill-down with a path back – and the buildings as the other grouping (IEC 81346 location instead of function).
  - **Data flow of an asset** (asset page → _Data flow_): interfaces → collector → target systems → KPI jobs → datasets and dashboards; edges carry the number of data points and move in the direction of the flow; inactive interfaces and failing plant checks are dashed and say so in words; pointing at a data point highlights its way end to end.
  - **Lineage of a data point** (data point page): the assets that deliver it, where each publishes it and the KPIs that read it; one node per site when many assets have the profile.
  - **KPI coverage matrix** (_Explore data → KPI coverage_, and a _Coverage_ card on the KPI page): has / could have / cannot (with the missing inputs) / problem per asset and KPI definition, filtered by site, area and profile; a cell opens the asset.
  - **Site overview tiles** on _Plant → Sites_: assets by status, KPI coverage in per cent, open checks, linked monitors.
  - **OT network diagram** (_OT network → Diagram_, with #220): IEC 62443-3-2 zones as areas with their IEEE 802.1Q VLANs and the assets on them, conduits between the zones, data paths from their sources over the collector to their destinations – a way into another zone runs through its conduit (hosts are placed by the network segments they are in), one without a documented conduit says so; devices and VLANs on hover or focus; paths coloured by the live status of their asset (only from #220's linked checks).
  - **Live status only from linked items** – the monitors an asset links to, the freshness of the dataset a KPI instance names, the plant checks; everything else is drawn neutral, and an item without data says _No data_, never OK. A legend says which is which.
  - **Accessible and calm**: a table for every picture (phones start with it), focusable labelled nodes, statuses with icon and word, light and dark; with `prefers-reduced-motion` or the switch _Animation off_ nothing moves. Every picture exports as SVG or PNG. Layouts are computed once (dagre, as the process canvas) and cached; large graphs draw only what is visible and show only names when zoomed out.
  - Internal routes `GET /api/plant/views/overview`, `/api/plant/views/coverage`, `/api/plant/sites/:siteId/structure`, `/api/plant/assets/:assetId/flow`, `/api/plant/profiles/:profileId/points/:pointId/lineage`, `/api/plant/sites/:siteId/network/placement` with the rights of the pages they picture: hidden sites are neither drawn nor counted. No new dependency, no migration.
- **Monitor check _DynamoDB table_** (#233, [docs/MONITORS.md](docs/MONITORS.md#dynamodb-tables), [docs/AWS.md](docs/AWS.md#dynamodb-monitor-checks)): whether the data in a DynamoDB table is current, on an AWS account platform, signed with SigV4 for `dynamodb` and `monitoring` like Steward's other AWS calls (no AWS SDK).
  - **Newest item**: one `Query` per partition key value (up to 20, each with its own age) – `Limit 1`, newest first, on the table or a secondary index whose sort key is the time; only the key and the time attribute are projected and every attribute name is an `ExpressionAttributeNames` placeholder. The time may be ISO 8601, epoch seconds or epoch milliseconds (detected or set). Cadence or maximum age like the freshness check. Nothing of the item is stored.
  - **Write activity**: CloudWatch `GetMetricData` of `ConsumedWriteCapacityUnits` (AWS JSON 1.0) – the newest minute with writes is the age, for tables without a time-sorted key.
  - **Table hygiene**: `DescribeTable` (status `ACTIVE`, item count and size), `DescribeContinuousBackups` (point-in-time recovery), `DescribeTimeToLive` – each required or only reported.
  - Never `Scan` or `GetItem`; bounded calls; throttling is `unknown` with the reason, never `down`; messages are masked like other AWS errors. Ages, writes, item count and size are metrics of the `monitor.<key>` dataset for trends.
  - Editor form with _Check now_, the example monitor _CRM contacts (DynamoDB)_ with all three modes, the template _DynamoDB freshness_, a link from each DynamoDB table of an AWS inventory to a new monitor, and the read-only IAM statements in docs/AWS.md. The demo's AWS mock answers for its table `crm-contacts`. `/api/v1` lists the check type `dynamodb`. No migration.
- **Platform freshness checks can POST** (#233): the freshness check with `source: platform` takes `method` (`GET` default, `POST`), `headers` and a `body`, like the HTTP check – for APIs that read with POST (search endpoints, GraphQL, JSON protocols). The fields are validated (only for the platform source, a body only with POST, valid header names, none the connection sets) and shown in the monitor editor.

- **Database schemas: snapshots, breaking changes, tbls import and exports** (#222, step 1, [docs/DATABASES.md](docs/DATABASES.md#database-schemas)): Steward knows the structure of the databases it cares about and notices when it changes.
  - **Schema read** of database platforms – schemas, tables, views, columns (type, NULL, default, comment), primary, foreign and unique keys, indexes – with Steward's own catalog statements for PostgreSQL, MariaDB/MySQL and SQL Server, in the same read-only transaction as every statement, within limits (2,000 tables, 50,000 columns, the platform's time, row and size limits; more is kept as a _truncated_ snapshot). The demo database has a small shop schema.
  - **tbls import**: the `schema.json` of [tbls](https://github.com/k1LoW/tbls) (not bundled – it runs where the database is), uploaded on the page or pushed from CI with a **push token** – bound to one database, able to do nothing but push its schema, stored as a hash and shown once, with an optional expiry, allowed addresses and a rate limit, created, rotated and revoked on the Schema tab and audited, and working only while its creator is active and may still manage the database (like an API token; the list then says _Creator inactive – create a new token_), checked from the headers before the body is read – for a database platform or for a system without one; checked against the tbls JSON format, at most 10 MB, audited with the file's SHA-256. The output of `tbls lint` becomes checks named by their rule.
  - **Snapshots** per read or import (an unchanged schema adds none), the newest 30 kept by default (an import removes only imported snapshots, never Steward's reads; a recorded breaking change stays while a monitor check still counts it), and an optional **scheduled read** every N hours (job `db-schemas`).
  - **Changes between snapshots**: added, removed and changed tables, columns, keys and indexes, marked **breaking** (removed table or column, narrower or other type, NOT NULL without a default, removed key) or not; copied as Markdown or turned into a new **change request** (each change one code span on one line, the name escaped – nothing of a name becomes a heading, a link or a new block).
  - **Monitor check _Database schema changes_** (`db-schema`): a breaking change (or any change) within the last N hours; optionally reads the platform first. Saving it or _Check now_ needs the editor to see the platform or system (a hidden one answers like a missing one – for every monitor check that names a platform); _Check now_ with `read: true` needs the editor's own _use_ right.
  - **Export** as DBML (written by Steward, no new dependency), SQL DDL of the source dialect, or JSON – for dbdiagram, drawDB, ChartDB or Azimutt; no editor is bundled. Nothing of a snapshot can end a statement or a comment: names are quoted for the dialect, texts are one-line string literals of the dialect, a type or default is written only when it is a plain one (otherwise a comment says it was left out), an expression index only from Steward's own PostgreSQL read; tabs and line breaks in names are kept visible as `␉ ␊ ␍`.
  - **UI**: the tab _Schema_ of database platforms (tables with search and a table detail, changes, history, lint checks, export, settings) and the schema page of a system; phone layout and accessibility checked.
  - **API**: internal routes `/api/db-schemas/:kind/:id[/read|/settings|/import[/preview]|/snapshots/:snapshotId[/export]|/compare]`; push tokens under `/api/db-schemas/:kind/:id/push-tokens`; the push endpoint `POST /api/hooks/db-schema/:kind/:id` (push tokens only – no API token, no session); public read endpoints `/api/v1/platforms/{platformId}/schema/…` and `/api/v1/systems/{systemId}/schema/…` (snapshots, a snapshot, its export, changes, compare – `/api/v1` stays read-only). Rights follow the platform or system; one the caller may not see answers 404. Who may not see the database platform of a system sees it as a system like any other. Audit actions `dbschema.read`, `dbschema.imported`, `dbschema.settings_changed`, `dbschema.push_token_created`, `_rotated`, `_revoked`. Migrations `dbschema-001-snapshots` and `dbschema-002-push-tokens` (additive: new tables only).

### Changed

- **An absolute URL in a platform path goes through the outbound policy.** A platform or freshness check, _Check now_, a platform's test path or an ad-hoc request may still name a full `http(s)://` URL instead of a path, but it is now checked like every other outbound request: it must be the platform's own origin (its base URL, its trusted origins, the AWS endpoints of an AWS account), another platform's origin, or on the outbound allow-list. Platform and freshness checks also check their platform's base URL against the policy, as the DynamoDB and Entra ID checks do. Process http steps and the MCP gateway checked this already. **Check before updating:** a monitor whose platform or freshness check names a host outside the policy now fails with "Blocked by the outbound policy: … is not a configured platform or on the allow-list" – add the host under _Settings → Outbound access_ (`outboundAllowList`), add it to the platform's trusted origins, or set up a platform for that host and use a path; with the policy _allowList_, the base URL of every platform these checks use must be on the list.

### Fixed

- **The live status of the OT network tells nothing the reader may not see** (#220, #230). Linking a dataset to an asset's or device's checks needs the right to see it – in the editor, the API and the YAML import, with the same answer for a dataset that does not exist –, and its columns must be columns of the dataset. A dataset is read as the reader: only the partitions they may read count. A linked monitor or dataset the reader may not see still counts towards the asset's status, but shows only as "a check you may not see" with online, offline or unknown – no name, key, check, reason, time, value or filter –, by one rule for the OT network and the visualizations (which now count such monitors too); the editor keeps or removes it, the export leaves it out and an import keeps it. Choosing another check of a linked monitor or another filter of a linked dataset is a new link. A time column holds a time only between 2000 and a year from now.
- **A VLAN's other sites name only sites the reader may see** (#220) – in the internal API, `/api/v1` and the export (`sites`, as `otherSites` did); a save or an import keeps the ones the caller may not see.
- **Chains of data paths stay fast with many paths** (#220): they are computed from an index built once per request and list at most 50 next and previous data paths each, with the number of the others (`nextMore`, `previousMore`).

## [0.7.0] - 2026-11-06

Steward 0.7.0 brings part 2 of the **plant registry** and direct **SQL database platforms**. The plant registry (opt-in, preview) now documents device profiles with their interfaces and data points (OPC UA, Modbus/TCP, MQTT, file transfer), which asset delivers what and where it is published, KPI definitions after ISO 22400-2 and their instances, and checks for KPIs whose inputs are missing; the public API serves the whole registry, its checks and an export in the import format. Database platforms connect to PostgreSQL, MariaDB/MySQL and SQL Server, read-only in three layers, for access reviews with generated REVOKE statements, account hygiene, monitor checks and datasets. The database migration `plant-002-data-points` only adds tables. The SQL Server driver brings `@azure/msal-node` 6.0.1 with an advisory that does not apply to Steward's SQL logins; it is accepted until 2026-11-30 and the fixed version follows in a patch.

### Added

- **Plant registry, part 2: data points and KPIs (preview, #219, [docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#device-profiles-data-points-and-kpis), [ADR 0007 – Part 2](docs/adr/0007-plant-registry.md#part-2-data-points-and-kpis-070))**: what each asset's interfaces deliver and which KPIs are computed from it.
  - **Device profiles** (_Plant → Device profiles_, a catalogue of the installation): manufacturer, model, version, category and the **interfaces** – OPC UA, Modbus/TCP, MQTT, web, SMB, WebDAV, SFTP, FTP – with direction, default port, sign-in and the client they need, their **data point groups and data points** (data type after OPC UA IEC 62541 or IEC 61131-3, unit as a UCUM code, address, access, update rate, values, notes) and the files of a file-transfer interface. OPC UA NodeIds and Modbus addresses (table, address, count; the function code is shown) are checked on every save. A data point has its own page with the assets that deliver it and the KPI definitions that read it.
  - **KPI definitions** (_Plant → KPI definitions_, a catalogue): category, the ISO 22400-2 attributes (formula, unit, range, trend, timing, audience, production methodology), inputs (a profile's data points), parameters with type, default, range and aliases, result columns of kind _sum_, _ratio_ (numerator and denominator) or _ignore_ with their scope, the computation with its steps, identities and limits; the assets with an instance and those that could have one.
  - **On the asset page – Data and KPIs**: the device profile and controller firmware, per interface whether it is set up and its endpoint, data points that differ on this asset, where its data points are published (target system, path, name), its **KPI instances** (job, active, parameters, the dashboard or dataset with the result) and the KPIs it can have.
  - **Checks** (on the asset, the KPI definition, _Plant → Checks_ and in the import preview, never blocking): a KPI instance whose data point inputs the asset does not deliver (no or another profile, an interface not active, a data point it lacks), parameters outside the definition (unknown, twice, wrong type, out of range, required but missing), a ratio without its numerator or denominator.
  - **Explore data**: which assets deliver a data point (a text across every profile), which KPIs an asset can have; the asset list filters by profile and data point.
  - **YAML import and export** in format 1 with new optional keys `device_profiles`, `kpi_definitions` and `assignments` (also one per file), the same modes and preview; usual spellings of other tools (`OPC-UA`, `Modbus/TCP`, `float[]`, `kWh`) are read as their standard value. The JSON Schema is regenerated; files of 0.6.0 stay valid. Exports of the catalogues, one profile and one KPI definition.
  - **Rights**: everyone who sees the registry sees the catalogues (the Plant viewer too); designers and administrators manage them; assignments and KPI instances follow the asset's site. Lists, counts, search and checks leave out assets of sites the reader may not see. _Duplicate_ for profiles and KPI definitions; an asset's copy takes its profile, not its endpoints or KPI instances. Every change is audited.
  - **API**: internal routes `/api/plant/profiles`, `/api/plant/kpis`, `/api/plant/assets/:assetId/assignment`, `/api/plant/explore/points`, `/api/plant/checks`; read endpoints `/api/v1/plant/profiles[/{profileId}]`, `/api/v1/plant/kpis[/{kpiId}]`, `/api/v1/plant/assets/{assetId}/assignment` and the filters `profile`, `dataPoint` on `/api/v1/plant/assets`.
  - The demo adds three invented device profiles, two KPI definitions and the assignments of four assets (`examples/plant/brightwater-data-points.plant.yaml`), one with a check problem on purpose. Migration `plant-002-data-points` (additive: new tables only).
- **Database platforms: PostgreSQL, MariaDB/MySQL and SQL Server, read-only** ([docs/DATABASES.md](docs/DATABASES.md), [ADR 0008](docs/adr/0008-database-platforms.md), #203): Steward connects to databases directly for access reviews, account hygiene, monitor checks and datasets.
  - **Platform kind _database_** (presets _PostgreSQL_, _MariaDB / MySQL_ and _Microsoft SQL Server (read-only)_, group _Databases_ in the setup wizard) with pure-JavaScript drivers (`pg`, new: `mysql2`, `tedious`). The new tab _Database_ holds the connection – host, port, database, TLS (verify with an optional CA, encrypt only, off), the limits and the pool size – sealed and audited (`platform.database_changed`); user name and password are credentials. The **connection test** reports the read-only session, an account that can write and the catalog queries the account may not read.
  - **Process step _Query a database_** (`type: query`): Steward's **catalog queries** (`status`, `accounts`, `role-members`, `privileges`, `replication`, `tables` – the same columns for every DBMS, with generated `REVOKE` / lock statements quoted for it) or **own SQL** – one `SELECT` or `WITH … SELECT` with bound parameters (`$1`, `?`, `@p1`). Always read-only: a read-only transaction on PostgreSQL and MariaDB/MySQL, a transaction that is rolled back on SQL Server, plus a conservative statement check in the editor and on the server; a time, row and size limit per statement (the platform's limits are the ceiling). The result is stored like an HTTP answer (run reports, evidence, the editor's _Last run_ shows a table); dry runs query, test runs use the recorded response `POST /sql/<step id>`.
  - **Monitor check _Database_**: the connection, the replication lag, or the value of a read-only query against WARN and CRIT.
  - **MCP gateway**: off by default; when on, the tool `database_query` (read-only, the same checks, audited); changing statements are refused (`MCP_WRITES_NOT_AVAILABLE`).
  - **Templates** for every supported DBMS: _Database – Access review_ (four-eyes, generated `REVOKE` statements as a manual step, documented access changes), _Database – Account hygiene_, _Database – Table sizes_, the own-SQL example _PostgreSQL – Connections per database_, the dashboard _Databases_, a monitor example and the bundle _Databases_. The demo has the simulated platform _Shop database (demo)_ (canned catalog rows; it never reads Steward's own database).
  - The host:port of a database follows the outbound policy like Kafka and MQTT; connections are pooled per platform (at most 5, closed after 60 s idle, on any change of the platform and on shutdown). New internal route `PUT /api/platforms/:id/database`; `/api/v1` lists the check type `database`. No migration.
  - **Hardened before release** (security review): the statement check also reads quoted function names and refuses functions that run SQL given as text, MariaDB/MySQL optimizer hints `/*+ … */`, row locks (`FOR SHARE` …, SQL Server lock hints) and the T-SQL words that start another statement (also a second `SELECT` outside parentheses) – a best-effort extra layer, the read-only account stays the protection; refusals are shown translated in the editor. PostgreSQL connections release session advisory locks before they are reused; a MariaDB/MySQL statement stopped by the time limit is ended on the server with `KILL QUERY`; a platform may not name Steward's own database (`DB_OWN_DATABASE`; other databases on its server are allowed); driver errors are masked in monitor results and run steps.
- **Plant registry in the public API: checks and export** (#219, [docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md#fetching-the-registry-from-other-tools)): two more read endpoints for other tools and backups.
  - `GET /api/v1/plant/checks` lists what _Plant → Checks_ shows – kind, English message, asset, site, KPI definition, job and details – for the assets the token owner may see and the KPI definitions; filters `site`, `asset`, `kpi` (the definition's own problems and those of its instances), `kind`; paginated like the other lists.
  - `GET /api/v1/plant/export` gives the registry in the import format, as the export in the web app: everything the token owner may see, one site, the catalogues (`part=catalogue`), one profile or KPI definition; YAML (default) or JSON (`format=json`). Sites the owner may not see and their assets are never in it; it imports back unchanged and is audited (`plant.exported`, with the token id).
  - While the module is off, the plant endpoints of the public API now say so in their `404` (before: "Plant registry not found").

### Fixed

- **Plant registry: deleting a device profile or KPI definition in use** no longer hints at assets the person may not see: when an asset of a hidden site has it, the answer is only that it is in use (`PLANT_PROFILE_IN_USE_HIDDEN`, `PLANT_KPI_IN_USE_HIDDEN`, without a count); who sees every asset that has it gets the count.
- **Plant registry pages: long identifiers** no longer break mid-word ("ProgramStatu / s"): paths, names, addresses and ids wrap after `/`, `.`, `_`, `:` and `;`; in the publications table of an asset and the table of assets that deliver a data point they stay whole and the table scrolls when they do not fit.

## [0.6.0] - 2026-11-05

Steward 0.6.0 brings **AI models through LLM gateways** and part 1 of the **plant registry**. The process step _Ask an AI model_ sends a saved question with context from earlier steps, datasets or run inputs to an LLM gateway (OpenAI Chat Completions and every OpenAI-compatible API, OpenAI Responses, Anthropic Messages, Bedrock Converse), with allowed data per gateway, input limits per call, token budgets per day and month, a usage log and the _AI usage_ dataset; answers are marked as AI-generated. The plant registry (opt-in, preview) keeps the factories of an enterprise in one place: sites with their ISA-95 hierarchy and buildings, assets, links to Steward's systems and applications, and YAML import and export with a published JSON Schema, including replacing an earlier import. Status viewers are now also refused by the public API and the MCP endpoint. The database migrations (`ai-001`, `ai-002`, `plant-001`) only add tables and a column.

### Added

- **AI models through an LLM gateway** ([docs/AI.md](docs/AI.md), #216): a process asks an AI model a predefined question with context from earlier steps, CheckMk and other platforms or datasets, and uses the answer in later steps – a report, a check message, an e-mail or Teams notification. The AI does not act on its own; there are no tool calls and no chat.
  - **Platform kind _LLM gateway_** (preset _LLM gateway (AI models)_, group _AI models_ in the setup wizard): a base URL and the usual sign-in types – an API key in any header (`x-api-key` by default, `api-key`, `Authorization`) or a bearer token. One gateway can serve several request formats, so **the format belongs to the model**: OpenAI Chat Completions (also every OpenAI-compatible API), OpenAI Responses, Anthropic Messages (with `anthropic-version`) and Bedrock Converse (model id in the path). No streaming, no vendor SDK: one small adapter per format.
  - **Models** on the new tab _AI models_: read from `GET /v1/models` or entered by hand, each with its format, an optional path, the context window, the most output (and input) per call, an optional price per 1,000 input and output tokens and the characters per token of the estimate. **Allowed data** says what the gateway may be sent – data of chosen platforms, datasets by key pattern, run inputs; nothing until someone allows it. It is checked on the values sent, whichever step wrote them: the run records where each variable's value comes from, and a computed value counts as carrying everything of its run. Both are part of the platform's sealed settings and audited (`platform.ai_changed`). The **connection test** lists the models and asks each one once.
  - **Process step _Ask an AI model_** (`type: ai`): instructions and a question as a template saved with the process – `{{ name }}` inserts a context source, nothing else is filled in at run time; context from earlier steps, datasets or inputs, each with a size limit and an optional expression; secrets and secret-looking values removed before sending; the answer as text, or as JSON checked against a JSON Schema in the step (`AI_ANSWER_INVALID` otherwise; no `pattern` keywords); a fixed model, temperature and output limit; an error output. Dry runs use a sample answer and test runs a recorded response – neither calls the model.
  - **AI-generated marking**: reports and check messages (in-app, e-mail, Teams, webhook) that carry an answer say so and name the model; an approval with four-eyes before such a message leaves Steward uses the existing user tasks.
  - **Limits**: the input is estimated **before** sending (characters / 3.5, plus overhead; no tokenizer) and refused above the step's or the model's limit (`AI_INPUT_TOO_LARGE`), without a call. **Budgets** of tokens per day and month (UTC) for the installation, a gateway and a process (including its sub-processes): a call whose estimated input plus its most output no longer fits is refused before it is sent (`AI_BUDGET_EXHAUSTED`); the worst case is reserved in the same transaction, so parallel steps cannot overspend. A notification (event _AI budget_) goes out at 80 % and when a budget is used up. Raising a budget is audited (`ai.budget_changed`) and needs a step-up – the new control point `ai.budget` of the assurance policy (on by default). Token counts come from each format's usage fields.
  - **Reporting**: a usage log per call (time, process, run, step, gateway, model, format, tokens, duration, result, fingerprints of the prompt and the input – no content), the tab _AI usage_ on the gateway page (budgets, use today and this month, top processes and models, budgets of single processes, the latest calls), the built-in dataset _AI usage_ (`ai.usage`) with the dashboard template _AI usage_, the report section _AI usage_ for a monthly report schedule, and audit entries for refused calls (`ai.call_refused`).
  - **Demo and tests**: a simulated multi-format LLM gateway (`/mock/llm`, one model per format) as the demo platform _LLM gateway (demo)_, and the example process _CheckMk – AI summary of the trends_ (JSON answer, four-eyes approval, report and check).
  - New: the setting `SM_AI_USAGE_RETENTION_DAYS` (default 400 days), the background job `ai-usage`, the database migrations `ai-001-usage-and-budgets` (additive: tables `ai_usage`, `ai_budgets`, `ai_budget_notices`) and `ai-002-usage-parent-processes` (additive: column `ai_usage.parent_process_ids`), and the internal routes `PUT /api/platforms/:id/ai`, `POST /api/platforms/:id/ai/discover`, `GET /api/platforms/:id/ai/usage`, `GET`/`PUT /api/ai/budgets` and `GET /api/ai/usage`.
- **Plant registry, part 1 (preview, opt-in module, #217/#218, [docs/PLANT.md](docs/PLANT.md), [ADR 0007](docs/adr/0007-plant-registry.md))**: the factories of the enterprise in one place – off by default; turned off, nothing of it shows and its routes answer 404.
  - **Settings → Modules → Plant registry**: the switch, the enterprise's name, own display names per record type and language (_Machine_ instead of _Asset_) and own fields per record type, checked on every save and import. The sidebar gets the area **Plant** (_Sites_, _Assets_, _Import_).
  - **Sites** with code, translated names, position (a link to OpenStreetMap), contacts, external references (system, id, link) and own fields; their hierarchy after **ISA-95 / IEC 62264** – areas, work centers, work units, with an optional ISA-95 type and domain – and a separate axis of **buildings** (halls, floors, outdoor areas; IEC 81346: function and location apart), where the innermost building of a node wins.
  - **Assets** with a stable id, manufacturer, model, status, location (the innermost place decides, the levels above follow; an optional own building), the equipment they are part of (same site, no cycles) and their parts, taxonomy, tags, lifecycle, capabilities, an image (PNG, JPEG or WebP) and external references; the list filters by site, place, domain, status and tag. Assets can be duplicated (without their external references).
  - **Links**: sites and assets are link kinds – documents, records, tickets, services and applications name them, an asset names the systems, applications and platforms it uses (shown on the system page under _Linked from_). A reader sees only targets in sites they may see.
  - **YAML import and export** in a versioned format with a published **JSON Schema 2020-12** (`docs/plant/plant-registry-1.schema.json`): one file per site, assets apart or a whole tree, several files at once; a **preview** of what is added, changed and removed; the modes _add only_, _add and update_ and _replace an earlier import_ (missing records removed, or kept and marked _not in source_); stable ids; problems with the place in the file; limits like the other YAML importers; every import audited with its counts and the files' SHA-256. The export imports back unchanged.
  - **Permissions per site**: role defaults (everyone who works in Steward sees a site, designers manage it), an access list (_view_, _manage_; adding entries asks for a step-up), owning teams, and the new role **Plant viewer** (`plant`) that sees only the registry (the server refuses everything else to a user with no other working role; the web app shows the Plant area and the profile). Nothing of it is on status pages.
  - **API**: the internal routes under `/api/plant/…` and read endpoints `/api/v1/plant/sites[/{siteId}]` and `/api/v1/plant/assets[/{assetId}]` (`ref=system:id` finds an asset by another tool's id).
  - The demo loads an invented enterprise with two sites (`examples/plant/brightwater.plant.yaml`) when the module is turned on. Migration `plant-001-registry` (additive: new tables only).

### Fixed

- **Status viewers and API tokens**: a status viewer is refused by the public API (`/api/v1`) and the MCP endpoint, also with a token made before the account became a status viewer; before, such a token could still read the API.
- **Process editor**: expression fields no longer carry `aria-expanded` on their text area (an accessibility error screen readers and axe report); the suggestions stay announced.
- **Platform page on phones**: the panel with the last connection test no longer squeezes its text to one letter a line next to the periodic test.

## [0.5.1] - 2026-11-05

Steward 0.5.1 publishes the container images of 0.5.0. Their build stopped at the vulnerability scan: Debian published a fix for CVE-2026-109518 in `apt` (high) after 0.5.0 was merged, and the pinned base image still had the old version. The application is the same as in 0.5.0; the executables of 0.5.0 are not affected (they contain no Debian packages). No database migrations; update from 0.4.x straight to 0.5.1 (the notes of 0.5.0 apply).

### Fixed

- **Container image**: the image installs Debian's security updates when it is built (`apt-get upgrade`), so a fix published after the pinned base image – here `apt` 3.0.4 for CVE-2026-109518 – no longer waits for a new base image digest. The Trivy and Grype scans check the result as before.

## [0.5.0] - 2026-11-04

Steward 0.5.0 makes changes audit-proof and reads more platforms. **Firewall changes** (opt-in, no CMDB): network segments as a system kind, systems in segments with their addresses, the document types _Firewall change_ (`FWC`, four-eyes, evidence, verified by a second person) and _Approved firewall rules_ (`FWR`, a living document per segment or system), table fields for document types, and an optional _Verify_ step on the change workflow. The **assurance policy** (_Settings → Assurance policy_) sets per control point which actions need a second person and which a fresh confirmation, optionally with MFA; baselines cannot be lowered, loosening waits for a second administrator, every confirmed event carries its proof in the audit chain, and the assurance report (PDF/A, JSON, in evidence packs) shows it for a period. Two accounts of one single sign-on identity count as one person. **Microsoft Graph / Entra ID** (read-only, client secret or certificate): access check source, monitor check, credentials import and the bundle _Entra ID access governance_. **IBM Security Verify Privilege Vault 12.0**: permissions and roles for access reviews, secret hygiene, privileged-use evidence and a credentials register link – Steward never reads a secret value. **Export for Postman**: processes and platforms as a collection and an environment, without secrets. Three database migrations, all additive: `catalogue-012-network-segments`, `assurance-001-policy`, `credentials-004-vault-link`. **Check before updating:** nothing changes with the default assurance policy; change requests gain an optional _Verify_ step (closing from _Implemented_ still works); existing installations get the document types `FWC` and `FWR` (an own `FWC` key is kept); the sign-in template variable `baseUrl` no longer ends with a slash; the field names of the vault integration are taken from Delinea's public SDKs – check them against your installation with the checklist in docs/IBM-PAM.md.

### Added

- **Audit-proof firewall changes for network segments and systems** (opt-in, no CMDB, [docs/FIREWALL-CHANGES.md](docs/FIREWALL-CHANGES.md)). Only what needs it is documented – a shopfloor IoT VLAN, a server whose firewall a provider manages; systems in a cloud need nothing.
  - **Network segments**: the system kind _Network segment_ with optional attributes – VLAN ID (1–4094), subnets in CIDR notation (IPv4 and IPv6, format-checked only), zone or security level (e.g. an IEC 62443 zone), site and who manages its firewall (own team or a provider). Everything systems have works for segments (owners, owning teams, environments, CMDB link, documents, _runs on_).
  - **Systems in segments**: any system can be in segments, with an optional address (IP address or host name, format-checked) and a note; the segment lists the systems in it. Who may change the system decides; segments and systems a reader may not see are left out.
  - **Systems are a link kind**: documents, firewall changes and ticket references can name a server or segment directly; a reader who may not see the system sees the link as _not visible_.
  - **Firewall & network** panel on the system page: segments and addresses, the approved firewall rules with their edition and next review, open and closed firewall changes, and the ticket references (e.g. the provider's tickets); records that link to the system under _Linked from_.
  - **Firewall change (`FWC`)**, a built-in document type on the change workflow, also in existing installations: affected segments and systems (links), reason, risk, who implements it (internal or a provider), the provider's ticket, the maintenance window (planned start and end), rollback plan and the rule lines (add/remove, source, destination, protocol/port, allow/deny, enforced at perimeter, host or cloud security group, purpose, expiry). Approved under four-eyes, implemented, **evidence** added (or a run linked), **verified by someone other than the implementer**, then closed. Before closing, each rule line shows what it does to the approved rules.
  - **Approved firewall rules (`FWR`)**, a built-in living document per segment or system: closing a firewall change applies its rule lines and submits the next edition, which an approver confirms – so the document always shows the approved state; a yearly review recertifies the rules.
  - **Process template _Firewall change via provider_** (`examples/processes/87-firewall-change-via-provider.yaml`): request, four-eyes approval, submit to the provider, the provider's confirmation as evidence, an HTTP connectivity check, verification by a second person, close.
  - **Control mappings**: new installations map `FWC` to ISO/IEC 27001 A.8.20, A.8.22 and A.8.32 and `FWR` to A.8.20–A.8.22; the documentation has an importable IEC 62443-3-3 catalogue for SR 5.1 and SR 5.2.
- **Table fields** for document types: rows with fixed columns (text, choice, date; required or not), checked per cell, limited to 12 columns, 200 rows and 500 characters per cell. Shown below the text, edited row by row, compared row by row under _Versions_ and printed as a table in the PDF. The value is JSON in the field, so the content hash and the API stay as they are ([docs/RECORDS.md](docs/RECORDS.md#table-fields)).
- **Verify a change**: the change workflow has an optional step _Verify_ (Implemented → Verified) for someone other than the person who marked the change implemented; _Close_ works from _Implemented_ as before and from _Verified_. Firewall changes require it.
- **Evidence after the approval**: while a change is approved or implemented, editors add evidence files (e.g. a provider's confirmation). They are not content – the approved content and its hash stay unchanged –, each is audited with its SHA-256 and cannot be removed; the PDF lists them.
- **Duplicate systems**: _Duplicate_ on a system or segment copies its attributes, owners and the stack entered by hand; a copied system is in the same segments without the original's addresses.
- API: `POST/PUT/DELETE /api/systems/:id/segments…`, `POST /api/records/:id/evidence`, `GET /api/records/:id/firewall`, `POST /api/systems/:id/duplicate`; `/api/v1/systems` carries `attributes` and `segments`; link kind `system`; attachment kind `evidence`. Migration `catalogue-012-network-segments` (additive: column `systems.attributes`, table `system_segments`).
- **Export for Postman** (#204): a process becomes a Postman collection (v2.1) with one folder – its requests in step order – and an environment for its connections; a platform becomes an environment and a collection of the operations of its API descriptions. **No secret is exported**: every credential, secret input and secret-looking value is an empty environment variable of type `secret`. Sign-ins map to Postman's basic, bearer, API key and AWS Signature auth; OAuth 2.0 client credentials and login requests become a collection pre-request script that fetches the token and keeps it until it expires; key-pair JWT asks for a pasted token. Values a later step reads are set by the test script of the request that produces them; checks become `pm.test(...)` – plain JavaScript for a documented JSONata subset, JSONata itself via `pm.require('npm:jsonata@2.2.2')` otherwise (Postman app and CLI, not Newman). Steps without a request (approvals, manual steps, browser steps, datasets, sub-processes, loops, waits) are listed in the folder description. In the process editor's actions menu and on the platform page; a ZIP with both files, or one of them as JSON (`?file=`). API: `GET /api/processes/:id/export/postman`, `GET /api/platforms/:id/export/postman`; audited as `process.exported_postman` and `platform.exported_postman` ([docs/POSTMAN.md](docs/POSTMAN.md)).
- **Microsoft Graph / Entra ID platform**, read-only: the connector _Microsoft Graph (Entra ID, read-only)_ signs in with OAuth 2.0 client credentials – a client secret or, recommended, a certificate – against the tenant's token endpoint (variables `tenantId` and `loginUrl`; national clouds by base URL and login URL) and brings the Graph read endpoints as its API description for process steps and the MCP gateway. Graph's pages (`@odata.nextLink`) are followed on Graph's own host only, throttling (429/503 with `Retry-After`) is waited out a bounded number of times. The **connection test** names in plain words what is missing: admin consent, each application permission, a licence (Entra ID P1/P2), and explains Entra ID's sign-in errors (`AADSTS…`). Step-by-step app registration and the permissions table in [docs/ENTRA.md](docs/ENTRA.md); demo platform _Microsoft Graph (demo)_ on a simulated tenant.
- **Access check source _Entra ID_**: users and guests by user principal name (e-mail addresses as other names, so **person reviews** span Entra ID and the other platforms), group memberships (nested ones marked) and ownerships, directory roles (active, through role-assignable groups, on administrative units and – with Entra ID P2 – eligible through PIM), app role assignments and disabled accounts; the scope reads one part (`groups`, `roles`, `apps`, `guests`). Last sign-in and MFA registration are reviewer hints. Parts the licence or the permissions do not allow are left out and named (_not available (licence or permission)_), never failing the check ([docs/COMPLIANCE.md](docs/COMPLIANCE.md#access-reconciliation)).
- **Entra ID templates and the bundle _Entra ID access governance_**: guest user review, privileged directory role review (four-eyes), stale accounts, group owner and membership review, expiring app secrets and certificates, with the dashboard _Entra ID governance_ and the example monitor _Entra ID app credentials_. The reviews attest, ask a second person, list the removals for the Entra admin center as a manual step with maker–checker and document them as access changes in the rights the access source reads. The setup wizard and _Get started_ list them for a Graph platform.
- **Monitor check _Entra ID_**: app registrations whose secrets or certificates end within N days or have ended (a rotated one next to its replacement does not count), or the Microsoft 365 service health ([docs/MONITORS.md](docs/MONITORS.md#check-types)).
- **Credentials → Import from Entra ID**: the client secrets and certificates of a tenant's app registrations become register entries with their end date as the expiry, so the register's reminders cover them; a later import keeps their expiry in step with Entra ID (history event _Expiry updated from Entra ID_). Preview first; names, ids and dates only. API: `POST /api/credentials/import/entra` ([docs/CREDENTIALS.md](docs/CREDENTIALS.md#app-credentials-from-entra-id)).
- **Certificate sign-in for OAuth2 client credentials**: a client assertion (RFC 7523 `private_key_jwt`) signed with the platform's private key and naming the certificate by its `x5t#S256`/`x5t` thumbprints, instead of the client secret – _Send client credentials as → Client assertion_, or no secret but a private key. The token scope may use `{{ baseUrl }}` and variables ([docs/PLATFORM-SIGN-IN.md](docs/PLATFORM-SIGN-IN.md#client-assertion-certificate)).
- **Assurance policy** (_Settings → Assurance policy_, [docs/ASSURANCE.md](docs/ASSURANCE.md), #210): one place that shows and sets which actions need a second person (four-eyes) and which a fresh confirmation (step-up). Every such action is a **control point** in one catalogue, grouped by area; per point four-eyes (off / per item / required) and step-up (on or off, a maximum age, **require MFA**). A **baseline** cannot be lowered – the master passphrase, audit anchoring, API tokens, granting access, single sign-on and sign-in settings and the policy itself always need a step-up – and the page shows it locked. Overrides stay where they are (a process step, a platform's MCP mode, a person review, the four-eyes dismissal setting); the page lists every one with the rule that applies. With the default policy everything works as in 0.4.0.
- **New control points with a step-up an administrator turns on** (off by default): approving or rejecting a record (an electronic-signature-like confirmation), documenting or dismissing access check findings, submitting and approving person reviews, verifying a manual step, and closing a firewall change (`firewallChange.close`, records of the type FWC). The policy can also require four-eyes for every run approval, person review, finding dismissal and MCP write (a platform's _confirm_ then needs a second person).
- **Changing the policy**: every change needs a step-up and is audited as `assurance.policy_changed` with before and after. Tightening applies at once; **loosening waits for a second administrator** (approve or reject on the page, shown on _System status_ as a pending policy change, expires after 7 days). The stored policy is sealed; the policy in force at any time comes from the audit chain (`GET /api/assurance/policy/as-of`, `…/history`).
- **Multi-factor per control point**: where MFA is required a password step-up does not count (`ASSURANCE_MFA_REQUIRED`), and a single sign-on step-up needs `amr` or `acr` showing MFA (the policy lists which values count; Steward asks the provider for it).
- **Audit proof per event**: the audit entries of each confirmed or four-eyes event carry the control point, the policy version and rule, requester and approver, the method (password, single sign-on with the provider's `auth_time`, `amr` and `acr`, emergency) and a session reference – inside the HMAC chain. Step-up entries name the session reference too.
- **Assurance report** (PDF/A and JSON, `GET /api/assurance/report`) for a period: the policy in force and its changes, per control point the events and how they were confirmed, and the exceptions (emergency sign-ins, missing MFA, loosening with its second administrator). Download it from the policy page, or include it in an evidence pack (_Include the assurance report_; listed in the signed manifest). Example control mappings: ISO/IEC 27001 A.5.3, A.8.5, A.8.32.
- Migration `assurance-001-policy` (additive): table `assurance_policy_changes`, column `sessions.step_up_detail`.
- **IBM Security Verify Privilege Vault** (12.0, Delinea Secret Server under the IBM name; #205) as a source of evidence – who may use which privileged account, whether rotation works, who used a secret. Steward never reads a secret value ([docs/IBM-PAM.md](docs/IBM-PAM.md)).
  - **Platform preset** _IBM Verify Privilege Vault_: on premises (`https://HOST/SecretServer`) or in the cloud, sign-in with a service account through `POST /oauth2/token` (password grant, optional `domain`, the token renewed with its refresh token), a private CA under the HTTP settings, the built-in API description of the metadata endpoints, and a **connection test** that tries every read Steward makes and names a missing permission in plain words (_the service account may not list roles – the role permission "View Roles"_).
  - **Secret values are refused** for this kind, whatever asks – process steps, the MCP gateway, monitors, Steward's own readers, also after a redirect: `/secrets/{id}` (its answer holds the field values), its fields and files, restricted reads, exports, one-time codes, the internals and SOAP APIs, and searching by a field. Value keys (`itemValue`, `extendedFields` …) are removed from every answer before it is used, stored or logged.
  - **Access reviews**: the access check source _IBM Verify Privilege Vault permissions and roles_ – who reaches which secret and folder, directly, through a group or inherited from the folder, with the permission (List, View, Edit, Owner), and who holds which role; optionally one folder. Users appear as `domain\user` with their e-mail address, so person reviews match them across sources; four-eyes decisions and documented access changes work as for every source.
  - **Hygiene**: the template _IBM Verify Privilege Vault – Secret hygiene_ (dataset `pv.hygiene`), the dashboard _Vault hygiene_ and the monitor check _Vault hygiene_ find failed heartbeats, passwords not changed within N days (or never, or the last change failed), expired passwords, automatic changing turned off, secrets nobody owns, and privileged secrets – folders and templates you name – without checkout or approval.
  - **Credentials register link**: an entry can name its vault secret; every six hours (or _Read now_) Steward reads its name, template, folder, last change and expiry. The vault's last change counts as the entry's last rotation, so overdue rotation is flagged and reminded. Migration `credentials-004-vault-link` (additive). API: `POST /api/credentials/:id/vault/read`, `GET /api/credentials/vault-secrets`.
  - **Privileged-use evidence**: the template _IBM Verify Privilege Vault – Privileged use_ (dataset `pv.privileged-use`) and a section _privileged-use/_ in evidence packs of the vault platform – views, checkouts, password displays, edits and access requests of the privileged secrets in the period, from the vault's audit.
  - The template bundle **Privileged access governance**, a simulated vault for the demo and the tests, and the demo platform _IBM Verify Privilege Vault (demo)_.
- **Token request sign-in with a refresh token**: a platform with a custom login (`token_request`) can name a refresh call (`auth.refresh`); an expired token is renewed with it before Steward signs in again ([docs/PLATFORM-SIGN-IN.md](docs/PLATFORM-SIGN-IN.md)).
- **Paging that follows `hasNext`**: offset and page pagination of HTTP steps take `morePath`, an expression that says whether another page follows – for APIs that return fewer items than asked for before the end.

### Changed

- **Requester ≠ approver by identity**: four-eyes checks treat two accounts linked to the same single sign-on identity (issuer and `sub`, e.g. through two providers of one identity provider) as one person, like the former names of a moved account. Person reviews and MCP requests now use the same identity check (they compared user names only).
- **The template variable `baseUrl` in sign-in settings no longer ends with a slash**, so `{{ baseUrl }}/.default` (the Microsoft Graph scope) stays one URL. No built-in template used it; a platform's own sign-in settings that add a slash after `{{ baseUrl }}` get one slash fewer.

### Fixed

- **Open menus are accessible.** Every dropdown menu (the _More_ menus of platforms, processes, dashboards and monitors, the user menu, the Help menu) began with an invisible focusable element that is not a menu item, so screen readers got an invalid menu (axe `aria-required-children`, critical). Opening a menu now focuses its first item, as the WAI-ARIA menu button pattern describes; the arrow keys, Escape and the return of the focus work as before. Red menu items (_Delete platform_, _Archive_, _Sign out_) were too light to read in both colour schemes (3.3:1 on white, 4.1:1 in dark mode) and now reach 4.5:1, also when hovered. `e2e/accessibility.spec.ts` checks open menus in light and dark mode.
- **Closed and withdrawn documents are readable in dark mode**: their status badge used a near-black colour (2.8:1 on the dark background); it is grey now, like _Cancelled_.

## [0.4.0] - 2026-11-04

Steward 0.4.0 is robust to run and easy to start. Background work runs as visible jobs on _System status_; a stop on SIGTERM lets running steps finish (`SM_SHUTDOWN_GRACE_SECONDS`, default 25 s); on PostgreSQL a lease keeps one instance active and a second one waits instead of exiting; schedule times missed while Steward was down are reported; at most `SM_MAX_CONCURRENT_RUNS` runs (default 20) run at once. The container runs the compiled server (`node server/dist/index.js`), every environment variable is checked at start-up and documented from one schema (docs/CONFIGURATION.md), and the internal API answers errors as problem details. The audit log is verified incrementally from the newest signed checkpoint and can be archived in signed monthly epochs with retention; datasets are stored once. The navigation has six areas with pinned entries, the service page is a hub with tabs, and lists and detail pages share one layout. _Duplicate_ works the same way for eleven kinds of items. Template bundles, a platform setup wizard, platform health with an optional periodic test, Kafka topics by prefix and Confluent Cloud access reviews make a new installation easier to start. Eight database migrations, all additive: `runtime-001-schedule-missed-fires`, `runtime-002-background-jobs`, `audit-001-archives`, `ops-014-dataset-rows-in-parts`, `platforms-003-health`, `catalogue-011-topic-prefixes`, `duplicates-001-copied-from`, `duplicates-002-maintenance-enabled`. **Check before updating:** configuration values that used to be ignored now stop the start (the log names each one); container tools are `node server/dist/<tool>.js` instead of `node --import tsx server/src/cli/<tool>.ts`; `/api/v1/audit/verify` is incremental (pass `full=true` for the whole log); more than 20 runs at a time now queue (`SM_MAX_CONCURRENT_RUNS=0` for no limit); keep `stop_grace_period` at 30 s or more; on PostgreSQL 0.3 and 0.4 instances exclude each other; internal `/api` errors are `application/problem+json`; _Service management_ is now _Catalogue_ and _Administration_ is _Admin_.

### Added

- **Navigation in six areas**: Home, Operate (runs, monitors, dashboards, status pages, reports, my tasks, AI requests), Automate (processes, with schedules, triggers and Git sync as tabs), Catalogue (services, applications, systems, platforms, credentials, documents, ticket references, duties, scorecard, maintenance), Compliance and Admin (users and teams, audit log, settings, system status). The area of the current page opens, the others fold; the phone drawer has the same areas. Every address of 0.3 still works, and `/operate`, `/automate`, `/catalogue`, `/compliance` and `/admin` lead to an area's first page.
- **Pinned entries**: the pin beside a sidebar entry puts it at the top, under _Pinned_ (per user, in this browser).
- **The service page is a hub**: tabs for the overview, applications, the systems it runs on (from its applications), duties, objectives, maintenance, documents, tickets and the scorecard, each with its own address (`/services/<id>/duties` …).
- **Command palette** (Ctrl/⌘ K): _Test connection to …_ for each platform the user may use, _Open failing checks_, _New person review for …_ (designers and administrators), applications and systems, the last ten entries opened (per user, in this browser) and the keyboard shortcut sheet.
- **Same page layout everywhere**: lists have the title with the main action, folding filters on phones, a table/card switch where the list has cards, and an empty state that says who may add the first entry; detail pages share one header (title, status, actions).
