Every dataset metric gets a robust **trend line**: KPIs show the change of the trend and when a limit is reached at this rate, checks use `$trend` and `$daysUntil`, and the daily summary lists limits reached soon. **CheckMk** and **Prometheus** become monitor check types – the current state of a host or service, a PromQL query against WARN and CRIT levels with the worst series named – and availability objectives can take their figures from PromQL. **Kubernetes** namespaces of applications are read with a read-only service account: deployments, the images as tech stack entries for the end-of-life checks, pods that are not well and cert-manager certificates, plus a monitor check. Monitors post **Teams cards** to their own channels, and systems get their own end-of-life messages. Three database migrations, all additive: `ops-008-metric-limits`, `ops-009-prom-objective-readings` and `catalogue-006-namespace-cluster`.

### Added

- **Trends and forecasts on metrics** (#145): a robust trend line (Theil–Sen, counter resets followed, too few points said plainly, stable or rough) for every dataset metric. KPIs show the change of the trend line and what it is based on, and when a limit is reached at this rate; trend widgets can draw dashed trend lines. The publish step takes `limits` and `floors` per metric, a KPI its own limit. Checks use `$trend('dataset/metric', '7d')` and `$daysUntil('dataset/metric')`; the daily summary gets a _Trends_ section (limits within 30 days, strong changes); `GET /api/v1/datasets/{key}/metrics?trend=7d` returns `trend`, `limit` and `forecast`. Migration `ops-008-metric-limits` (additive).
- **CheckMk** (#130): monitor check type `checkmk` – the current state of a host or service (OK / WARN / CRIT / UNKNOWN → up / degraded / down / unknown, the plugin output as message), one shared read per site and cycle. Platform type _CheckMk (monitoring)_ with an automation user and a connection test, a demo site, and the example process _CheckMk – trend summary_ with the dashboard _CheckMk trends_: averages, peaks, time above WARN/CRIT, week-on-week change and days until CRIT, checks for levels crossed and series heading for CRIT, a morning report. Alerting on raw metrics stays in CheckMk ([docs/CHECKMK.md](docs/CHECKMK.md)).
- **Prometheus** (#110): monitor check type `prometheus` – a PromQL query against WARN and CRIT levels (above or below), every series judged and the worst one named, no data unknown by default – and a platform type _Prometheus (PromQL)_ for Prometheus, Thanos, Mimir and VictoriaMetrics with a connection test and a demo. Availability objectives can take their figures from a PromQL query with `$window` (read hourly per window; with several series the lowest counts). Migration `ops-009-prom-objective-readings` (additive).
- **Kubernetes** (#111): a platform type _Kubernetes cluster (read-only)_ with a read-only service account (role in [docs/KUBERNETES.md](docs/KUBERNETES.md)). Application components of type _Kubernetes namespace_ name their cluster; Steward reads their deployments with ready replicas and images, pods that are not well and cert-manager certificates every 6 hours or on _Read now_. Images become tech stack entries (source _read from the cluster_; a built-in image → product list that administrators extend with their own images; unmapped images are listed, not guessed), so end-of-life checks cover them. Monitor check type `kubernetes` (deployments ready, no pod crash-looping), the example _Kubernetes – namespace health_ with the dashboard _Kubernetes_, and a demo cluster. Migration `catalogue-006-namespace-cluster` (additive).
- **End of life for systems** (#129 follow-up): the people responsible for a system get the end-of-life messages of its tech stack, also for systems no application runs on yet (audited as `system.eol_notice_sent`). The example _End of life – tech stack overview_ and its dashboard now list systems too – per application and environment, and systems no application uses – so applications whose stack is only on their systems show up. New expression function `$systems()`.
- **Teams cards for webhooks** (#130): monitor webhook outputs and the notification webhook take the format _Teams card_ (an Adaptive Card in the configured look). The new platform type _Microsoft Teams channel (Workflows)_ keeps the Workflows URL as an encrypted credential (a Teams card always goes to such a platform, never to a URL in the monitor definition), so each monitor or team posts to its own channel.

## [0.2.5] - 2026-10-30

The service catalogue learns what applications run on: **systems** with their own tech stack, per environment, and an ordered list of environments. Credentials can belong to applications, components and systems, owners are picked from the users and teams, and tags take a value (`env=prod`) and filter lists. **Maintenance windows** – once or repeating – keep affected monitors quiet, can pause run messages, hold back objective breach messages and tell the owners in advance, with a calendar and .ics files. **Incident reviews** record what happened with a timeline prefilled from what Steward saw, and track their measures with owners, due dates and reminders. Four database migrations, all additive: `catalogue-004-systems`, `credentials-003-belongs-to`, `catalogue-005-maintenance` and `records-005-measures`.

### Added

- **Systems** (#129): servers, VMs, clusters, appliances and cloud services that applications run on, kept once under _Service management → Systems_ – with vendor or provider, CMDB link, owners, note, their own tech stack (by hand or from an SBOM) and optionally the Steward platform they are. Applications **run on** systems per environment; the application page groups them by environment, the system page lists its applications. The application's tech stack, the end-of-life messages (production first, `db01 (prod)`) and the scorecard part _Tech stack_ include systems. API v1: `/systems`, `/systems/{systemId}`, `/environments`.
- **Environments** (#129): _Settings → Environments_ keeps an ordered list (name, colour, production), offered on platforms, "runs on" links and components; other values stay possible and values in use can be adopted. Components get an optional environment (empty: all environments) and the components table filters by it. The process input types _Application_ and _Applications_ return `systems` and `environment` on each component.
- **Credentials that belong to the catalogue** (#143): an entry of the credentials register belongs to an application, a component or a system – the platform stays optional, so applications without an API in Steward document their technical users, keys and certificates too. New entries take the owners of what they belong to; the application page lists the entries of the application and its components, the system page those of the system. Filter `belongs` in the list and in API v1 (`belongsTo` on every entry), CSV column `belongs_to`.
- **Owner picker** (#143): owners of services, applications, systems and duties are picked from the users and teams (with avatars), like the owners of credentials and the responsibles of tickets already were. The server refuses users and teams that do not exist (`USER_UNKNOWN`, `TEAM_UNKNOWN`); owners stored before stay valid. A renamed team stays the owner, responsible, recipient and access-list entry everywhere; a deleted team leaves owner, responsible and recipient lists, while access lists keep the entry so that access never widens. Before a team is deleted or an account deactivated, the Users page lists where it is still named (`GET /api/users/{id}/references`, `/api/teams/{id}/references`).
- **Tags with a value** (#143): tags are `key` or `key=value` (shown as `env: prod`) on services, applications, processes, monitors, records and credentials. The tag input takes a key, Tab moves to the value, Enter adds it; keys and values are suggested from the tags in use. Clicking a tag filters the list (`?tag=env=prod`, or `?tag=env` for every value); the public API filters the same way and now also on services, applications and credentials. Existing tags stay as keys.
- **Maintenance windows** (#108): plan work on platforms, systems, applications and services under _Service management → Maintenance_ (list and month calendar) – once, or weekly or monthly with an optional end, in the instance's time zone. While a window lasts, monitors that check an affected platform show _maintenance_ and send no alerts, check and run messages of runs on those platforms can go to the app only, and availability objectives of affected services record breaches without a message. Objectives show the planned minutes per window next to Gatus' figures, which stay unchanged. The responsible people and the owners of what it reaches are told when it is planned, before each occurrence, when it starts and when it ends (event `maintenance.announced`). Each window offers a calendar file (.ics) and can name its change record; platform, system, application and service pages show the maintenance going on and coming up. API v1: `/maintenance-windows`.
- **Incident reviews** (#109): a built-in record type (`PIR`) for post-incident reviews on the decision workflow – incident window, severity, impact and cause as fields, summary, timeline and learnings in the text. Start one from a resolved ticket reference (its time, title and services), from a monitor outage, or on its own. _Fill in the timeline_ offers what Steward saw in the window – monitor changes, failed runs, ticket events and, for administrators and auditors, audit entries, only what the author may see – and copies the ticked entries into the text. **Measures** with owners and due dates are tracked like duties: reminders before and on the due date and weekly while overdue (event `measure.due`), open measures on the service page, still editable after the review was accepted. API v1: `/measures`.
- Migration `credentials-003-belongs-to` (additive: columns `credentials.belongs_kind`, `belongs_id`).
- Migration `catalogue-004-systems` (additive: tables `systems`, `system_stack`, `application_systems`, column `application_components.environment`).
- Migration `records-005-measures` (additive: tables `record_measures`, `record_measure_reminders`).
- Migration `catalogue-005-maintenance` (additive: tables `maintenance_windows`, `maintenance_targets`, `maintenance_notices`).

### Changed

- The record type of the Git sync example is now `RCA` (_Root cause analysis_): `PIR` is the key of the built-in incident review type. An installation that already has a type `PIR` (synced from Git or created by hand) keeps it; the built-in type then gets the key `IR`.

## [0.2.4] - 2026-10-29

Automated runs get one notification policy – schedules, webhook triggers and monitor outputs decide when and where failed checks notify, what counts as a change, and can pause their messages – and the messages say what is wrong, which way it changed and what to do. A dataset keeps one partition per source platform, so the same process run against several tenants no longer overwrites the others, and dashboards filter by source. The monitor editor keeps cards apart and validates while you type, the run dialog offers one run mode, failed checks on the start page are grouped, and the tenant usage example reads the real Usage Transparency API. Four database migrations, all additive: `ops-005-run-retention-default`, `ops-006-notify-policy`, `triggers-002-notify-policy` and `ops-007-dataset-sources`.

### Added

- **Datasets per source platform** (#142): a dataset keeps one partition per source – the platform its rows were read from (the only connection, the publish step's new `source` connection, or all bound platforms; the instance without connections). A run replaces only its own partition, so the same process scheduled against several tenants no longer overwrites the others; rows carry `_source`, metrics are kept per source. Dashboards get a _Sources_ filter (in the URL, remembered per person) that every widget follows or pins, a _Source_ column in tables, one line per source in trends, a value per source in KPIs and freshness per source. A partition whose platform has an access list without the reader is hidden (administrators and auditors see all). Staleness follows the schedule: 2.5 × its interval (at least 15 minutes) unless the step sets `staleAfterMinutes`, else 24 hours. `GET /api/v1/datasets/{key}/rows|metrics` take `source`; `sources` in dataset metadata. Migration `ops-007-dataset-sources`; existing datasets become one partition of their last run's platform, with their metric history.
- **One notification policy for every source that repeats runs** (#139): schedules, webhook triggers and monitor outputs that start a process decide _when_ failed checks notify (on change, after every run, never; reminder; passes again), _what counts as a change_ (a different message or severity, or only the start and end of failing) and _where_ the messages go (as configured, or only chosen channels – a channel the instance turned off stays off). **Pause messages** until a time or until resumed, with a reason and optionally still in the app; a badge shows it, the pause ends on its own, and a check that still fails then sends one _Still failing after the pause_. Sub-processes follow the source of their parent (before, they notified every failure). Runs, checks, the start page and summaries are never affected. `POST /api/schedules|triggers/{id}/pause` and `/resume`, audited as `*.paused` / `*.resumed`; `notify` in the API (`/api/v1` too) and in monitor process outputs. Migrations `ops-006-notify-policy` (columns, the failing-check state per source in `notify_alerts`, moved from `check_alerts`) and `triggers-002-notify-policy` (columns); webhook triggers and monitor outputs keep _after every run_.
- **Messages say what is wrong, which way it changed and what to do** (#144): every message about a check has the state first, then where (_Warning · 11 of 58 endpoints down – Status_), one line on what happened (_Got better: 12 → 11 · failing since …_, _Still failing (reminder)_, _Passes again_), a small table, the items, _Open runbook_ (only when set), and why the reader got it with a link to the source's message settings – the same in e-mail, Teams, the app and the webhook data (new fields `headline`, `value`, `previousValue`, `direction`, `items`, `runbook`, `source`). Check steps take optional `headline`, `value` (with `betterWhen`), `items` and `runbook`; processes a `runbook` (a living document or a URL). The Gatus overviews, the IH health check, the data freshness monitor and the tenant usage check use them.
- **Monitor editor: Duplicate, Tidy up, Undo/Redo, Delete key and Check now before saving** (#141): _Check now_ on a card runs it as it is in the editor (`POST /api/monitors/check-preview`, nothing stored, with the owner's rights and the outbound policy, audited as `monitor.check_previewed`). A new check is placed below the selected card and depends on it; a hint explains linking until the first link exists.
- **Runs list: only runs with failed checks** (#138): new filter _Checks → With failed checks_ (`checks=failed` on `GET /api/runs`), linked from the start page.
- **Issue and idea forms** (#140): _About → Report an issue or idea_ links to [ISSUES.md](ISSUES.md), which says what helps and what never to include (credentials, personal data, internal details); GitHub issues use forms for bugs and ideas, blank issues are off and security reports go to the private advisory. Releases use a pull request template with the release checklist and an OWASP ASVS level 2 self-review.

### Changed

- **One choice of run mode** (#137): the run dialog offers _Dry run_ (default), _Test run_ and _Run_ instead of two nested switches, with the description of the chosen mode below it and the start button named after it. _Test run_ shows how many recorded responses each bound platform has, or links to where they are added. Schedules and webhook triggers choose between _Dry run_ and _Run_ the same way.
- **Start page: failed checks grouped and collapsible** (#138): one entry per process and check instead of one per run, with the latest message, since when it has been failing and in how many runs, and links to the latest run and to the runs with failed checks. Most severe first; checks that pass again are hidden behind _Show … that pass again_; at most 10 before _Show all_; long messages are cut to two lines. The header folds the list and keeps the counts; the choice is remembered per user in the browser. `failedChecks` in `GET /api/dashboard` has the grouped entries. Informational checks (`notify: false`) are left out, as documented – before, they were listed.
- **Runs are kept for 400 days on new installations** (#140): the default run retention was _forever_. Existing installations and imported migration packages without the setting keep _forever_ (migration `ops-005-run-retention-default` writes it), so nothing is deleted by an upgrade; _System status_ suggests a period once the database is larger than 1 GB.
- **The footer is on the About page only** (#140), with the full details there; the sign-in page keeps a short one.

### Fixed

- **Outbound allow list**: lines could not be added in _Settings → Outbound_ because the field dropped empty lines while typing. Each entry is now checked as you type (origin without a path, host or `*.host` with an optional port, IPv4 CIDR) and saving is refused with the reason; the server checks the same.
- **Monitor editor: a second card with the same id disappeared** (#141): cards were keyed by their check id, so the new card vanished and edits landed on the other one. Cards now keep their own identity, and a duplicate id is shown on the field. The editor validates with the server's rules while you type, marks the required fields per check type and mode (e.g. the variables of an Insights Hub freshness check), shows problems on the fields and lists them on save with a link to each; the server names the card (`Check "Name" (id) – field`) instead of `checks.1` and returns the paths as `issues`.
- **Tenant usage example** (#140): the example called a Usage Transparency path that does not exist (HTTP 500) and came with the limits of the demo tenant, so real tenants saw figures such as 308 %. It now counts users, assets, agents and **asset attributes per asset type** (new dataset `ih.usage-attributes`), compares them only with the limits you enter (default: count only), and can read one KPI with `aggregatedMonthlyConsumerUsage` of the Usage Transparency API 3.2.0. A figure that cannot be read is shown as _unknown_ and fails the new check _All figures were read_; the failed step keeps the platform's answer. Dashboard _Tenant usage_ and [docs/INSIGHTS-HUB-USAGE.md](docs/INSIGHTS-HUB-USAGE.md) updated.
- **Collapsed sidebar**: the icons sat off-centre.
- **Button colours**: two buttons used a fixed blue instead of the accent colour; a lint rule now allows only the semantic colours on buttons and switches.

## [0.2.3] - 2026-10-28

Applications list their components with a tech stack – entered by hand or imported from an SBOM – and Steward reads the end of life of every version from endoflife.date: coloured badges, messages to the owners and an optional scorecard part. Platforms keep recorded responses, so processes and monitors can be tried in test runs without calling the platform. Failed checks of scheduled runs notify on change instead of after every run, credentials document the applications that use them and the roles they hold, and two new integrations show how to build Streamlit apps and Grafana dashboards on Steward data. Seven database migrations, all additive: `catalogue-002-components`, `catalogue-003-lifecycle`, `ops-004-check-notifications`, `credentials-002-roles`, `scorecard-003-lifecycle`, `platforms-002-recordings` and `engine-003-test-runs`.

### Added

- **Components and tech stack of applications** (#103): an application lists what it consists of – repositories, container images, Kafka topics, Snowflake databases, cloud accounts, Kubernetes namespaces, platforms, other – with only a type and a name required. Each component has a tech stack of [endoflife.date](https://endoflife.date) products and versions (the list ships with Steward), entered by hand or imported from a CycloneDX or SPDX SBOM (file or URL): only products endoflife.date knows are kept, libraries stay out, the import shows its changes first, and entries entered by hand win. The application page shows the stack of all components together. Permissions follow the application; audited as `application.component_added` / `_changed` / `_removed` and `application.sbom_imported`; in the public API (`components`, `stack`, `componentCount`) and in migration packages. Migration `catalogue-002-components` adds two tables.
- **Failed checks of scheduled runs notify on change** (#127): a scheduled process that runs every few minutes no longer creates a message per run while the same check keeps failing. Each schedule has _Failed checks notify_: **on change** (default – when a check starts failing, when its result changes, and a reminder while it keeps failing, every 4 hours by default), **after every run** (as before) or **never**, and optionally a **Check passes again** message (new event `check.resolved`). In the app, by e-mail, Teams and webhook alike; the webhook carries `phase` and `since`. Check steps take `notify: false` for informational checks; three examples use it. Runs without a schedule notify as before. Migration `ops-004-check-notifications` adds the schedule settings (existing schedules: on change) and the table of failing checks. Docs: when to use a monitor and when a process check.
- **Credentials: applications, external links, roles and permissions** (#125): _Used by_ can name applications (the application page lists its credentials with status and next due date) and external pages with a label (http(s) only). An optional list of **roles and permissions** – role, scope, granted on, note – documents what a credential may do; changing it writes its own history event (_Roles changed_, with an optional reason or ticket) and audit entry `credential.roles_changed`, without a rotation. The overview shows the roles and filters by them (`role` also in `GET /api/v1/credentials`). Migration `credentials-002-roles` adds one column.
- **End of life of the tech stack** (#104): Steward reads the release cycles of every product in a tech stack once a day from [endoflife.date](https://endoflife.date) – a new platform preset with its API description – and colours the stack badges: supported, ends soon (within 90 days by default), end of life, no data, with cycle, LTS, date and latest version in the tooltip. _Applications → End of life_ shows the state of the data and _Refresh now_; administrators choose the platform, the warning period, whether the **application owners get a message** (new event `stack.eol`, once when a cycle starts ending and once at its end) and whether the scorecard counts a new part **Tech stack** (off by default). Stack entries carry `lifecycle` in the API and in process inputs. New process input type **Applications** (several, empty: all), the example process _End of life – tech stack overview_ and the dashboard _End of life_. Migrations `catalogue-003-lifecycle` (three tables) and `scorecard-003-lifecycle` (one column); `scripts/endoflife-products.mjs --repo` also refreshes the API description.
- **Recorded platform responses and test runs** (#105): _Platforms → (platform) → Recorded responses_ keeps answers per operation (method, path with `{placeholders}`, optional query parameters and parts of a JSON body to match), recorded from the API explorer with one click or entered by hand. A **test run** of a process (_Use recorded responses_ in the run dialog, `recorded: true` on `POST /api/runs`) is a dry run whose platform calls are answered by the recordings – nothing is sent, a call without one fails its step and is named, writes with a recording get its answer; runs are marked _Test run_ (`recorded` in the public API). _Test with recorded responses_ tries a monitor's platform checks the same way without storing anything. Never used by real runs or dry runs. Reading needs `use` on the platform, changing `manage`; credentials are redacted and credential headers dropped; 500 per platform, 1 MB each; audited; in migration packages. Migrations `platforms-002-recordings` (one table) and `engine-003-test-runs` (one column).
- **Process input type _Application_** (#103): the run gets the application with its components and tech stack (e.g. `inputs.app.components[type="kafka-topic"].name`), given by id or name – a name works in every installation, e.g. as a default in Git.
- **Examples: Streamlit and Grafana on Steward data** (#106): `integrations/streamlit/` is a small Streamlit app on the read-only API – uptime from the metric history of `gatus-uptime`/`gatus-uptime-metrics`, the privileged access review (`ih.privileged`, `ih.access-snapshot`) and a place to join other data sources – with a cached client (paging, timeouts, clear errors for 401/403/404), pinned dependencies, a non-root Dockerfile, a compose snippet behind the same proxy under a path, and notes on Streamlit's OIDC sign-in. `integrations/grafana/` provisions the Infinity data source (token in `secureJsonData`, limited to `…/api/v1/datasets`) and two dashboards, _Steward – Uptime_ and _Steward – Datasets_. [docs/EXTERNAL-DASHBOARDS.md](docs/EXTERNAL-DASHBOARDS.md) explains when to use them instead of Steward's dashboards, the API calls, what a token can see and the security notes.

## [0.2.2] - 2026-10-28

Notifications get an own logo and more styling, and messages from dry runs – failed runs, failed checks and their tasks – are clearly marked as tests (or kept in the app only). Platforms can fetch a new access token on demand, API specifications can be copied to other platforms of the same kind, and importing a monitor (or accepting it from Git) lets you choose the platform when its name fits several. Git sync no longer creates a copy of an item that was created after the fetch. No database migrations.

### Added

- **Copy API specifications to other platforms of the same kind** – _Platforms → (platform) → API specs → Copy to other platforms_ (`POST /api/platforms/{id}/specs/{specId}/copy`). Each target gets an independent copy (platforms of one kind can run different API versions), with the path prefix unless the target's base URL already ends with it. A spec with the same title and version is skipped, or replaced under its id with _Replace_. Needs `manage` on the source and all targets; audited with `copiedFrom`.
- **Choose the platform when importing a monitor** (#102): monitor files name their platform (`platform: Insights Hub`). When that name fits several of your platforms ("Insights Hub (modev)", "Insights Hub (prod)") or none, the import dialog – for examples, pasted YAML and templates – shows a platform picker per reference with the platforms of the right kind you may use, instead of failing with _Platform "Insights Hub" not found_. Git sync stages such monitors instead of marking them invalid, and the reviewer picks the platform when accepting. The API takes `platforms` (name → platform id) on the import and accept routes; `POST /api/monitors/import/preview` and `/api/monitors/examples/{file}/preview` list the references. A choice of another kind or a platform you may not use is refused.
- **Get a new access token on demand** (#101): platforms with an OAuth2 or custom login cache their token until shortly before it expires, so roles granted to the technical user in the meantime only worked after up to 30 minutes (the platform answered 403). _Get a new token_ (platform → … menu, and next to the token age in the connection test) drops the cached token and logs in again right away; `POST /api/platforms/{id}/token/renew` does the same. Needs `manage` on the platform, audited as `platform.token_renewed`; the token itself is never shown or logged. _Test connection_ now shows when the cached token was issued and when it expires.

### Fixed

- **Git sync no longer copies an item created after the fetch** – a file staged as _new_ whose process, dashboard or monitor was created in Steward afterwards (e.g. from the same built-in template) became a second item (`…-2`) when accepted. Accepting now changes the existing item (shown as a conflict, since it never came from Git), and the next fetch drops the _new_ update when the item is identical.
- **Monitor templates in the example list** showed their parameter placeholder as the name ("{{ params.title }} (template)"); they are listed by a readable name now.
- **An own logo and more style for e-mails and Teams messages** (#99): _Logo → Own logo_ takes a PNG or JPEG up to 512 KB just for messages (`POST`/`DELETE /api/settings/notifications/look/logo`, audited as `notifications.logo_uploaded` / `notifications.logo_removed`, moves with migration packages). New style settings: logo size and position, header (coloured, white with a line, minimal), button colour, corners and font (system, Arial, serif). The defaults keep the look of 0.2.1; Teams cards take only the logo's size and position.
- **Dry runs: where their notifications go** – _Dry runs_ in the same card: e-mail, Teams and webhook marked as a test (default), or only in the app.

### Changed

- **Notifications about dry runs no longer look urgent** (#99): the subject and the in-app title start with _Dry run –_ (German _Probelauf –_) instead of ending in "(dry run)", e-mails get a grey banner _Dry run – a test, nothing was changed. No action needed._ and a grey strip instead of the red one, and Teams cards the same sentence in a grey card. The same applies to task messages of dry runs (a decision, a step to verify, a step sent back); the tasks themselves stay normal tasks. Webhooks keep `data.dryRun: true` (task messages now carry it too). The look preview can show a failed dry run.

## [0.2.1] - 2026-10-27

Fixes from running 0.2.0 in a real setup – API specs keep their server URL path, access lists accept picked users, teams and roles, the Insights Hub and Industrial Edge examples handle epoch and empty timestamps, the container health check works under a path prefix – plus Git sync that follows tags with Steward's own example library as a ready (disabled) source, and branded e-mails and Teams messages. Upgrading from 0.2.0 adds only columns and settings; no data is rewritten.

### Added

- **Branded e-mails and Teams messages** (#96): _Settings → Look of e-mails and Teams messages_ sets name, colour, logo (app logo, report logo or none) and a footer text; empty fields follow _Settings → Appearance_. E-mails – notifications, the daily summary and test messages – get one layout with a header in the colour, the logo attached inline (`cid:`, never loaded from Steward), a strip by severity, a button and the footer, and the subject prefix `[name]`. Teams cards show the name, a small embedded logo (up to 12 KB, cards may be at most 28 KB), the footer and a strip in the Adaptive Card colour of the severity. A preview shows both with unsaved settings (`POST /api/settings/notifications/look/preview`); changes are audited as `notifications.look_updated` and move with migration packages. Generic webhooks are unchanged; nothing to migrate.
- **Gatus uptime overview from `/metrics`** – process _Gatus – uptime overview (from /metrics)_ (74) and dashboard _Uptime (from /metrics)_ for a Gatus whose API is behind OIDC: it reads only `/metrics` and Gatus' open uptime and response-time API, needs no Gatus login, and adds TLS certificate expiry (dataset `gatus-uptime-metrics`). The demo feeds it every 10 minutes.

- **Git sync follows tags** as well as branches (_Follow: Branch / Tag_; Forgejo/Gitea, GitHub, GitLab; annotated tags are followed to their commit, a moved tag is staged like a new commit, signed-commit checks and webhooks work for tags). A branch or tag name can contain **`{version}`**, this installation's Steward version, resolved at every fetch: `v{version}` follows the release you run and moves along after an upgrade. Migration `gitsync-003-refs-min-version` adds the columns; existing sources keep following their branch.
- **Steward's example library as a ready Git sync source** – _Steward example library_ (GitHub `vxrz/Steward`, tag `v{version}`, folder `examples`; processes, dashboards, monitors). Added **disabled**, once per installation (new ones and the upgrade to 0.2.1, after the vault is unlocked; audit entry `sync.source_created` by `system`), without a token, schedule or webhook; changes to it stay and a deleted one is not added again. Turn it on to review improved examples of your version in _Updates from Git_; it needs `api.github.com` in the outbound policy.
- **Minimum Steward version per file** – a YAML file can start with `# steward-min-version: 0.2.1`. Git sync lists files that need a newer Steward as invalid (_Needs Steward 0.2.1 or newer_) instead of staging them, and stages them after the upgrade. For every importer the line is a comment, so the same file still imports into 0.2.0.

### Fixed

- **Platform connection test and login errors say which request failed**: _Test connection_ shows the request it made (`GET /openness/… answered HTTP 401`), and without a test path it says that the base URL itself was asked – often a portal with its own login, whose 401 looked like a failed API login. A failed custom login or OAuth2 token request names its method and address (`Login request POST https://gateway:9443/openness/iam/api/v1/token failed with HTTP 401`), never its query.
- **Adding a user, team or role to an access list** (platforms, processes, monitors): picking an entry from the list put its label into the field ("Team Service desk"), and _Add_ stayed disabled because it expected `team:…`. Picked entries are added now; typing `user:…`, `team:…` or `role:…` still works.
- **API specs keep the path of their server URL** (e.g. `/api/identitymanagement/v3` in the Insights Hub Identity Management spec): operations used only the spec's paths, so _Pick from API spec_ built `https://gateway.eu1.mindsphere.io/Groups` instead of `…/api/identitymanagement/v3/Groups`; Industrial Edge specs had the same problem. On import, the path of the server URL (OpenAPI 3 `servers[0].url` with the defaults of its variables, or Swagger 2 `basePath`) becomes the spec's **path prefix**, unless the platform's base URL already ends with it; the prefix can be edited per spec in the list of API specs (`PATCH /api/platforms/{id}/specs/{specId}`, audited as `platform.spec_updated`), which also shows where requests go. The editor's operation picker, the API explorer, the contract check and the MCP gateway use the prefixed path; MCP references in the old form (`GET /Groups`) still work. Specs imported earlier get their prefix from the stored server URL when read (migration `platforms-001-spec-path-prefix` adds the column). **HTTP steps already saved with the short path must be corrected by hand: open the step and pick the operation again.** The demo's Identity Management spec now has the same shape as the real one.
- **Insights Hub and Industrial Edge examples with last login or last seen**: tenants send `lastLogonTime` as epoch milliseconds, and `null` for people who never signed in. The examples failed with _Argument 3 of function "substring" does not match function signature_, and `$exists()` counted `null` as signed in. The access reviews (10–12), group hygiene (14), inventory snapshot (15) and the device examples (50, 51, 53) now read the time with `$ts()` and show it with `$fromMillis()`, so ISO dates, numbers, `null` and a missing attribute all work. The demo's mock platform sends all of these, so the example tests cover them. Processes imported from 0.2.0 examples keep the old expressions: import the fixed files again or paste them into the process's _Source_ tab.
- **The container health check under a path**: the image's `HEALTHCHECK` always asked `http://127.0.0.1:4310/api/health`, so a container under `SM_PUBLIC_URL=…/steward` was reported _unhealthy_ (and the log warned about requests outside the base path); with `SM_PORT` or `SM_TLS_CERT` it failed too. It now uses the server's own base path, port and TLS settings (`server/src/cli/healthcheck.ts`); with Steward's own TLS it accepts only the certificate in `SM_TLS_CERT`.

### Changed

- `docs/INSTALL.md`:
  - **the passphrase file in Docker**: owned by the container user (uid 1000), with user namespaces (`userns-remap`) and SELinux; the comments in both compose files said "a file only root can read", which Steward cannot open;
  - **Steward in Docker**: which address `SM_TRUST_PROXY` must name when the proxy runs on the host or in another container, with a Caddy example on a shared network;
  - **company proxy and TLS inspection**: `HTTPS_PROXY`/`NO_PROXY`, `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS` and the per-platform settings;
  - **health checks under a path**, and why a monitor can get 401.
- `docs/GATUS.md`: Gatus OIDC cannot be combined with an API login – keep OIDC with metrics mode, or use `security.basic` (now with how to make the `password-bcrypt-base64` value); reading Gatus over the Docker network.

## [0.2.0] - 2026-10-26

A service catalogue with applications, CIA ratings and derived protection needs, service duties, and a service scorecard with availability objectives per Gatus endpoint; manual steps with checklists, optional evidence and second-person verification; single sign-on only, with vendor-neutral claim mapping and emergency access from the command line; step-up re-authentication for sensitive actions; migration packages to move an instance; and a signed container image on the GitHub Container Registry. **If you use single sign-on, read the upgrade note on `email_verified` under _Changed_ before updating.**

### Added

- **Availability objectives and the service scorecard** (#86): an availability objective names one Gatus endpoint and targets for the windows Gatus reports – 1 h, 24 h, 7 days, 30 days, each optional ("24 h ≥ 99 %, 30 days ≥ 99.5 %") – for a service or one of its applications (service page → _Availability objectives_). The values are Gatus' own uptime figures, which metrics mode reads hourly with _Also read Gatus' uptime figures_; no service hours, no extra call to Gatus. Each window is _met_, _below target_ or _no data_ (with the reason). Steward stores an hourly sample per objective (90 days); a breach starts with the first sample below target and ends with the first that meets every target, and objectives with _Send a message_ notify the service owners once per breach (new notification event `objective.breached`). The _scorecard_ (new page _Service management → Scorecard_, a section on the service page) shows per service four parts, each with its own status and no weighting: **duties** (from the stored duty periods: mandatory met in the last closed period and now; recommended separately; optional shown), **availability** (objectives), **documentation** (released documents, reviews current, and documents expected by administrator rules on the CIA rating, e.g. an authorization concept for confidentiality _high_ or above – the rule list starts empty) and **protection need** (rated, including unrated or re-rate members and an invalid scale); _attention needed_ when any part needs it. Rollup overall and per owner, a trend from daily snapshots that are never rewritten after their day (730 days), a start page tile _Services needing attention_, the report section _Service scorecard_, the public API (`/api/v1/scorecard`, `/api/v1/scorecard/trend`, `/api/v1/services/{serviceId}/scorecard`, `/api/v1/objectives`, `/api/v1/objectives/{objectiveId}/samples`) and migration packages; audited (`objective.*`, `scorecard.rule_*`). Migration `scorecard-001-objectives-and-snapshots` adds `availability_objectives`, `objective_samples`, `objective_breaches`, `scorecard_rules` and `scorecard_snapshots`. See [docs/SCORECARD.md](docs/SCORECARD.md).
- **Service duties** (#85): a service says which recurring tasks it must or should have done – an access review every quarter, a restore test every month, a firmware check every week. A duty (service page → _Duties_, optionally about one of the service's applications) names a process, an interval (weekly, monthly, quarterly, half-yearly, yearly: calendar periods in the server's time zone, daylight saving time included), a level (mandatory, recommended, optional), an optional grace period, reminder days, owners and optionally the schedule that runs it. Each period is judged by the real runs of exactly that process – never dry runs, cancelled runs or runs of other processes; runs waiting for an approval or a manual step count once they end – as _done_, _late_ (started in the grace period), _failed_ (only failed, interrupted or rejected runs), _missed_, _ended_ (archived or changed before anything counted), or _skipped_ by an exception ("skipped for this period, because …") that a second person approves (four-eyes; never the requester, former names included). A duty job closes ended periods and stores their result with what they were judged against (name, level, interval, grace, process name, time zone), so history never changes when the duty, the process or the runs change later; it sends a reminder before a period ends and a "missed" message once (new notification events `duty.due` and `duty.missed`). Duties are on the service and application pages, on a new _Service management → Duties_ page and duty pages with the periods and exceptions, in the start page tile _Duties due_, in the daily summary (section `duties`), in the scheduled report section _Service duties_, in the public API (`/api/v1/duties`, `/api/v1/duties/{dutyId}`, `/api/v1/duties/{dutyId}/periods`) and in migration packages; everything is audited (`duty.*`). Migration `duties-001-service-duties` adds `service_duties`, `duty_periods`, `duty_skips` and `duty_notifications`. See [docs/DUTIES.md](docs/DUTIES.md).
- **Manual steps and manual procedures** (#83): the User task node has a third mode, _manual_, for work people do outside Steward: Markdown instructions, a fixed checklist (optionally every item required), fields (text, number, date and time, link, ticket reference – linked when documented under _Tickets_; each off, optional or required, with a hint) and evidence files (off, optional by default, or required). Something required that is _not available_ is confirmed with a reason. **Second-person verification (maker–checker)** per step: someone other than the person who completed it – optionally not the run starter, optionally only listed users, teams or roles – verifies it before the run continues, or sends it back with a comment (or fails the run, `onReject: fail`). **After-the-fact entries** store the stated _done at_ time and the entry time, never in the future and not before the run unless the step allows up to 30 days. _Not available_ and after-the-fact entries are always verified. _Processes → New manual procedure_, an example (`85-manual-edge-device-replacement`), a _Manual steps_ section in the run protocol PDF (checklist ticks, fields, evidence with SHA-256 and thumbnails, who completed and verified it), audit entries `task.completed`, `task.backdated`, `task.evidence_added`, `task.verified`, `task.verification_rejected`, steps to verify on the start page, in _My tasks_, the daily summary and notifications, and `mode: manual` with a `manual` object in `/api/v1/tasks`. Existing approvals and selections are unchanged. See [docs/MANUAL-STEPS.md](docs/MANUAL-STEPS.md).
- **Applications in the service catalogue, CIA rating and protection needs** (#84): _Service management → Applications_ lists what services consist of – Insights Hub, Industrial Edge and smaller applications – with name, description, vendor, version, URL, responsible users and teams, criticality, tags, CMDB entry and links (both ways, like services). Steward platforms are mapped to an application as its integrations (a platform belongs to one application at most; the platform page names it). Services consist of applications with a role (primary, dependency or free text); the service page shows them with their integrations. Each application has an optional **CIA rating** – confidentiality, integrity, availability, each with a justification, who and when – on the BSI IT-Grundschutz scale normal / high / very high, or on an administrator's own scale (one for all dimensions or one per dimension; changing it maps the ratings of removed levels or flags them for re-rating, never deletes one, and asks for a step-up). A service **derives its protection need** per dimension with the maximum principle and can override it with a reason (cumulation or distribution effect). Living documents name the applications they **apply to**; the service page groups its documents by application, the application page lists them with their review status. All of it is audited, in the public API (`/api/v1/applications`, `/api/v1/cia-scale`, `applications` and `protectionNeed` on services), in evidence packs of services and platforms, and in migration packages. See [docs/APPLICATIONS.md](docs/APPLICATIONS.md).
- **Step-up re-authentication for sensitive actions** (#14, review proposal P1, OWASP ASVS 3.7.1): creating API and MCP tokens, changing the master passphrase, creating accounts with roles, changing roles, binding an account to a provider, (de)activating administrators, resetting passwords, adding, changing or deleting single sign-on providers (incl. claim mapping), changing the sign-in mode, the outbound policy or allow-list, turning the four-eyes rule for findings off, where notifications go (SMTP server, Teams and webhook URLs, base URL, allowed e-mail domains, emergency alert channels), adding team members, giving anyone a right in an access list (a new or raised entry, the role defaults of an emptied list, a new owner), changing the audit anchoring, exporting evidence packs, the audit log or the credential register and exporting or applying migration packages need a confirmation within `SM_STEP_UP_MINUTES` (default 5, 1–15) – for the session that made it only. Password sessions enter the password again (limited and counted like a sign-in); single sign-on sessions sign in again at their provider in a popup (`prompt=login`, `max_age=0`), accepted only for the same identity by its stable identifier with an `auth_time` within the window, and – per provider, _Require multi-factor authentication_ – with `mfa` in `amr`, a configured `acr` value or an Entra ID Conditional Access authentication context in `acrs`. A fresh sign-in counts as a confirmation; emergency sessions count as confirmed; API tokens never do these actions. The server checks a route flag (`config: { stepUp: { action, who, when } }`) before the handler – only for callers the route serves – and answers `403 STEP_UP_REQUIRED` with how to confirm and the action; the web app's dialog names the action(s), confirms and sends the request again once, and the single sign-on callback page says what is confirmed and when it started. New routes `GET/POST/DELETE /api/auth/step-up`, `POST /api/auth/step-up/sso`, `POST /api/auth/step-up/sso/complete` (signed-in sessions only); audit entries `auth.step_up` (method `password`, `sso`, `sso+mfa`), `auth.step_up_failed` and `auth.step_up_ended`. The mock identity provider honours `prompt=login`/`max_age`, `acr_values` and a claims request for `acrs` and sends `auth_time`, `amr`, `acr`, `acrs` (configurable per identity). Migration `signin-003-step-up` adds `step_up_at` and `step_up_method` to `sessions`. See [docs/SECURITY-HARDENING.md](docs/SECURITY-HARDENING.md#step-up-re-authentication) and [docs/SSO.md](docs/SSO.md#step-up-re-authentication).
- **Single sign-on claim mapping, presets and a test sign-in** (#77): vendor-neutral settings per provider for how claims become an account – the user name claim and a user name rule (lower case, lower case without `@domain`, as sent; collisions of the provider's accounts are reported with the provider), first and last name (`given_name`, `family_name`), the display name from a claim or a template like `{given_name} {family_name}`, read-only **profile fields** from (nested) claims such as `address.locality` (at most 20, values at most 500 characters; shown on the user dialog and the profile and in `/api/v1/users` as `firstName`, `lastName`, `profile`), a **sync rule** per field (at every sign-in and then not editable in Steward, or only when the account is created or linked), groups as names (ignoring case) or ids (exactly), required claim values (e.g. `hd`, `tid`) and the userinfo endpoint. The **stable identifier** is the issuer plus configurable claims (default `sub`; Entra ID preset `tid` + `oid`); links from before and links made with other claims are re-linked at the next sign-in through the recorded issuer + `sub` (`user.sso_relinked`). A **test sign-in** in the provider editor signs an administrator in once through the provider in a new window and shows the ID token and userinfo claims and a preview of the account with the unsaved settings of the form – user name, names, e-mail, roles, teams and which account it would sign in to, link, create or refuse and why – without a session or any account change (`POST /api/sso/providers/:id/test-sign-in`, `POST /api/sso/test-sign-ins/:id`, audited `sso.test_sign_in`). **Presets** for Microsoft Entra ID, Keycloak, Okta, Google Workspace, Authentik and generic OpenID Connect pre-fill issuer pattern, scopes, claims and groups claim and link to the provider-side steps; they are only pre-fill. The mock identity provider of the demo emits configurable claims (`PUT /mock/oidc/identities/:sub`, demo mode only) and has a userinfo endpoint. Migration `signin-002-sso-claims` adds `first_name`, `last_name`, `profile` and `external_sub` to `users`; provider settings are not migrated (they stay sealed) and settings a provider was saved without keep the behaviour of earlier versions. See [docs/SSO.md](docs/SSO.md).
- **Single sign-on only, with emergency access from the command line** (#52): _Settings → Sign-in_ has a third mode, _Single sign-on only_ (`passwordLogin: 'none'`), next to _Password and single sign-on_ and _Single sign-on, administrators also with a password_. It can only be turned on while a single sign-on provider is enabled and by an administrator who signed in through it. Then the sign-in page shows only the single sign-on buttons, `POST /api/auth/login` gives the usual "wrong user name or password" for every attempt, password resets and password changes are refused (`PASSWORD_LOGIN_OFF`), and new accounts are created without a password and linked at their first single sign-on. `SM_PASSWORD_LOGIN=all|adminsOnly|none` pins the mode for locked-down deployments (the setting is then read-only); on a new installation `none` takes effect once an administrator has signed in with single sign-on (audit entry `settings.password_login_effective`), so the first administrator can set Steward up with a password. The mode is shown in _System status_. When the identity provider fails, `steward admin emergency-access <admin> [--minutes 60]` on the server (data folder and master passphrase, like `reset-password`) prints a one-time link `/emergency-access#code=…` for one administrator account, usable once within the window (at most 24 hours; one open window per administrator – a new link replaces the older one of that account) – while Steward runs (a grant signed with the vault's key, accepted only over loopback, fresh and for the running instance) or stopped. The session is marked: a banner, at most one hour, no new API tokens, every audit entry flagged `[Emergency access]`, and all other administrators are notified (new notification event `security.emergency`: in-app, e-mail and Teams by default). Audit entries `auth.emergency_access_granted`, `auth.emergency_sign_in`, `auth.emergency_sign_in_failed`. `steward admin password-login all|adminsOnly|none` switches the mode on the server, and `steward admin list-admins` shows how each administrator can sign in (single sign-on, password, open emergency window). Migration `signin-001-emergency-access` adds the table `emergency_access_grants` and two columns to `sessions`. See [docs/INSTALL.md](docs/INSTALL.md#locked-out-of-single-sign-on).
- **Move an instance with a migration package** (#51): _Settings → Migration_ (or `steward migrate export|inspect|import` with Steward stopped) moves a whole instance – for example the desktop app to a container with PostgreSQL and Entra ID – in one `.smig` file: all data, the stored files and the audit log, encrypted with a transfer passphrase (scrypt + AES-256-GCM) and with a manifest (versions, counts, SHA-256 of every table and file) signed with the source's Ed25519 key. The import runs a dry run first (signature, content, audit history, what comes in, what does not, conflicts) and needs a decision for every source user – an existing account, a new single sign-on account, a new local account or a former user – and team; decisions for administrators and mappings to administrators are confirmed, and the importer enters the source key's fingerprint as read on the source before anything changes. Owners, access lists, assignees, responsible people and recipients follow the mapping; history (authors, approvers, deciders, the audit log, protocols) keeps the source's names, and the former names become aliases of the new accounts for access lists and the four-eyes rules. The source's audit log is kept unchanged as imported history (verifiable with the source's key, shown with who each actor is now), the target's chain gets one `instance.imported` entry with the package hash and the mapping table, and run protocol seals, approvals and report fingerprints that moved stay verifiable. Credentials are re-encrypted with the target's vault; passwords, sessions, tokens and instance-specific settings (single sign-on, SMTP, outbound allow-list, Git sync) do not move; schedules, triggers, monitors and other automation arrive paused. An older package is migrated on import with the regular database migrations, a newer one is refused, and the target must be new. Migration `move-001-instance-imports` adds `instance_imports`, `imported_audit_log` (immutable) and `identity_aliases`. See [docs/MIGRATION.md](docs/MIGRATION.md).
- **Container image on the GitHub Container Registry** (#75): every release tag `vX.Y.Z` publishes `ghcr.io/vxrz/steward:X.Y.Z`, `:X.Y` and `:latest` for linux/amd64 and linux/arm64, plus the same tags with `-browser` (Chromium for browser steps, `WITH_BROWSER=1`); pre-releases get only their own tag, and `latest`/`X.Y` never move back to an older line. The image is pushed only after Syft, Grype and Trivy passed, and it is the same image that was scanned (each platform is built once, the scanned OCI archive is pushed with its digest unchanged). The pushed image is signed with cosign (keyless, GitHub OIDC) and has SLSA build provenance and a CycloneDX SBOM as attestations; OCI labels name the source, version, commit and licence. `docker-compose.yml` and `docker-compose.postgres.yml` now use the release image (building from the checkout stays possible); update with `docker compose pull && docker compose up -d`. Verify with `cosign verify` or `gh attestation verify`: [docs/SECURITY-HARDENING.md](docs/SECURITY-HARDENING.md#verifying-downloads). A manual run of the Container workflow builds and scans both platforms, and with _push_ tries the whole chain on a `test-<commit>` tag.
- **Report branding: readability note and reset** – _Settings → Report branding_ warns when the accent colour would barely stand out from the white report page (lines, section markers and chart bars below 3:1 contrast, like the readability notes of _Appearance_), and _Reset to default_ puts colour, company name, classification and footer note back to the shipped values (the logo keeps its own reset).

### Changed

- **Upgrade note – single sign-on and `email_verified`** (#77): sign-ins now need `email_verified=true` whenever the e-mail claim names or identifies accounts (also with `usernameClaim: 'email'`). A provider configured that way at an identity provider that never sends `email_verified` (e.g. Entra ID without the optional claim) **refuses everyone after the update**. Steward names such providers in its start-up log and in the provider editor until an administrator decides; turn on _This provider verifies e-mail addresses_ only if the provider verifies every address, or use another user name claim. See [docs/SSO.md](docs/SSO.md#upgrading-from-an-earlier-version).
- Validation errors (`INVALID_INPUT`, invalid MCP tool arguments, invalid process, dashboard and monitor definitions) word their details the way zod 4 does, e.g. `name: Invalid input: expected string, received undefined` instead of `name: Required`, and `tags: Too big: expected array to have <=30 items`. Error codes, `params.details` and the translated texts in the web app stay the same.
- Timestamps in request bodies and query strings of the web app's API (access changes, compliance periods, evidence packs) must have seconds and write an offset as `+HH:MM` (RFC 3339; the web app always sent them so); URL fields are stored without surrounding spaces.
- `/api/v1`: a repeated query parameter (`status`, `trigger`, `level`, `type`, `reviewStatus`, `source`, `names`) takes at most 50 values (`maxItems: 50` in `docs/openapi.json`); more are refused with `400`. The rest of the OpenAPI document is unchanged.
- Container workflow: the browser variant and (on tags, weekly and manual runs) linux/arm64 are scanned too; the SBOM and scan report artifacts are named per image (`container-sbom-default-amd64`, …). The tag-only `container-image` artifact (an attested `docker save` archive) is gone – pull the image from ghcr.io instead, verify it, and `docker save` it for an air-gapped host.

### Security

- **Availability objectives only name Gatus platforms their author may see** (#86): an unknown or hidden platform is "not found" (no probing of platform ids), and readers who may not see the platform get an objective's status and values but neither the platform nor the endpoint. Documents a reader may not read do not count on the scorecard, also not in the stored daily history (shown as hidden). The scorecard cannot be cleaned by removing history: objectives are archived, never deleted (samples and breaches stay; an archived objective counts with its last status until its longest window has passed), and a duty archived during its current period counts until that period ends. An objective without data for more than 24 hours needs attention (a source turned off or an always-up endpoint chosen to hide a problem shows), breach messages are limited to one per objective a day, and the migration dry run refuses scorecard rows out of range.
- **Exceptions of service duties need a second person** (#85): skipping a period of a duty is a four-eyes decision – the requester (also under a former name of an imported account) can never approve it; administrators, designers and the duty's and the service's owners as they were when it was asked for can (an owner added afterwards cannot, so the requester cannot pick the second person), and they are notified (`duty.exception`). Exceptions reach at most three periods ahead, and the duty shows how many are planned. Before a duty is archived or its process, interval, grace period or level change, its open periods are closed under the old rules, so nobody can erase or re-judge them: a current period without a run that counts is recorded as _ended without a run_ (not met, like missed; stored under its key with a suffix, `2026-10~1`, and for mandatory and recommended duties the owners get the new message `duty.ended`, not the person who made the change), and the new rules start at the moment of the change – no run from before it counts, and no past period is judged again. A change that only makes a duty stricter (a higher level, a shorter grace period) applies to the current period, which stays open. It is not a step-up: it changes no rights or secrets, and both people, the reason and the decision are in the audit log. Duties only name processes their author may view, and people who may not view the process see the status but neither its name nor its runs.
- **Evidence files of manual steps** (#83) are handled like record attachments: allow-listed types whose content must match, at most 20 files of 20 MB per step, written once and read-only with their SHA-256 (a changed file is refused), always downloaded as attachments with a sandbox CSP. Only the person working on the step adds or removes them, and only while the step is being worked on (checked again when an upload finishes); an entry's files are fixed when it is completed. Whoever may see the run reads them. A migration package checks their paths and content again. Verification rights are checked on the server and never let the person who completed a step, or anyone who added evidence to it, verify it, also not under a former name of a moved instance.
- **Mapping a platform to an application needs the manage right on the platform** (#84 review): the mapping decides which application – and whose CIA rating – a platform's evidence pack names, so an application owner who only uses a platform can no longer claim it (or remove it).
- **Protection needs show what they leave out, and say why they moved** (#84 review): a service lists the applications it does not count (archived, not rated, needing re-rating), and every change of an application, of the members or of the scale that moves a service's effective protection need writes `service.protection_need_changed` on that service. A document scoped to an application is also recorded on the application.
- **CIA scale changes that re-order levels, or swap their labels, need a confirmation and flag the ratings** (#84 review); stored scales are validated like a save (an invalid one falls back to the BSI default, every protection need and rating says it is not reliable – UI, public API, evidence packs – and ratings and overrides are refused until an administrator saves a scale; a migration package with one is refused in the dry run).
- **Changing the CIA scale asks for a step-up** (#84): `PUT /api/catalogue/cia-scale` (administrators) maps or flags the ratings of every application at once, so it needs a fresh confirmation like other bulk changes. Ratings, overrides and document scopes are everyday catalogue changes by the people responsible: audited, without a step-up.
- **Sensitive actions ask for a fresh confirmation** (#14): a stolen or unattended session can no longer create API tokens, grant roles, add team members, give anyone a right in an access list (a new or raised entry, the role defaults of an emptied list, a new owner), reset passwords, export the audit log, the credential register or evidence packs, or change single sign-on, sign-in, outbound, notification delivery (SMTP server, webhook URLs, e-mail domains) or audit anchoring settings without the password or a fresh sign-in at the identity provider. The stored SMTP password is never sent to another server: changing the server, port or user, or turning TLS off, needs it again (`SMTP_PASSWORD_AGAIN`). API tokens can no longer use `GET /api/audit/export` and `GET /api/credentials/export.csv`. Single sign-on providers that send no `auth_time` cannot confirm (Entra ID: add the optional claim `auth_time`), see [docs/SSO.md](docs/SSO.md#step-up-re-authentication).
- **Single sign-on claims, hardened after review** (#77): _lower case without @domain_ strips only the domains the provider lists (required) and refuses user names of any other domain, so `boss@evil.example` cannot claim `boss`; a stable identifier must include `sub` or `oid` or custom claims the administrator confirms as unique per person (the confirmed claims are stored and audited, and the confirmation holds only for exactly those claims) – claims many people share (`tid`, `groups`, `roles`, `hd` …, also nested or in another case) are refused (`SSO_STABLE_ID_NOT_UNIQUE`); numbers and strings never give the same link; identifier values are taken exactly as sent (no trimming, joining or cutting; lists, objects and oversized values are refused); the recorded issuer + `sub` changes only when an identity is linked, never at a plain sign-in; user names are NFKC-normalised and limited to letters, digits and `. _ @ + -` when they link an account by name or create one (identities linked already keep signing in); a name that keeps a domain after stripping one is refused; group overage is checked for the configured claim only; `email_verified` comes from the same source as the e-mail address; long (hashed) links survive a kept issuer change; linking a local account ends its sessions; a refused sign-in shows the person only a reference (`SSO_SIGN_IN_REFUSED`) – the reason is in the audit entry; the sign-in page no longer reads accounts.
- **Linking existing accounts at the first single sign-on is a setting per provider** (#77): _only accounts an administrator bound to this provider_ (the default for new providers), _any local account with the same user name_ (what earlier versions did – providers saved before keep it, and the editor warns) or _never_. `email_verified` is now required whenever the e-mail claim names or identifies an account (also with `usernameClaim: 'email'`), unless the provider is marked as verifying every address. Links name the issuer: when a provider's issuer changes, links from before are tied to the former issuer (a person at the new issuer with the same `sub` cannot take the account over) unless the administrator keeps them. An account linked by the configured stable identifier claims is never re-linked to another identity. Group overage indicators (Entra `_claim_names`/`_claim_sources`, `hasgroups`) never grant roles and are logged and audited (`sso.groups_overage`). The editor warns about claims people or other tenants can influence (`email`, `upn`, `preferred_username` with multi-tenant issuers, `name`), a multi-tenant issuer without a required `tid`, Google without `hd`, and stripped domains; every change of the claim settings is audited with the former values. Fields the provider keeps in sync can no longer be changed by an administrator either. Claim values are bounded and control characters removed; the test sign-in never returns tokens or the client secret and is visible only to the administrator who started it.
- **zod 4 with a length limit on every list** (#63, [GHSA-mrhx-c7xc-m3w5](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-mrhx-c7xc-m3w5) / CVE-2023-54404): zod is upgraded from 3.25 to 4.6.7, which stops at a list's maximum length before it checks any item. Every list a route, an import, a webhook mapping, an MCP tool, a Git sync file or a process definition accepts now has a maximum length, and every map (inputs, bindings, headers, fields …) a maximum number of keys, counted before the values are checked; a test makes sure no schema leaves one out. The request body shape limits of 0.1.1 stay as a second line of defence. The accepted risk is removed from `osv-scanner.toml`, `.trivyignore` and `.grype.yaml`. `zod-to-json-schema` is replaced by zod's own `z.toJSONSchema()`. See [docs/SECURITY-HARDENING.md](docs/SECURITY-HARDENING.md#request-body-limits).
- Adding a documentation link to a platform with a URL that can't be parsed answered `500` instead of `400`.
- **Request bodies are limited to 1 MB by default** (#17, was 25 MB for every route): only the routes that carry a file's content in a JSON body accept more, each sized from its own content limit – the record, control catalogue, monitor, bundle and connector type imports, monitor template instances from CSV, process and dashboard definitions (create, save, validate, convert; 2 MB of text), API specifications (now also at most 20 MB when pasted, as when fetched), the report logo and report verification as base64. They are named in one table (`server/src/bodyLimits.ts`); the route matrix test fails when any other route accepts more than 1 MB. Multipart uploads keep their per-file limits. A body above the limit is answered `413` with the error code `BODY_TOO_LARGE` (`BODY_TOO_LARGE_KIB`) and the limit, translated in the web app. Process inputs, also a CSV table loaded from a file, now fit into 1 MB. See [docs/SECURITY-HARDENING.md](docs/SECURITY-HARDENING.md#request-body-limits).
- Migration packages are refused when their signature, content, file list or audit history does not match; file paths in a package cannot leave the stored-file folders or overwrite files, and uploads are size-limited (`SM_MIGRATION_MAX_MB`) and content-checked. A former user name of an imported account cannot become a new account (also not by single sign-on auto-provisioning), so it cannot inherit that account's grants.
- **The sign-in mode applies to open sessions:** password sessions that the current mode no longer allows (all of them with _Single sign-on only_, those of non-administrators with _administrators also with a password_) end at their next request, and switching the mode signs them out right away. Emergency sessions and the session of the administrator making the change stay. API tokens keep working.
- `steward admin reset-password` refuses while password sign-in is turned off and points to `emergency-access`.
- **Stored files and logos are served with the type of their content**, never with the type a database row (or an imported package) names: run artifacts are shown inline only when they are PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP images, anything else is a download (`application/octet-stream`, `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox`, `nosniff`); a record attachment of a type uploads never produce is a plain download; the header logo and the report logo take their type from their bytes. So no row can make Steward serve a page or a script on its own origin.
- **Single sign-on claims and sessions:** an account without a password belongs to one sign-in provider (chosen when it is created, _Users → Signs in with_) and only that provider can claim it at the first sign-in; a local administrator account is never taken over by its name – an administrator binds it to a provider first; a user name taken from the e-mail claim needs `email_verified`. The sign-in mode applies by how a session was opened (sessions from before this version count as password sessions), so a password session also ends when its account was linked to single sign-on later. Audit entries of an emergency session are flagged also when a service names the person as the actor (check runs, reports). `SM_PASSWORD_LOGIN=none` on a new installation takes effect once an administrator has signed in with single sign-on, so the setup cannot lock itself out.
- **Migration packages, hardened before the first release:** runs still running or waiting on the source arrive cancelled and are never resumed; a source name that is an account or team here can only become that account or team (never an alias of another one), and former names count as the same person in four-eyes rules, access-change subjects and document authorship; a guess by e-mail address or by the name before "@" needs a confirmation; the manifest signature covers the purpose line `steward-migration-manifest-v1`; the importer enters the source key's fingerprint as read on the source (all 32 hex digits) instead of confirming the value the target shows, and every answer that rests on imported history says so (_verified against the imported audit history of <fingerprint>_; `protocol.importedHistory` in `/api/v1`); the history is checked and taken over line by line (entries at most 4 MB), what a package unpacks to is capped (`SM_MIGRATION_MAX_UNPACKED_MB`, default 20480) and nothing is unpacked without free disk space.

## [0.1.2] - 2026-10-24

Runs under a path behind a reverse proxy, a documentation section on the service page, typed and editable links, an update notice for administrators, services in the public API, and more consistent sizes in the web app.

### Added

- **Documentation section on the service page** (#65): every living document linked to the service either way, with number, title, current edition (or draft), status, review status and next review date – only documents the reader may read, which also holds for every count. _New document_ creates a document with the link to the service already set (choosing the type when there is more than one document type) and opens the editor; `POST /api/records` takes `links` for that (up to 20, same checks and audit entries as adding them later). The document page names its services next to the title, the services list marks services with an overdue document review, and `/api/v1` has `GET /services/{serviceId}/documents`. See [docs/RECORDS.md](docs/RECORDS.md#documentation-of-a-service).
- **Link types for external links** (#66): an optional type on external links of records and services – built-in _Runbook_, _Repository_, _Monitoring_, _Vendor portal_, _Documentation_, _Ticket system_ and _Other_, each with an icon, translated – and administrators' own types (name and an icon from a fixed set; renamed and archived, never deleted) under _Records → Record types → Link types_. Links store the type's stable key. The _Add link_ dialog offers the type, and the Links section groups external links by type; internal links are unchanged. Existing links keep working without a type (shown as _Other_; migration `records-004-link-types` adds one nullable column and the table `link_types`). The type is in the audit entries, `/api/v1` (`linkType`, `linkTypeName`), the evidence pack's `record.json`, `db:copy` and the record PDF's link list. The CMDB entry of a service stays its own field. See [docs/RECORDS.md](docs/RECORDS.md#link-types).
- **Edit an existing link** (#68): _Edit link_ in the Links section of records and services changes label, type and note of an external link, or the note of an internal link, without removing and adding it again (who added it and when stays). Same rights and checks as for adding links: an unknown type is refused, an archived type cannot be chosen but stays on a link that has it, links of closed records cannot change, links to items the user cannot see cannot be edited. One audit entry `record.link_updated` / `service.link_updated` with the changed fields and their old and new values; an edit that changes nothing writes none. New routes `PATCH /api/records/:id/links/:linkId` and `PATCH /api/services/:id/links/:linkId`. See [docs/RECORDS.md](docs/RECORDS.md#editing-a-link).
- **Update notice for administrators**: after an update, administrators see _Steward was updated from X to Y_ at the top of the web app, with the migrations applied, the path of the automatic copy of the database (or why there is none) and a link to the release notes of the new version. Closing it is stored per administrator on the server; the next update replaces it. Only administrators can read it (`GET /api/system/update-notice`), since it names the copy's path. See [docs/INSTALL.md](docs/INSTALL.md#6-updating).
- **Run under a path prefix** (#50): `SM_BASE_PATH=/steward` – or an `SM_PUBLIC_URL` with a path, `https://apps.corp.example/steward` – serves everything below the prefix behind a reverse proxy: the web UI, `/api` (including `/api/v1`, `/api/mcp`, webhooks, health, readiness, metrics and the API documentation), the manifest and the service worker. The proxy passes the path on unchanged; requests outside the prefix get `404` (only `/` redirects to the prefix), and a proxy that strips the prefix is named in the log. One web build works under any prefix (relative asset URLs, the server writes `<base href>` into the page). Generated addresses follow the prefix: the single sign-on redirect URI and return path, links in e-mails, Teams cards, webhooks and the daily summary, the OpenAPI `servers` and `Link` headers of `/api/v1`, the MCP endpoint and client snippets, webhook, heartbeat and health URLs shown in the UI. `SM_BASE_PATH` is validated strictly and must match the path of `SM_PUBLIC_URL`. Root installations are unchanged. See [docs/INSTALL.md](docs/INSTALL.md#behind-a-reverse-proxy) for nginx, Traefik and Caddy, on an own host name (recommended) or under a path.
- **Services in the public API**: `GET /api/v1/services` (paginated, ordered by name, `archived=exclude|include|only`) and `GET /api/v1/services/{serviceId}` – description, criticality, contacts, responsible users and teams, tags, CMDB entry, archived, timestamps, and counts of links and living documents that only include what the token owner may see; the single service also has its links (items the owner may not see without id or name). See [docs/API.md](docs/API.md).

### Changed

- More consistent sizes in the web app: every page header uses the same small buttons (Platforms, the platform page, the process editor and Settings used larger ones). Headings inside records, documents, services and ticket references are smaller (18 / 16 / 14 px instead of 22 / 18 / 16 px), so content no longer looks larger than the rest of the app. Links inside text take the size of the text around them, and links in tables use the table's size (the control catalogue showed 16 px links in a 14 px table).
- The services list counts only the links the reader may see: external links and links to items they may open. Links to processes, platforms, monitors, records or credentials register entries hidden from the reader (or deleted) no longer count; the service page's count is the same. The list checks each linked item once, not once per service.
- Without `SM_PUBLIC_URL`, the single sign-on redirect URI takes the host name from `X-Forwarded-Host` when the request comes from a trusted proxy (`SM_TRUST_PROXY`), like the scheme already did from `X-Forwarded-Proto`; otherwise from `Host` as before.

### Security

- **Under a path prefix the session cookie is `__Secure-sm_session` with `Path=<base path>`** (`__Host-` requires `Path=/`): still `Secure`, `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Strict` and host-only. Every application on the same host name shares Steward's origin, though, so an own subdomain stays the recommendation ([docs/INSTALL.md](docs/INSTALL.md#behind-a-reverse-proxy)). Root installations keep `__Host-sm_session`.
- The guard that keeps processes from calling Steward's own API now also recognises the API below the base path and percent-encoded spellings of `/api`.
- Steward refuses to start with an `SM_PUBLIC_URL` that is not an absolute `http(s)://` URL, or that contains user info, a query string or a fragment.

## [0.1.1] - 2026-10-23

Safe in-place updates, living documents for service documentation, and security fixes (some also affect v0.1.0 installations – updating is recommended).

### Added

- **Safe in-place updates** ([docs/INSTALL.md](docs/INSTALL.md#6-updating)): before an update migrates an existing SQLite database, Steward writes a consistent copy to `<data folder>/backups/pre-<version>-<UTC time>.db` (0600; the newest 3 are kept, `SM_UPGRADE_BACKUPS` changes the number, `0` turns it off). Nothing is copied when there is nothing to migrate or for a new database. A failed migration stops the start with a message that names the migration and the copy. After the vault is unlocked, the audit log gets one `system.upgraded` entry (old and new version and schema, migrations applied, path of the copy).
